# Closure Audit — Evidence of Record

**Companion to:** [`2026-07-26-closure-audit.md`](2026-07-26-closure-audit.md) · **Date:** 2026-07-26 · **Baseline:** main `9107ed1d`

## Why this file exists

The ledger's rows cite `file:line` and paraphrase. **This file holds the quoted code those rows rest on** — all 128 claims, ~114,000 characters of verbatim evidence, plus every adversarial skeptic verdict.

It is committed deliberately. The audit's own §6 names, as a permanent evidence gap, the fact that the 2026-07-11 editorial review's working artifacts *"were session-scoped working files, not committed separately"* — leaving ~10 counted-but-unnarrated drift rows permanently uncheckable. The first draft of this audit reproduced that exact pattern: it stated *"long quotes live in the fan-out record, not here"* while committing no such record. Its own completeness critic caught it. This file is the correction.

## How to read a row

- **Disposition (verifier)** — what the group verifier concluded.
- **Skeptic verdicts** — present only for `CLOSED` / `CLOSED-BY-REFUTATION` claims. Verification was **asymmetric by design**: refute closed claims hard, accept open claims cheaply, because a false `CLOSED` silently drops real work while a false `OPEN` costs one re-check. Three skeptics per group, each with a distinct lens (find-the-defect · attack-the-scope · attack-the-mechanism), all defaulting to *refuted*. **A claim flips to open on ≥2 refutes.**
- **Final disposition** — after that merge. Where it differs from the verifier's, the flip is shown.
- Line numbers are **as of `9107ed1d`**. Findings were re-located by symbol and behaviour, never by line number — the source review's line numbers are stale by design (+36,297/−5,218 lines since its baseline).

⚠️ The ledger's §7 exclusion list is the machine-readable artifact. This file is the human-auditable substrate behind it.

---

## Group: `books-view`
### `code-12` — CLOSED
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-code-review.md § "12. server/views/books.html:2092 — The chapter activity panel reads field names the API doesn't send" (ranked #12, orig #17, Medium/CONFIRMED, lens=contract)
**Original severity:** Medium (CONFIRMED, contract lens)
**Claim:** The chapter activity panel in books.html read a.action / a.userName / a.details / a.timestamp while the activity API actually sends type / username / description / createdAt, so every row rendered a generic icon, the placeholder name 'Kerfi', blank description and blank time — and the identical broken block was duplicated word-for-word a second time in the same file.
**Current location:** `server/views/books.html:2805-2827 (renderActivityRows), called at server/views/books.html:2106 (loadChapterActivity) and :2532 (cvLoadActivity)`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
Client now reads the exact field names the API emits, in a single shared renderer:
server/views/books.html:2805 `function renderActivityRows(activities) {`
:2811 `getActivityIcon(a.type) +`
:2815 `escapeHtml(a.username || 'Kerfi') +`
:2818 `escapeHtml(a.description) +`
:2821 `formatTimeAgo(a.createdAt) +`
Both former copies now delegate to it — :2106 `panel.innerHTML = renderActivityRows(activities);` (loadChapterActivity) and :2532 `panel.innerHTML = renderActivityRows(activities);` (cvLoadActivity).
Server side of the contract, server/services/activityLog.js:299-311 `function parseRow(row) { return { id: row.id, type: row.type, userId: row.user_id, username: row.username, book: row.book, chapter: row.chapter, section: row.section, description: row.description, metadata: …, createdAt: row.created_at }; }` — returned as `{ activities: rows.map(parseRow), total, limit, offset }` (activityLog.js:292) by `GET /api/activity` (server/routes/activity.js:24-36, `res.json(result)`).
Icon vocabulary was also re-keyed to types that are actually logged — server/views/books.html:2830-2847 `function getActivityIcon(type) { var icons = { segment_edit_saved: …, assign_chapter: …, unassign_chapter: …, assign_reviewer: …, assign_localizer: … }; return icons[type] || '📌'; }`.
Regression-pinned: server/__tests__/viewRouteContracts.test.js:19 `describe('books.html chapter activity panel (F12)', …)`, :37 `expect(src.match(/function renderActivityRows\(/g)).toHaveLength(1);` and :38 `expect(src.match(/panel\.innerHTML = renderActivityRows\(activities\)/g)).toHaveLength(2);` (dedup pinned, so the duplicate can't return).
```
**Notes:** Fixed by commit 32878c35 "fix(views): chapter activity panel reads real /api/activity fields, deduped renderer, live icon vocabulary (F12, item 16 PR1)" plus follow-up cc5013b9 (icon-key cleanup), both merged to main in PR #303 (merge a1fb853f, 2026-07-18, 'item 16 PR1 — Batch F dashboard/view contract repair'). Register confirms closure: docs/plans/2026-07-11-pre-semester-coding-campaign.md:102 — "PR1 ✅ MERGED PR #303 … the 7 mechanical contract repairs (F11/F12/F13/F18/F26/F27/F31)". Line drift is large (2092 → 2805) but the finding was re-located by symbol (renderActivityRows/getActivityIcon/'Kerfi'), not by line. NEW observation, not a ledger row: `escapeHtml(a.description)` has no `|| ''` guard, so a NULL description column would render the literal string 'undefined' — same class as the review's own finding #30 on my-work.html:1435; every current writer supplies a description, so it is dormant.
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — Scope holds at every site the claim covers. books.html is one <script> block (1460-2850) so the hoisted renderActivityRows (:2805) is in scope for both callers, and BOTH former duplicates now delegate: :2106 and :2532 are each `panel.innerHTML = renderActivityRows(activities);`. The renderer reads exactly the emitted names — :2811 getActivityIcon(a.type), :2815 escapeHtml(a.username || 'Kerfi'), :2818 escapeHtml(a.description), :2821 formatTimeAgo(a.createdAt) — matching activityLog.parseRow (server/services/activityLog.js:296-311 `{ id, type, userId, username, book, chapter, section, description, metadata, createdAt: row.created_at }`) returned by GET /api/activity (routes/activity.js:36 res.json(result)). I enumerated for missed siblings: grep of getActivityIcon|\.timestamp|\.details|\.action across server/views/*.html finds no residual old-field activity read in books.html (my-work.html:1477 already reads a.type — separate findings #26/#31; admin.html hits are .action-btn CSS); server/public/js/*.js has no activity rendering; find shows only one books.html in the repo. The 'blank time' leg is genuinely fixed rather than merely renamed: formatTimeAgo (:2788) does `new Date(String(dateStr).replace(' ', 'T') + 'Z')`, which parses SQLite CURRENT_TIMESTAMP's 'YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS' UTC form; and migration 040-service-table-ownership.js declares `username TEXT NOT NULL` / `description TEXT NOT NULL`, so the 'Kerfi'/blank fallbacks cannot fire on real rows. Pinned by server/__tests__/viewRouteContracts.test.js:19-49 (positive reads, negative phantom-field asserts, and a dedupe pin requiring exactly 1 definition and 2 delegating call sites). Attribution: 32878c35 (client half), cc5013b9 (icon vocabulary).
- ✅ upheld — Independently confirmed fixed. server/views/books.html:2805 `function renderActivityRows(activities)` reads a.type (:2811), a.username (:2815), a.description (:2818), a.createdAt (:2821) — the exact names emitted by parseRow in server/services/activityLog.js:298-311 (`type: row.type, username: row.username, description: row.description, createdAt: row.created_at`), returned at :288 as `activities: rows.map(parseRow)` and shipped by GET /api/activity (server/routes/activity.js:24-36 `res.json(result)`). A grep for the phantom fields (`\.action|\.userName|\.details|\.timestamp`) across books.html returns ZERO hits, so no step of the failure (generic icon / 'Kerfi' placeholder / blank description / blank time) is reachable. Duplication half also dead: the two call sites at :2106 (loadChapterActivity) and :2532 (cvLoadActivity) both delegate to the single renderer, and both sit inside the one <script> block spanning 1460-2850 so the hoisted declaration resolves. I separately checked the icon half, which reading a.type alone does NOT fix: the icon map at books.html:2830-2847 is keyed on strings the callsites actually pass to activityLog.log() — segment_edit_saved, segment_edit_approved, segment_edit_rejected, segment_edit_discuss, segment_edits_applied, segment_accepted, acceptance_revoked, assign_chapter, unassign_chapter, assign_reviewer, assign_localizer all appear in both the map and the emitted vocabulary, so real rows get real icons rather than the 📌 fallback.
- ✅ upheld — Mechanism verified end-to-end, not just by absence of the old lines. Producer: server/services/activityLog.js:299-311 parseRow() returns `{id, type, userId, username, book, chapter, section, description, metadata, createdAt}` and is the only shaping function for GET /api/activity (server/routes/activity.js:24-36 `res.json(result)` where result comes from search() -> `rows.map(parseRow)`, activityLog.js:288). Storage backs every one of those fields: migration server/migrations/040-service-table-ownership.js:17-28 declares `type TEXT NOT NULL, user_id TEXT NOT NULL, username TEXT NOT NULL, ... description TEXT NOT NULL, ... created_at DATETIME DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP`, so username/description cannot be NULL -> the 'Kerfi' placeholder and blank description are structurally impossible for real rows. Consumer: server/views/books.html:2805-2827 single `function renderActivityRows(activities)` reads `getActivityIcon(a.type)` (:2811), `escapeHtml(a.username || 'Kerfi')` (:2815), `escapeHtml(a.description)` (:2818), `formatTimeAgo(a.createdAt)` (:2821) -- exactly the emitted names. The 'generic icon' half is dead too, not merely the field names: getActivityIcon (:2830-2847) is re-keyed to live ACTIVITY_TYPES *values* (`segment_edit_saved`, `segment_edit_approved`, `segment_edit_rejected`, `segment_edit_discuss`, `segment_edits_applied`, `review_submitted`, `version_restored`, activityLog.js:20-52) rather than the old action vocabulary, so the 226-fallback fires only for genuinely unmapped types. Blank-time step also dies: formatTimeAgo (:2788-2802) does `new Date(String(dateStr).replace(' ','T')+'Z')` and only returns '' for a falsy/unparseable value, and created_at is always populated. Duplication: grep of the whole file finds zero occurrences of `a.action`, `a.userName`, `a.details`, `a.timestamp`; `renderActivityRows` is defined once and both former copies delegate -- server/views/books.html:2106 (loadChapterActivity) and :2532 (cvLoadActivity) are identical `panel.innerHTML = renderActivityRows(activities);`. Pinned against regression at server/__tests__/viewRouteContracts.test.js:19-45 including `expect(src.match(/function renderActivityRows\(/g)).toHaveLength(1)` and negative assertions on all four phantom fields. I could not construct a path on which the described failure still completes.

### `code-12b` — CLOSED
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-code-review.md § "12. server/views/books.html:2092" — second half of the same finding ("the 'chapter' filter is never applied server-side")
**Original severity:** Medium (CONFIRMED, contract lens) — sub-claim of ranked finding #12
**Claim:** Even though the panel is scoped to one chapter and sends `&chapter=`, the chapter parameter was never applied server-side, so the panel would show whole-book activity rather than the chapter in view; the fix was to wire up server-side chapter filtering or drop the parameter.
**Current location:** `server/services/activityLog.js:128-143 (search/count prepared statements) and :243-292 (search()); route at server/routes/activity.js:24-36`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
The client still sends the parameter — server/views/books.html:2096 `'/api/activity?book=' + book + '&chapter=' + chapter + '&limit=10'` (and identically at :2522) — and the server now honours it end-to-end:
server/routes/activity.js:25 `const { book, type, user, chapter, limit, offset } = req.query;` → :28-34 `activityLog.search({ book: book || null, type: type || null, userId: user || null, chapter: chapter || null, … })`.
server/services/activityLog.js:128-134 `search: db.prepare(\`SELECT * FROM activity_log WHERE (book = ? OR ? IS NULL) AND (type = ? OR ? IS NULL) AND (user_id = ? OR ? IS NULL) AND (CAST(chapter AS INTEGER) = CAST(? AS INTEGER) AND (chapter GLOB '-[0-9]*' OR chapter GLOB '[0-9]*') OR ? IS NULL) ORDER BY created_at DESC LIMIT ? OFFSET ?\`)` — the same predicate is mirrored in the `count` statement (:136-143).
server/services/activityLog.js:252-261 normalises the param: `let chapterText = chapter == null ? null : String(chapter); if (chapterText !== null && !/^-?\d+$/.test(chapterText)) { if (chapterText === 'appendices') { chapterText = '-1'; } else { return { activities: [], total: 0, … }; } }` and binds it at :263-286.
Regression test: server/__tests__/activityChapterFilter.test.js:2 `* F12 server half (item 16 PR1): the chapter activity panel sends`.
```
**Notes:** Fixed by commit be375496 "feat(server): activity search honors the chapter filter the panel already sends (F12 server half, item 16 PR1)", merged to main in PR #303 (merge a1fb853f). The register records why the shipped SQL differs from the plan's: docs/plans/2026-07-11-pre-semester-coding-campaign.md:102 — "plan's TEXT-cast chapter-filter SQL failed its own test (better-sqlite3 REAL-binds JS ints → '1.0' in the TEXT column) → INTEGER-cast + GLOB numeric guard + non-numeric param guard ('appendices'→-1, garbage→empty)". Note the chosen route was 'wire up filtering', not 'drop the parameter', so the panel is now genuinely chapter-scoped.
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — The chapter parameter is now honoured end-to-end and I found no sibling call site that keeps the defect. routes/activity.js:25 destructures `chapter` and :28-34 passes `chapter: chapter || null` into activityLog.search, which normalises it at services/activityLog.js:252-261 (String(); 'appendices'->'-1'; any other non-numeric returns an empty result rather than colliding with ch0) and binds it at :263-286 into a real SQL predicate present in BOTH prepared statements — search (:127-134) and count (:136-143) — as `AND (CAST(chapter AS INTEGER) = CAST(? AS INTEGER) AND (chapter GLOB '-[0-9]*' OR chapter GLOB '[0-9]*') OR ? IS NULL)`, so `total` cannot disagree with `activities`. Precedence groups correctly as ((numeric match AND is-numeric-string) OR omitted-filter bypass); param counts are 10/10 and 8/8. Scope sweep: the only non-test callers of search() are routes/activity.js:28 plus status.js:216/240/353/445/558/679/876, and none of the status.js ones pass a chapter, so they take the `? IS NULL` bypass and are behaviourally unchanged — the fix neither missed a site nor silently narrowed one. The one sibling that could have had the same bug, GET /api/status/activity/timeline (status.js:349), does not accept a chapter param and its sole client status.html:681 never sends one, so no second panel still shows whole-book activity. Client still sends the param (books.html:2096 and :2522, '/api/activity?book=' + book + '&chapter=' + chapter + '&limit=10'), which is now meaningful rather than ignored. The storage shapes that would have made the filter silently miss ('1.0' from REAL binding, '' from segment-editor.js's String(edit?.chapter || '')) are explicitly handled by the CAST-to-INTEGER plus GLOB numeric guard and pinned in server/__tests__/activityChapterFilter.test.js. Attribution: be375496 plus 4bff769a.
- ✅ upheld — Independently confirmed fixed, by behavioral proof rather than inspection. The client still sends the param (books.html:2096 and :2522 `'/api/activity?book=' + book + '&chapter=' + chapter + '&limit=10'`), and it is now honoured: server/routes/activity.js:25 destructures `chapter` from req.query and :32 passes `chapter: chapter || null` into activityLog.search(); search() (activityLog.js:252-259) normalises it (`String(chapter)`, non-numeric -> empty result, 'appendices' -> '-1') and binds it twice at :263-273 into the search/count statements. I executed the production predicate verbatim from activityLog.js:128-134 (`AND (CAST(chapter AS INTEGER) = CAST(? AS INTEGER) AND (chapter GLOB '-[0-9]*' OR chapter GLOB '[0-9]*') OR ? IS NULL)`) against an in-memory SQLite table seeded with chapters '3','5','','-1','0': param '3'=>["3"], '5'=>["5"], '0'=>["0"], '-1'=>["-1"], null=>all five rows. The described failure requires the parameter to be dropped so whole-book rows come back; that step no longer exists. The two coercion escapes that could resurrect it are also closed: '0' survives `chapter || null` because it is a truthy string, and 'appendices' maps to -1. Writers store chapters as numeric strings (routes/segment-editor.js:433,529,650,1271,1346 `chapter: String(req.chapterNum)`), so the filter matches real rows instead of silently over-filtering.
- ✅ upheld — The described failure (panel scoped to one chapter but showing whole-book activity) cannot complete today; I traced every link and empirically tested the SQL. (1) Client still sends it: server/views/books.html:2096 and :2522 `'/api/activity?book=' + book + '&chapter=' + chapter + '&limit=10'`. (2) Route no longer drops it: server/routes/activity.js:25 `const { book, type, user, chapter, limit, offset } = req.query;` -> :28-34 `activityLog.search({ book: ..., chapter: chapter || null, ... })`. (3) Service binds it into the predicate: server/services/activityLog.js:127-134 `search: db.prepare(...) AND (CAST(chapter AS INTEGER) = CAST(? AS INTEGER) AND (chapter GLOB '-[0-9]*' OR chapter GLOB '[0-9]*') OR ? IS NULL)`, mirrored in `count` (:136-143), with chapterText bound at :263-286. I did NOT take the comment's word for the AND/OR precedence -- I ran the exact prepared statement against an in-memory better-sqlite3 activity_log: with chapter='3' it returned only the chapter-3 rows (including the REAL-bound row stored as '3.0'), with chapter='0' it returned [] (the GLOB guard correctly suppressed the empty-string row that CAST('' AS INTEGER)=0 would otherwise match), and with chapter=null it returned all 5 rows. So the `? IS NULL` bypass only fires when no filter is supplied, and cross-chapter leakage is not reachable. (4) The one remaining way the filter could be a no-op is a non-numeric chapter param hitting the `return { activities: [], total: 0 }` early-out at activityLog.js:253-260 -- I checked both call sites and it is not reachable from this panel: loadChapterDetailFromBooks receives a bare JS number inlined into the onclick (books.html:1793 `+ ch.chapterNum +`), and cvLoadChapter receives `<option value="' + ch.chapter + '">` (books.html:2325) sourced from GET /api/books/:book, where chapter is numeric (server/routes/books.js:198 `{ chapter: -1, ... }` for appendices, `chapter: chapterNum` elsewhere) -- both match /^-?\d+$/, and 'appendices' is explicitly mapped to '-1' anyway. Regression test present at server/__tests__/activityChapterFilter.test.js. Separate note, deliberately NOT flipping this verdict: rows written as `chapter: String(edit?.chapter || '')` on failed-lookup edges (server/routes/segment-editor.js:611, :827, :958) store '' and are excluded from every chapter filter by the GLOB guard -- that is a different (invisible-rows) defect than the reviewed one, and the SQL comment documents the exclusion as intentional.


## Group: `localization-editor-client`
### `code-10` — OPEN-TRACKED
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-code-review.md — summary table row 10 (orig. #15) + detail section "### 10. server/public/js/localization-editor.js:1622 — The review tab calls an API endpoint that doesn't exist"
**Original severity:** (unrated)
**Claim:** The localization review tab's section picker calls GET /api/sections/<bookSlug>/<chapterNum>, but the sections router only defines a single-segment /:sectionId route, so every such request 404s and the dropdown is silently replaced by an "Error" placeholder — the picker-driven review flow (load section → suggestions → sync log) is unreachable except via a direct ?sectionId= deep link.
**Current location:** `server/public/js/localization-editor.js:1767 (defect); server/routes/sections.js:53 + server/index.js:244 (the router that fails to match)`
**Register item:** C5 · B1-F1 (docs/plans/2026-07-21-post-item17-followup-campaign.md:89 — "Blocks I16-R1 (dead-endpoint disposition)") and P3 tail (same file, :115 — "I16-R1 (dead endpoint keep-vs-retire, rides B1-F1)"); originating item I16-R1 in docs/plans/2026-07-11-pre-semester-coding-campaign.md:105 — "F10 review-tab picker calls nonexistent GET /api/sections/:bookSlug/:chapterNum (localization-editor.js rvLoadSections); deferred to the suggestions-family keep-vs-retire decision (B1-F1)"
**Evidence:**

```
CLIENT — server/public/js/localization-editor.js:1761-1780 (drifted from :1622, same function, same URL shape):
`​``js
  async function rvLoadSections(bookSlug, chapterNum) {
    var select = document.getElementById('rv-section-select');
    select.innerHTML = '<option value="">' + UI.common.loading + '</option>';
    select.disabled = true;

    try {
      var data = await fetchJson('/api/sections/' + bookSlug + '/' + chapterNum);
      ...
      data.sections.forEach(function (sec) { ... });
      select.disabled = false;
    } catch {
      select.innerHTML = '<option value="">' + UI.common.error + '</option>';
    }
  }
`​``
SERVER — server/index.js:244 `app.use('/api/sections', sectionsRoutes);` and the COMPLETE route inventory of server/routes/sections.js (grep of every `router.<verb>(`):
`​``
53:router.get('/:sectionId', requireAuth, requireRole(ROLES.EDITOR), (req, res) => {
89:  '/:sectionId/assign-reviewer',
172: '/:sectionId/assign-localizer',
259: '/:sectionId/status',
360: '/:sectionId/submit-review',
426: '/:sectionId/approve-review',
498: '/:sectionId/request-changes',
`​``
The only GET is single-segment `/:sectionId`; nothing matches a two-segment `<book>/<chapter>` GET, so `/api/sections/liffraedi-2e/3` 404s. `fetchJson` (server/public/js/htmlUtils.js:34-46) throws on `!res.ok`, which lands in the `catch` that writes `UI.common.error` into the dropdown — exactly the described failure. The deep-link path still works because it hits the route that DOES exist: localization-editor.js:1822 `var section = await fetchJson('/api/sections/' + sectionId);`.
ATTRIBUTION: `git log -S"'/api/sections/' + bookSlug" -- server/public/js/localization-editor.js` returns exactly one commit, `b75ed4b5 refactor: extract inline JS from localization-editor.html into separate file` — a file move, not a fix. Nothing has closed it.
```
**Notes:** Line drift only: :1622 → :1767, caused by commit b75ed4b5 extracting the inline JS out of localization-editor.html into public/js/; the symbol (rvLoadSections), the URL literal and the catch-writes-"Error" behaviour are byte-for-byte the review's. Re-located by symbol, not by line, per HARD RULE 1.

Tracking is unambiguous and decision-gated, not forgotten: the live campaign carries it twice (C5 · B1-F1 as the blocker, P3 as the opportunistic low), and the register already records the exact fix shape the review proposed — "fix shape if kept = one book-scoped route matching the client URL (deep-link ?sectionId= path works meanwhile)". Whether it is fixed at all depends on the lead's keep-vs-retire call on the localization-suggestions family; if retired, the finding is moot.

The review's alternative fix ("change the client to call an endpoint that already exists") is live and appendix-aware today: server/routes/status.js:1290 `router.get('/:book/:chapter/sections', requireAuth, …)` returns per-section status for a book+chapter — i.e. GET /api/status/:book/:chapter/sections. Response-shape compatibility with the client's `data.sections[].{id,sectionNum,status}` reads was not verified here (out of closure-audit scope).

The review's second-pass claim that "no test in the project's suite exercises this call shape" still holds: every /api/sections/ reference under server/__tests__ and server/e2e uses a numeric single-segment id (sectionsAssignLinkModuleId.test.js, crossBookAuthz.test.js).

Out-of-scope observation, NOT a ledger row (per SCOPE DISCIPLINE): even if the route were added, the predecessor register's I14-R4 (pre-semester campaign :141) notes bookRegistration.registerBook iterates bookData.chapters only, so the section registry contains no appendix rows — a book+chapter picker would return nothing for appendices.


## Group: `pipeline-service`
### `code-5` — CLOSED
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-code-review.md — finding table row 5 (line 75) and detail section "### 5. `server/services/pipelineService.js:439` — Pipeline job tracking doesn't know which book a job belongs to" (lines 186-198); also rolled up in docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-review-joint-summary.md line 30 (theme 5, "give pipeline jobs a `book` field") and Batch C of the code review (line 708)
**Original severity:** Medium, CONFIRMED (lens: state)
**Claim:** Pipeline job objects carried `{id, type, chapter, moduleId, track, userId}` with no `book` field, and the "is a job running" check matched on chapter+type+status only — so a running chemistry ch3 job would return a false "already running" (with chemistry's job id) for a biology ch3 request, and the same blindness propagated to the publish function, the publication-status display, and the admin source-fetch dedupe.
**Current location:** `server/services/pipelineService.js:469 (hasRunningJob) and :386 / :285 / :888 (job objects)`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
server/services/pipelineService.js:463-481 — "/**\n * Check if a job is already running for this book/chapter/type combo.\n * Strict equality on all three keys — chapter is deliberately NOT normalized\n * (live values include numbers, null for fetch-source, and 'all' for\n * whole-book generate-tm), so callers pass the same shape the creators store.\n */\nfunction hasRunningJob(book, chapter, type) {\n  for (const job of jobs.values()) {\n    if (\n      job.book === book &&\n      job.chapter === chapter &&\n      job.type === type &&\n      job.status === 'running'\n    ) {\n      return job;\n    }\n  }\n  return null;\n}". All three job-creation sites now stamp `book`: spawnJob :383-391 "const job = {\n    id: jobId,\n    type,\n    book,\n    chapter,\n    moduleId: moduleId || 'all'," ; the pipeline runner :282-287 "type: 'pipeline',\n    book,\n    chapter," ; the generate-tm runner :885-889 "type: 'generate-tm',\n    book,\n    chapter: chapter ?? 'all',". `listJobs` also filters on book (:452 "if (book) result = result.filter((j) => j.book === book);"). Every call site named in the finding now passes the book first: server/routes/pipeline.js:75/:125/:175 "pipeline.hasRunningJob(params.book, params.chapter, 'inject'|'render'|'pipeline')"; the publish function server/services/publicationService.js:216 "const existing = pipelineService.hasRunningJob(bookSlug, chapterNum, 'pipeline');"; the publication-status display :353 "const runningJob = pipelineService.hasRunningJob(bookSlug, chapterNum, 'pipeline');"; the admin source-fetch dedupe server/routes/admin.js:262 "const alreadyFetching = pipeline.hasRunningJob(slug, null, 'fetch-source');" and :335 "const existing = pipeline.hasRunningJob(slug, null, 'fetch-source');"; server/routes/segment-editor.js:1307 "pipelineService.hasRunningJob(req.params.book, req.chapterNum, 'pipeline')". Grep for a residual chapter-only comparison across server/ returns only pipelineService.js:453 (the listJobs filter) and :473 (inside the book-scoped conjunction) — no unscoped survivor. Regression tests pin the exact failure scenario: server/__tests__/new-features.test.js:54-107, describe('pipelineService job book-scoping (item 12, F5)'), incl. "it('a running job for one book does not block the same chapter of another book'" → "expect(hasRunningJob('liffraedi-2e', 3, 'pipeline')).toBeFalsy();" with a chemistry ch3 job in the map, plus the positive control "expect(hasRunningJob('efnafraedi-2e', 3, 'pipeline')?.id).toBe('test-f5-chem');" and a per-book fetch-source case. Attribution: commit ceb53bb8 (2026-07-17) "fix(item12): pipeline jobs carry book; hasRunningJob book-scoped (F5)", merged via 615a7462 "Merge pull request #298 from SigurdurVilhelmsson/fix/item12-apply-job-version-integrity" (ceb53bb8 verified present in 615a7462^2).
```
**Notes:** Re-located by symbol, not line: the review cited pipelineService.js:439, which today is the getJob JSDoc — the finding's actual subject moved to :469. Do NOT read that drift as the fix; the fix is the added `book` parameter and the `job.book === book` clause, quoted above. The finding's own second-pass verification ("every call site checked passes only chapter and type... The job registry itself is a single shared in-memory list with no per-book separation") no longer describes the tree. Cross-ref on issue #328 as the brief asked: #328 is NOT this finding and does not keep it open. #328 is tracked in the live campaign as C5 · B1-F5 + I12-R1 (docs/plans/2026-07-21-post-item17-followup-campaign.md:86) and is a DISTINCT, route-level authorization leak that the item-12 data-model fix deliberately did not address — `GET /api/pipeline/jobs` and `/jobs/:jobId` still return jobs regardless of which books the caller is assigned to, i.e. a read-only cross-book information leak, not the dedupe/state collision finding 5 described. The register even records that the #328 fix shape changed on investigation (delete the zero-caller `GET /jobs`; scope `/jobs/:jobId` on `job.book` vs `req.user.books`, keep the ADMIN bypass, 404 not 403) and that it is [LEAD]-queued after C1d. So: finding 5 CLOSED; its authz sibling OPEN-TRACKED under C5 · I12-R1 / issue #328 — but that sibling is outside this group's claim set and is not filed as a row here per scope discipline. One out-of-scope observation, flagged not filed: `runningJobCount()` (:352) and the MAX_JOBS capacity guard remain deliberately global across books, so a busy book can still exhaust the shared concurrency budget of another. That is a capacity-cap design choice, not the identity-collision defect finding 5 raised, and the review did not claim it.
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — I attacked the defect independently (ignoring the verifier's evidence) and could not reach it. Enumeration of BOTH halves of the failure scenario is complete: (a) Job creation — there are exactly three writes into the `jobs` map in server/services/pipelineService.js (`jobs.set` at :298 for the `const job = {` at :282 `type:'pipeline', book, chapter,`; :398 for `spawnJob`'s object at :383 `id: jobId, type, book, chapter,`; and :899 for the generate-tm object at :885 `type: 'generate-tm', book, chapter: chapter ?? 'all',`). All six `spawnJob` callers pass a book: runExtract :76, runProtect :121, runUnprotect :163, runInject :213, runRender :251, runFetchSource :794 (`book: slug, chapter: null`). There is no fourth job-creation site, so no job can exist with an absent/undefined `book`. (b) Consumption — `hasRunningJob(book, chapter, type)` at server/services/pipelineService.js:469-481 conjoins `job.book === book && job.chapter === chapter && job.type === type && job.status === 'running'`, and every one of the four surfaces the finding named passes a real slug: the running-job check in routes/pipeline.js:75/:125/:175 (`pipeline.hasRunningJob(params.book, ...)`, where `validateParams` at :35-40 rejects anything not in `VALID_BOOKS`); the publish function publicationService.js:216 (`hasRunningJob(bookSlug, chapterNum, 'pipeline')`); the publication-status display publicationService.js:353 (same, feeding `status.runningJob`); and the admin source-fetch dedupe admin.js:262 and :335 (`hasRunningJob(slug, null, 'fetch-source')`, matching runFetchSource's stored `book: slug, chapter: null`). A grep for any other `job.chapter` comparison in server/ (excluding tests) returns only pipelineService.js:453 (the `listJobs` filter, which also filters on book at :452) and :473 (inside the book-scoped conjunction) — no unscoped survivor. Because book slugs are exact strings from a closed set, no normalization gap can make 'efnafraedi-2e' collide with 'liffraedi-2e': the precise step of the failure scenario that cannot execute is 'a running chemistry ch3 job matches a biology ch3 query and returns chemistry's job id'. OUT OF SCOPE, notes only (a different failure mode, not this finding, and it does not affect the verdict): `req.chapterNum` is a String at requireRole.js:207 but a parsed int at validateParams.js:33, so the strict `job.chapter === chapter` compare at segment-editor.js:1307 could miss a genuine same-book duplicate — that is a false NEGATIVE, the opposite of the cross-book false POSITIVE claimed here. Separately, `GET /jobs` → `listJobs` (routes/pipeline.js:218-229) is book-scoped only when the caller supplies `?book=`, but that is the tracked issue #328 (C5), not this finding.
- ✅ upheld — MECHANISM VERIFIED — the described failure cannot complete. (1) The gate itself: server/services/pipelineService.js:469-481 `function hasRunningJob(book, chapter, type)` makes `job.book === book` a HARD CONJUNCT with chapter/type/status, so a chemistry job can never be returned to a biology query. (2) Completeness of the write side: there are exactly THREE `jobs.set` sites in the service — :298 (runPipeline inline object, `type:'pipeline', book, chapter`), :398 (spawnJob's shared object, `type, book, chapter`), :899 (runGenerateTm inline, `type:'generate-tm', book, chapter: chapter ?? 'all'`) — and all three stamp `book`. Every other producer funnels through spawnJob: runExtract:75, runProtect:120, runUnprotect:162, runInject:212, runRender:250, runFetchSource:793 (`book: slug`), each passing its own `book`. There is no unstamped creation path, so no job object can carry `book === undefined` and collide. (3) `book` cannot be undefined on the query side either: routes/pipeline.js:37-40 rejects with 400 unless `VALID_BOOKS.includes(book)` before `validateParams` returns. (4) All four consumers the finding names now pass the request's own book: routes/pipeline.js:75/:125/:175 `pipeline.hasRunningJob(params.book, params.chapter, 'inject'|'render'|'pipeline')`; the publish function publicationService.js:216 `hasRunningJob(bookSlug, chapterNum, 'pipeline')` inside `publishChapter(bookSlug, ...)`; the publication-status display :353 `hasRunningJob(bookSlug, chapterNum, 'pipeline')` feeding `status.runningJob`; the admin source-fetch dedupe admin.js:262 and :335 `hasRunningJob(slug, null, 'fetch-source')` — and I checked BOTH halves of that consumer: the creator at admin.js:378 passes the same `slug` (`runFetchSource({catalogueSlug: book.catalogueSlug, slug, ...})`) that :335 queries, and runFetchSource:795 stamps `book: slug`, so query key and stored key are the same value. segment-editor.js:1307 (`req.params.book`) and its runPipeline at :1333 (`book: req.params.book`) match too. (5) No alternate gate survives: a sweep for `status === 'running'` / 'already running' across server/ (excluding node_modules/tests) finds only `runningJobCount` (a global MAX_JOBS capacity counter, deliberately book-blind and unrelated to identity), hasRunningJob itself, and message strings in routes/publication.js. The step that dies is precisely the one the review described: `job.book === book` is false for a chemistry job under a biology query, so the 409 with chemistry's job id is unreachable. Regression pins exist and encode the exact scenario (server/__tests__/new-features.test.js:75-107, 'a running job for one book does not block the same chapter of another book' plus positive control and per-book fetch-source case). Attribution confirmed: commit ceb53bb8 'fix(item12): pipeline jobs carry book; hasRunningJob book-scoped (F5)' is contained in main. NOTES (out of scope for this row, not counted against the verdict): (a) `listJobs` at :450-455 filters only `if (book)` — an omitted book returns every book's jobs; that is the separate GET /jobs leak tracked as vefur-adjacent issue #328 under campaign C5 (memory: fix changed to DELETE the zero-caller route), not this finding's 'is a job running' check. (b) The service comment at :464-468 says chapter is deliberately NOT normalized; any shape mismatch between routes/pipeline.js `normalizeChapter`, segment-editor `req.chapterNum` and publicationService `chapterNum` would cause a guard to FAIL TO FIRE (false negative), never a cross-book false positive — a different defect class from the one claimed here.
- ✅ upheld — Fix is complete at every site the finding enumerated; I could not find a surviving path. (1) The check itself, server/services/pipelineService.js:469-481, is `job.book === book && job.chapter === chapter && job.type === type && job.status === 'running'` — book is inside the conjunction, so returning book B's job for a book-A query requires B === A. That is an impossibility argument, not a plausibility one. (2) All three `jobs.set` creation sites stamp `book`: spawnJob (:383-391 `const job = { id: jobId, type, book, chapter, ...}`), the runPipeline inline job (:282-287 `type: 'pipeline', book, chapter,`), and the generate-tm inline job (:885-889 `book, chapter: chapter ?? 'all',`). All six spawnJob wrappers forward it — runExtract/runProtect/runUnprotect/runInject/runRender pass `book`, and runFetchSource (:794-800) passes `book: slug, chapter: null`, matching the admin dedupe's `(slug, null, 'fetch-source')` shape exactly. So no job object can carry an undefined book that a query could collide with. (3) Scope sweep of the four consumers the finding named: the running-check (routes/pipeline.js:75/:125/:175 all `pipeline.hasRunningJob(params.book, params.chapter, ...)`), the publish function (publicationService.js:216 `hasRunningJob(bookSlug, chapterNum, 'pipeline')`), the publication-status display (publicationService.js:353, same signature), and the admin source-fetch dedupe (routes/admin.js:262 and :335, both `hasRunningJob(slug, null, 'fetch-source')`) — plus segment-editor.js:1307 `hasRunningJob(req.params.book, req.chapterNum, 'pipeline')`. That is the complete grep for hasRunningJob in server/; there is no unscoped straggler. (4) Attacking the parameter chain rather than the service line: publishChapter and getPublicationStatus receive bookSlug as an argument, so a caller passing a constant or fallback would resurrect the defect. Every caller derives it per-request — routes/publication.js:75 (status), :142 (publishMtPreview), :197 (publishFaithful), :253 (publishLocalized) and :332 (overview) each do `const { bookSlug } = req.params;` guarded by requireHeadEditor('bookSlug') or the VALID_BOOKS check; publishMtPreview/Faithful/Localized (publicationService.js:278-293) are thin forwarders to publishChapter(bookSlug, ...). No non-request-derived book anywhere. (5) No second consumer of the old chapter-only shape survives: the `.chapter ===` sweep across server/ returns only pipelineService.js:453 (the listJobs book-aware filter) and :473 (inside the scoped conjunction); the only runningJob display is publicationService.js:353-359; client JS never lists jobs (it polls /api/pipeline/jobs/${jobId} with an id it already owns). (6) Regression tests pin the finding's literal failure scenario at server/__tests__/new-features.test.js:74-107 — chemistry ch3 running, `expect(hasRunningJob('liffraedi-2e', 3, 'pipeline')).toBeFalsy()`, with a positive control and a per-book fetch-source case. Attribution: commit ceb53bb8 'fix(item12): pipeline jobs carry book; hasRunningJob book-scoped (F5)' exists in history. Residual out-of-scope observations (not this claim): listJobs filters by truthiness so chapter 0 / null-chapter jobs are unfilterable; chapter type-shape consistency across entry points is unverified but a mismatch yields a false NEGATIVE, the opposite of this finding; getJob and the unscoped GET /jobs listing remain, which is the separately tracked issue #328 / C5.


## Group: `sections-routes`
### `code-2` — CLOSED
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-code-review.md § "### 2. `server/routes/sections.js:627` — Cross-book section actions allowed for any head-editor" (High, CONFIRMED, lens: authz; also row 2 of the findings table at :72 and the joint-summary cluster at :692)
**Original severity:** (unrated)
**Claim:** The four mutating section routes (approve-review, assign-reviewer :296, assign-localizer :378, request-changes :698) gate on a global `requireRole(ROLES.HEAD_EDITOR)` with no per-book ownership check, and the status route's elevated branch (:506-514) is a bare role check too — so a head-editor of one book can approve/assign/request-changes on another book's section by knowing its numeric section ID.
**Current location:** `server/routes/sections.js:88-92 (assign-reviewer), :171-175 (assign-localizer), :425-429 (approve-review), :497-501 (request-changes), :303-313 (status elevated branch)`

**Evidence:**

Test-pinned: server/__tests__/crossBookAuthz.test.js:327-338 tables all four routes, :378 (own-book passes via the section resolver), :384-388 (status elevated branch cross-book 403 / owning HE passes), :402 (admin bypass).
```
**Notes:** The review's claim was scoped to the four mutating routes plus the status route's ELEVATED branch; all five are now book-scoped, so the claim as written is closed. Residual outside this claim (do not read as reopening it): the status route's NON-elevated transitions still gate on bare `requireRole(ROLES.EDITOR)` (sections.js:258-262), and `submit-review` likewise (:359-363) — that is separately registered as B1-F2 in docs/plans/2026-07-11-pre-semester-coding-campaign.md:27 and carried into the live campaign at docs/plans/2026-07-21-post-item17-followup-campaign.md:87 under C5 ("adopt the established `requireBookAccessForSection` pattern"). Minor observation, not a ledger row: `loadSection` now runs BEFORE the role/ownership guard on the four routes, so a plain authenticated editor gets 404-vs-403 discrimination on section-ID existence — an enumeration oracle only, no data returned.
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — Defect not reachable. I re-located the four named mutating routes by route path in server/routes/sections.js (assign-reviewer :88, assign-localizer :171, approve-review :425, request-changes :497) and each now runs `loadSection` followed by `requireHeadEditorFor((req) => req.sectionData?.bookSlug)` (:92, :175, :429, :501), with the guard imported at :17. server/middleware/requireRole.js `requireHeadEditorFor` resolves the book from the loaded section and returns 403 unless `req.user.books && req.user.books.includes(book)` (:135-142), with ADMIN bypassing at :119-121 and a falsy book giving 404 — i.e. fail-closed, so a head-editor of book A hitting book B's numeric section ID cannot pass. The book values are commensurable and the failure direction is safe: getSection maps `rb.slug AS book_slug` → `bookSlug` (bookRegistration.js:786-814) and JWT `books` is `getHeadEditorBooks` → `bookAccess[].book` slugs (auth.js:210-213, :305; userService.js:522-526), so any mismatch denies rather than admits. The status route's elevated branch is scoped inline at sections.js:301-313: `const isOwningHeadEditor = req.user.role === ROLES.HEAD_EDITOR && req.user.books?.includes(section.bookSlug); if (req.user.role !== ROLES.ADMIN && !isOwningHeadEditor) return 403`. I also checked the capability, not just the URL: `grep -rn "updateSectionStatus|assignLinguisticReviewer|assignLocalizer" server/routes server/services` returns callers ONLY inside sections.js (:115, :198, :316, :384, :441, :524) — no second router reaches those mutations by section ID behind a global role check; and no other route file defines assign-reviewer/assign-localizer/approve-review/request-changes. Fixing commit fc6b8b67 is an ancestor of HEAD (verified with git merge-base --is-ancestor). RESIDUAL, explicitly outside this finding's scope (the review's code block and Fix line both scope the status route to "the elevated branch (:506-514)"): non-elevated transitions on POST /:sectionId/status are still gated only by `requireRole(ROLES.EDITOR)` at sections.js:261 with no book scope, so any editor can drive another book's section through e.g. mt_uploaded→review_assigned or review_submitted→review_in_progress. That is an adjacent surface, not survival of the claimed failure.
- ✅ upheld — I enumerated every site the claim names and every sibling that could reach the same mutation, and all of them are book-scoped on main (server/routes/sections.js is byte-identical to main; `git diff main` empty).

(1) All four mutating routes run `loadSection` FIRST and then the resolver guard — assign-reviewer at server/routes/sections.js:88-92, assign-localizer :171-175, approve-review :425-429, request-changes :497-501, each literally `requireHeadEditorFor((req) => req.sectionData?.bookSlug)`; the guard is imported at :17. Middleware order matters here and it is correct (loadSection before the guard), so `req.sectionData` is always populated when the resolver runs.

(2) The status route's elevated branch — the fifth site the claim names — is scoped inline at server/routes/sections.js:302-313: `const headEditorRequired = ['review_approved','localization_approved']; ... const isOwningHeadEditor = req.user.role === ROLES.HEAD_EDITOR && req.user.books?.includes(section.bookSlug); if (req.user.role !== ROLES.ADMIN && !isOwningHeadEditor) return res.status(403)`.

(3) The guard itself makes the failure impossible rather than merely unlikely: server/middleware/requireRole.js:135-142 grants only on `req.user.books.includes(book)` and otherwise falls through to 403; `!book` → 404 at :132; admin bypass at :119-121. Fail-closed is real, not assumed: middleware/requireAuth.js:58,89 set `books: decoded.books || []`, so `books` is always an array (a head-editor with no assignments gets `[]` → `includes()` false → 403), and services/userService.js:522-526 `getHeadEditorBooks` returns only rows where `ba.role === HEAD_EDITOR`, i.e. assigned slugs, not all books. bookRegistration.getSection (services/bookRegistration.js:786-814) INNER-JOINs registered_books and maps `bookSlug: section.book_slug`, so the resolver key exists and is the right case — no silent-undefined path.

Scope attack (sibling call sites), all negative: `grep -rn 'assignLinguisticReviewer|assignLocalizer|updateSectionStatus'` over server/ (excluding tests) returns ONLY routes/sections.js:115,198,316,384,441,524 plus the service definitions — no second router mutates sections. The only raw `UPDATE book_sections` in the tree is services/bookRegistration.js:918 inside `updateSectionStatus`; every other `book_sections` reference in routes/services is a SELECT/JOIN (bookDataGenerator.js:85, localizationLog.js:388, localizationSuggestions.js:384,468). The other exported section mutators (`scanAndUpdateStatus`/`scanStatusDryRun`) are reachable only from routes/status.js:1827,1860 (`POST /:book/sync`, `POST /:book/:chapter/sync`), both `requireAuth, requireAdmin()` — not a head-editor vector. sections.js has no `router.use`, and its full route set is the seven at :53,:88,:171,:258,:359,:425,:497.

Attribution and pinning: `git log -S"requireHeadEditorFor((req) => req.sectionData?.bookSlug)" -- server/routes/sections.js` → fc6b8b67 'fix(authz): book-scope section actions + elevated status transitions (review finding 2)'; `git merge-base --is-ancestor fc6b8b67 HEAD` succeeds. Its diff touches exactly the five sites the finding names, nothing narrowed. Regression-pinned in server/__tests__/crossBookAuthz.test.js:324-403 (cross-book 403 / owner-passes / plain-editor 403 / admin-bypass for all four routes, plus a resolver-discrimination case on section 47 in the OTHER head-editor's book, plus both elevated-status cases).

NOTE (not a refutation, deliberately not flipping this verdict): `POST /:sectionId/status` still gates its NON-elevated branch on `requireRole(ROLES.EDITOR)` at server/routes/sections.js:258-262 with no book check, so any editor can drive another book's section through transitions such as mt_uploaded→review_assigned, review_approved→faithful_published and localization_approved→localized_published (transition table at :275-290). Finding 2's text and its own prescribed fix both confine the status route to 'the status route's elevated branch (:506-514)', and its failure scenario is a head-editor acting cross-book on approve/assign/request-changes — different actor class, different branch. Flagging as a note per scope discipline.
- ✅ upheld — Mechanism traced end-to-end and it stops the described failure at every step. (1) Guard is imported: server/routes/sections.js:17 `const { requireRole, requireHeadEditorFor, ROLES } = require('../middleware/requireRole');` — the review's 2nd pass ('never imported into this file at all') no longer holds. (2) All four named mutating routes run `loadSection` then `requireHeadEditorFor((req) => req.sectionData?.bookSlug)`: assign-reviewer :88-92, assign-localizer :171-175, approve-review :425-429, request-changes :497-501 (verified by reading the whole 573-line file, not by line number). (3) The resolver's data is real: bookRegistration.getSection JOINs registered_books and returns `bookSlug: section.book_slug` (server/services/bookRegistration.js:811-814), so the middleware receives the section's OWNING book, exactly the datum the review said was loaded but never compared. (4) The compare exists and is fail-closed: server/middleware/requireRole.js:135-142 `if (req.user.books && req.user.books.includes(book)) return next();` else 403 — a missing/empty `books` falls through to 403, a falsy resolved book 404s at :131-133, admin bypasses at :119-121, sub-head-editor roles 403 at :110-116. (5) `req.user.books` is genuine head-editor ownership, not assignments: server/services/auth.js:213 `books: userService.getHeadEditorBooks(dbUser)` → userService.js:522-525 filters `bookAccess` to `ba.role === ROLES.HEAD_EDITOR`, carried into the signed JWT at auth.js:252 (`books: user.books || []`), so 'HE of book A knows book B's numeric section id' now yields 403. (6) Status route's elevated branch is scoped inline at sections.js:303-313: `const isOwningHeadEditor = req.user.role === ROLES.HEAD_EDITOR && req.user.books?.includes(section.bookSlug); if (req.user.role !== ROLES.ADMIN && !isOwningHeadEditor) return res.status(403)`. (7) No alternate path to the same mutations: `bookRegistration.updateSectionStatus`/`assignLinguisticReviewer`/`assignLocalizer` have no callers outside these guarded routes (grep over server/, excluding tests), and the router is mounted once at server/index.js:244. (8) Pinned end-to-end by server/__tests__/crossBookAuthz.test.js, which mints a REAL JWT (mintToken :31-43, same payload shape as auth.js createToken) against the real mounted router: cross-book 403 for all four routes, owning-HE clears authz, plain editor 403, admin bypass, plus :382 section 47 (efnafraedi-2e) → 200 for HE_A, which discriminates a real resolver from a hardcoded slug; :384-388 cover the elevated status branch both ways. Attribution: fc6b8b67 'fix(authz): book-scope section actions + elevated status transitions (review finding 2)', merged via 41105021 (PR #268); both verified ancestors of HEAD. CLOSED. Residue, explicitly outside this claim (notes, not a refutation): NON-elevated transitions on POST /:sectionId/status are still `requireAuth, requireRole(ROLES.EDITOR), loadSection` (sections.js:258-262) with no book scope, so any editor can drive another book's section mt_uploaded→review_assigned→review_in_progress. The review confined its status concern to 'the status route's elevated branch (:506-514)' and its fix said 'extend the same ownership check to the status route's elevated branch'; its failure scenario enumerates only approve-review/assign-reviewer/assign-localizer/request-changes. All five described paths are now impossible.

### `code-4` — CLOSED
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-code-review.md § "### 4. `server/routes/sections.js:156` — Unscoped file upload into human-verified content directories" (Medium, CONFIRMED, lens: authz; also row 4 of the findings table at :74 and the joint-summary cluster at :692)
**Original severity:** (unrated)
**Claim:** `POST /:sectionId/upload/:uploadType` required only the base editor role with no book scope, so any editor could upload a file into any book's `03-faithful-translation/` or `04-localized-content/` directories (destination derived from the loaded section's book) and change that section's workflow status.
**Current location:** `n/a — route removed from server/routes/sections.js (the file's complete route set is now :53 GET /:sectionId, :88 assign-reviewer, :171 assign-localizer, :258 status, :359 submit-review, :425 approve-review, :497 request-changes); regression guard at server/__tests__/crossBookAuthz.test.js:513-524`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_

### `code-6` — CLOSED
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-code-review.md §6 (summary table row 6; detail section "### 6. server/routes/segment-editor.js:1111"); also Batch C of the joint batching section

**Claim:** The "Vista + Birta" (apply-and-render) route calls applyApprovedEdits BEFORE checking pipelineService.hasRunningJob, so a concurrent pipeline job leaves the edits written to the faithful file but never rendered, while the browser sees a bare 409 and reports total failure (and the retry then fails differently with "all approved edits have already been applied").
**Current location:** `server/routes/segment-editor.js:1296-1333 (route POST /:book/:chapter/:moduleId/apply-and-render; guard at :1307, apply at :1325)`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_

```
server/routes/segment-editor.js:1305-1325 — the order is now guard-then-write, with an explicit comment naming the finding:

      // Guard FIRST (item 12, F6): a running pipeline means we could not

      // or the applied-but-unrendered dead-end returns via 500.
      if (!pipelineService.hasCapacity(2)) {
        return res.status(409).json({
          error: 'Pipeline queue is full — try again shortly',
        });
      }

      // Apply edits to files
      const applyResult = segmentEditor.applyApprovedEdits(
```
**Notes:** Fixing commit located by `git log -S"Guard FIRST (item 12, F6)"`: be98d5de "fix(item12): apply-and-render checks running pipeline before applying (F6)". Two things beyond the review's minimum fix also landed: (a) hasRunningJob is now book-scoped — it takes req.params.book as its first argument, which is the fix for code finding #5/#1 (calibrated CLOSED) and removes the cross-book false-positive the review said made #6 worse; (b) a second guard was added for pipeline capacity (runPipeline throws over MAX_JOBS), closing the same applied-but-unrendered dead-end via the 500 path. The 409 body no longer carries an `applied` field, consistent with nothing having been applied.

- ✅ upheld — Hunted the defect, not the fix. Route re-located by path: server/routes/segment-editor.js:1296 router.post('/:book/:chapter/:moduleId/apply-and-render'). The failure requires applyApprovedEdits to run BEFORE the hasRunningJob check; currently the guard is at :1307 (const existing = pipelineService.hasRunningJob(req.params.book, req.chapterNum, 'pipeline')) with an early return res.status(409) at :1309-1312, and applyApprovedEdits is at :1325 — so no 409 can be reached after a file write. I also chased the one path that could recreate the same applied-but-unrendered dead-end via 500: pipelineService.runPipeline (server/services/pipelineService.js:272) throws synchronously ONLY at :274 'if (runningJobCount() >= MAX_JOBS)'; everything after jobs.set(jobId, job) (:298) runs inside the async IIFE and cannot throw back into the route. That single throw is pre-empted by the hasCapacity(2) guard at :1318 (hasCapacity, pipelineService.js:366, is runningJobCount() + extraJobs <= MAX_JOBS, so passing means count <= 3 < 5). No interleaving window: the handler is a plain non-async (req, res) => { with no await between guard, apply and runPipeline, so on Node's single thread no other request can start a chapter job in between. Attribution: commit be98d5de 'fix(item12): apply-and-render checks running pipeline before applying (F6)'.
- ✅ upheld — Traced the review's exact failure step-by-step through today's code and it terminates at step 2. server/routes/segment-editor.js POST '/:book/:chapter/:moduleId/apply-and-render' (handler body starts :1302) now runs `pipelineService.hasRunningJob(req.params.book, req.chapterNum, 'pipeline')` at :1307 and returns 409 at :1308-1313 BEFORE `segmentEditor.applyApprovedEdits(...)` at :1325 — so no file write and no applied_at is set on the 409 path, which also kills the review's second-order symptom (the retry dying on 'already been applied'). The guard is genuinely atomic: applyApprovedEdits is synchronous and there is no await between the guard and `pipelineService.runPipeline` at :1332, so nothing can interleave. I attacked the two remaining post-apply throw paths and both are covered: (a) the only throw runPipeline can raise synchronously is the MAX_JOBS guard at pipelineService.js:274, and it is pre-empted by `if (!pipelineService.hasCapacity(2))` at segment-editor.js:1318 (hasCapacity at pipelineService.js:366-368 = runningJobCount()+2 <= 5, matching runPipeline's peak of parent+one phase child); (b) every later throw lands inside runPipeline's async IIFE, which is fully try/caught (pipelineService.js:340-345, sets job.status='failed') and therefore cannot surface as a post-apply 409/500. The cross-book false-409 amplifier the finding leaned on (finding #5) is also gone: hasRunningJob (pipelineService.js:469-481) now compares job.book, and both callers pass numeric chapters (middleware/validateParams.js:28-33 req.chapterNum; routes/pipeline.js:43 normalizeChapter), so the guard actually matches real jobs rather than silently missing. Regression-pinned behaviorally, not statically, by server/__tests__/applyAndRenderGuard.test.js:76-115, which asserts status 409 AND `row.applied_at` is null / status still 'approved'. Attribution: commit be98d5de 'fix(item12): apply-and-render checks running pipeline before applying (F6)'. CLOSED — I could not construct a surviving path.

The decisive point is that there is NO YIELD POINT between guard and write: the handler is non-async, segmentEditorService.js:831 `function applyApprovedEdits(book, chapter, moduleId, options = {})` is a plain synchronous function over synchronous better-sqlite3/fs, so no concurrent request can interleave and make the guard stale. The capacity arithmetic also holds: hasCapacity(2) is `runningJobCount() + 2 <= 5` (pipelineService.js:366-368), i.e. count <= 3, so runPipeline's own `if (runningJobCount() >= MAX_JOBS) throw` (pipelineService.js:274) and spawnJob's identical throw (:375) cannot fire after the apply. SCOPE ENUMERATED: applyApprovedEdits has exactly three non-test call sites (segment-editor.js:887, :1259, :1325); :1259 is the plain /apply route and :887 is the auto-apply inside POST /reviews/:reviewId/complete — neither launches a render, so neither can strand edits behind a pipeline 409. The only other runPipeline callers are routes/pipeline.js:198 (no apply) and publicationService.js:225, which already guards at :216 (the site the review itself called correct). Chapter typing agrees across the guard (validateParams.js:33 sets req.chapterNum via parseInt; pipeline.js validateParams returns `chapter: chapterNum`), so hasRunningJob's strict === cannot silently miss on type. Client uses a single call (public/js/segment-editor.js:2223). Pinned by server/__tests__/applyAndRenderGuard.test.js, which asserts on a 409 that `row.status === 'approved'` and `row.applied_at` is null, plus a second case for the capacity path. Register corroboration: the predecessor register logs only I12-R2, a client-side 409 polish, as the remaining tail. Residual (note only, different failure class): the guard matches type 'pipeline' only, so a standalone inject/render job for the same book+chapter is invisible to it — but in that case a render IS still launched, so the reviewed symptom (edits applied, no render, bare 409) does not recur.

**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-code-review.md §21 (summary table row 21; detail section "### 21. server/routes/segment-editor.js:431"); Batch E "Fail-loud sweep"
**Original severity:** (unrated)
**Claim:** Several activity-log (audit-trail) writes in this file — specifically on the approve, reject and unapprove actions — are wrapped in bare `catch { /* fire-and-forget */ }` blocks that discard the failure with no log line, unlike a sibling block in the same file that logs it; the second pass corrected the count from ~11 candidates to 6 genuinely silent ones.
**Current location:** `server/routes/segment-editor.js:714-830 (approve :726, reject :760, discuss :793, unapprove :822) + server/services/activityLog.js:160-201 + server/__tests__/activityLogCallsiteGuard.test.js`

**Evidence:**

```
The hand-rolled guards were deleted, not re-logged: `grep -n "catch {" server/routes/segment-editor.js` returns nothing, and every `} catch` in the file (43 of them) binds an error identifier. activityLog.log is now called bare inside each route's outer try, e.g. approve at server/routes/segment-editor.js:726 and unapprove at :822:

The swallow moved into the service, which is contractually never-throw AND fail-loud — server/services/activityLog.js:155-201:

 * Log an activity. NEVER throws (design D1, batch 4): the mutation that
 * triggered an audit write must not fail over its audit record. On any
 * failure this pino-logs 'Activity log write failed' and returns null —
 * the error log is the fail-loud channel for a broken audit trail.
...
  } catch (err) {
    logger.error(

A static tripwire prevents regression across ALL of routes/ and services/ recursively — server/__tests__/activityLogCallsiteGuard.test.js:30-32:

const SILENT_CATCH = /catch\s*(?:\([^)]*\)\s*)?\{\s*\/\*\s*fire-and-forget\s*\*\/\s*\}/;
const TRY_WRAPPED_AUDIT = /try\s*\{\s*activityLog\.log\(/;
const EMPTY_CATCH_NO_COMMENT = /catch\s*(?:\([^)]*\)\s*)?\{\s*\}/;
```
**Notes:** Fixing commit located by `git log -S"fire-and-forget" -- server/routes/segment-editor.js`: d996ff77 (2026-07-12) "refactor(audit): delete 23 hand-rolled activityLog guards; add call-site tripwire (12 were silent)" — 275 changed lines in segment-editor.js, and it also swept the other file the finding named (routes/localization-editor.js, 150 lines) plus publication.js and contentVersionService.js. So the sibling half of the claim is closed too; localization-editor.js now has zero bare catches. The fix is stronger than the review's prescription ("replace each silent catch with the logging version nearby"): rather than 23 duplicated log-the-error blocks it centralises the never-throw + pino-error contract in activityLog.log and bans the call-site idiom by test. Note for the register-keepers: the tripwire's third pattern (EMPTY_CATCH_NO_COMMENT) bans comment-less empty catches repo-wide in routes/+services/, which incidentally guards the same defect class as findings 20/22 in Batch E — those are outside this group and were not checked here.
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — Searched for the swallow itself rather than trusting the fix. In server/routes/segment-editor.js the regex for a comment-only or empty catch (catch\s*(\([^)]*\))?\s*\{\s*(/*...*/)?\s*\}) matches NOTHING, and the same scan over all of server/routes/ and server/services/ is likewise empty — so no audit write anywhere in those trees is discarded by a bodiless catch. The four named actions now call the logger bare inside their own outer handler: approve at :726, reject at :760, discuss at :793, unapprove at :822 (e.g. unapprove is 'const edit = segmentEditor.unapproveEdit(...); res.json(...); activityLog.log({ type: 'segment_edit_unapproved', ... })' with no inner try). The swallow that remains is in the service and is explicitly fail-loud, not silent — server/services/activityLog.js:160-201: the catch does logger.error({ err, type: options?.type, book: options?.book, userId: options?.userId }, 'Activity log write failed') and returns null, documented as a never-throw contract, so the bare call sites (including unapprove, which sends res.json BEFORE logging) cannot produce a headers-already-sent secondary failure either. Regression is statically fenced by server/__tests__/activityLogCallsiteGuard.test.js:30-32, which bans SILENT_CATCH, TRY_WRAPPED_AUDIT and EMPTY_CATCH_NO_COMMENT across a recursive scan of routes/ and services/. I also checked the review's second file (server/routes/localization-editor.js): all seven activityLog.log call sites there (:113, :156, :190, :395, :447, :606, :653) are bare too.
- ✅ upheld — The swallow is gone and the failure it hid is now loud. Verified mechanically, not by eyeball: a Perl scan for any catch block in server/routes/ + server/services/ whose body is empty or comments-only returns ZERO hits in routes/segment-editor.js and routes/localization-editor.js (the two files the finding named); the surviving comment-only catches live in routes/books.js and routes/status.js and are not audit writes, so they are outside this claim. The three named actions now call the audit write bare inside their own outer try: approve at segment-editor.js:726, reject at :767, discuss at :800, unapprove at :822 — e.g. `const edit = segmentEditor.unapproveEdit(...); res.json({ success: true, edit }); activityLog.log({ type: 'segment_edit_unapproved', ... });`. That is only safe because the swallow moved into a contractually never-throw, fail-loud service: activityLog.log (server/services/activityLog.js:160-203) wraps BOTH the destructuring of `options` and `stmts().insert.run(...)` — including the lazy `initStatements(getDb())` at :148-150, so a DB-open failure is inside the try — and its catch at :195-202 emits `logger.error({ err, type: options?.type, ... }, 'Activity log write failed')` and returns null. `logger` is real pino (server/lib/logger.js:18,34-43, JSON to stdout in production), not a no-op, so the review's exact scenario — 'that failure is caught and thrown away with no log message at all' — now produces a durable error record. The never-throw contract also means the bare call cannot flip a committed mutation into a 400/500 via the surrounding catch. Regression is fenced across ALL of routes/ and services/ recursively by server/__tests__/activityLogCallsiteGuard.test.js:30-32 (bans the fire-and-forget idiom, any try-wrapped activityLog.log, and any comment-less empty catch); I re-ran its TRY_WRAPPED_AUDIT and EMPTY_CATCH_NO_COMMENT patterns by hand over routes/+services/ and both match nothing today. CLOSED for both files named in the finding, not just the segment-editor half.
- ✅ upheld — CANNOT REFUTE — and critically, the second file the finding named checks out too. The finding covered 'this file AND localization-editor's route file'; the verifier only evidenced segment-editor.js, so I read localization-editor.js directly. All seven of its audit writes (:113, :156, :190, :395, :447, :606, :653) are bare `activityLog.log({...})` calls inside the route's outer try with an identifier-binding catch — no swallow — and the two non-activityLog audit writes there use exactly the logging pattern the finding prescribed: `} catch (logErr) { log.error({ err: logErr }, 'Audit log failed (single save)'); }` (~:436) and the bulk twin (~:640). In segment-editor.js the four named actions are bare calls with the swallow removed: approve :726, reject :760, discuss :793, unapprove :822 (`activityLog.log({ type: 'segment_edit_unapproved', ... })` with no try/catch of its own). No `catch {` substring occurs in segment-editor.js or localization-editor.js at all (the only files with the bare form are status.js 13x, terminology.js 1x, books.js 1x), and all 43 `} catch` in segment-editor.js bind an identifier (43 total == 43 `} catch (`). The swallow moved into a contractually never-throw, fail-loud service: activityLog.js:160-201 wraps the insert and on failure does `logger.error({ err, type: options?.type, ... }, 'Activity log write failed'); return null;` — so every call site in the codebase, not just the six, now emits a log line on a failed audit write. Regression is pinned recursively over routes/ and services/ by server/__tests__/activityLogCallsiteGuard.test.js (SILENT_CATCH, TRY_WRAPPED_AUDIT, EMPTY_CATCH_NO_COMMENT), and no try-wrapped activityLog.log exists anywhere (grep -B1 over routes/ + services/ returns nothing). Residual (note only, does not revive this finding): the guard's EMPTY_CATCH_NO_COMMENT is evaded by comment-only catches — status.js has several `catch { /* ignore */ }` and `/* skip */` blocks that pass it — but status.js never calls activityLog.log (only activityLog.search), so no audit write is swallowed there. Separately noted for the parent, not audited here: in segment-editor.js approve/reject/discuss, `notifyDecision(...)` runs inside the same try after a successful state change, so a throw there would return 400 for an action that already succeeded.

**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-code-review.md § "Finding details" → "### 11. server/views/status.html:643 — Pipeline stage badges read the wrong data shape and never show 'done'" (Medium, CONFIRMED, lens: contract)
**Original severity:** (unrated)
**Claim:** status.html's pipeline-badge loader did `var stages = (data.status && data.status.stages) || data.status || {}`, but the /api/status/:book/:chapter response has no top-level `status` key — it returns stages as a top-level ARRAY of {stage, complete, …} — so `stages` collapsed to `{}` and every stage badge plus both publication badges rendered incomplete regardless of real pipeline state (books.html already handled the same response correctly).

```
server/views/status.html:643-649 now reads the array and keys it by stage name:

// API returns stages as an array of {stage, complete, ...} — convert to object keyed by name
var stagesArr = data.stages || [];
var stages = {};
for (var i = 0; i < stagesArr.length; i++) {
  stages[stagesArr[i].stage] = stagesArr[i];
}
var pub = stages.publication || {};
`​``
and the badge loop at :661 `var done = stages[b.key] && stages[b.key].complete;`. `grep -n "data\.status" server/views/status.html` → no matches, so the broken fallback chain is gone entirely. The producer side confirms the shape: `formatChapterStatus` (server/routes/status.js:1503-1531) builds `const stages = PIPELINE_STAGES.map(...)` with `{ stage, status, symbol, complete, ... }` and, for `stage === 'publication'`, attaches `entry.mtPreview / entry.faithful / entry.localized` — exactly what the fixed view reads via `pub.mtPreview.complete` / `pub.faithful.complete`.

**Original severity:** (unrated)
**Claim:** The chapter/activity timeline in status.html parsed a date from one of two field names the server never sends (the server sends only the camelCase raw field), so `new Date(undefined)` rendered the literal text "Invalid Date" as the timestamp prefix on every timeline row — while the server was already sending a ready-to-display "time ago" string the page ignored.
**Current location:** `server/views/status.html:700-713 (function `renderTimeline`)`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
server/views/status.html:708 now uses the server's pre-formatted string with no client-side date parsing at all — the exact fix the review recommended:
`​``js
var timeStr = a.timeAgo || '';

li.innerHTML = '<span class="timeline-time">' + escapeHtml(timeStr) + '</span>' +
  '<span class="timeline-msg">' + escapeHtml(a.description || a.message || a.type) + '</span>';
`​``
Producer confirmed: `loadTimeline` (status.html:680) fetches `/api/status/activity/timeline`, and that route maps every row through `timeAgo: formatTimeAgo(activity.createdAt)` at server/routes/status.js:364 (sibling at :223). No `new Date(` remains in `renderTimeline`.
```

Landed in PR #303 per docs/plans/2026-07-11-pre-semester-coding-campaign.md:102 (F11/F12/F13/F18/F26/F27/F31). Caveat worth noting (not a reopening): register line 111 records I16-R7 deferred minor "F27 escapeHtml-wrap unpinned" — the fix is in the code and the escapeHtml wrap is present, it is just not covered by a static pin, so a future edit could regress it undetected.
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — CLOSED, and the scope attack finds no surviving site. server/views/status.html:700-713 `renderTimeline` does all timestamping via `var timeStr = a.timeAgo || '';` (:708) — `grep -c "new Date(" server/views/status.html` returns **0**, so no client-side date parsing remains anywhere in the file, not merely in this function; there is exactly one timeline renderer (`timeline-list` is written only at :689 empty-state, :703 clear, :712 append). Producer chain verified end-to-end rather than assumed: status.html:681 fetches `/api/status/activity/timeline`, whose handler (server/routes/status.js:349-370) maps every row `{...activity, timeAgo: formatTimeAgo(activity.createdAt)}` (:364); `activityLog.search` returns `rows.map(parseRow)` and `parseRow` (server/services/activityLog.js:298-310) sets `createdAt: row.created_at` — so the camelCase field the formatter reads is the one actually present, and `created_at DATETIME DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` (server/migrations/040-service-table-ownership.js:27) plus the explicit `createdAt` on insert (activityLog.js:194) means `formatTimeAgo` cannot itself emit the string 'Invalid Date'. Fixing commit 0ce73b45 'timeline shows the server's timeAgo instead of Invalid Date (F27, item 16 PR1)'; pinned at server/__tests__/viewRouteContracts.test.js:67-74.
- ✅ upheld — Defect not reachable, including the server-side path that could have reproduced the same symptom. renderTimeline (server/views/status.html:700-718) does no date parsing at all — `var timeStr = a.timeAgo || '';` then `escapeHtml(timeStr)`; there is no `new Date(` anywhere in the function, so the literal 'Invalid Date' can no longer be produced client-side. I then chased the one remaining way the symptom could survive: whether the server's `timeAgo` is itself garbage. `formatTimeAgo` (server/routes/status.js:1663-1673) does `new Date(dateString)` with NO guard and falls through to `date.toLocaleDateString('is-IS')`, which WOULD emit 'Invalid Date' if fed undefined — so the field name mapping is load-bearing. It holds: the route at :349-366 maps every row through `timeAgo: formatTimeAgo(activity.createdAt)`, and `activityLog.search()` (server/services/activityLog.js:243-291) returns `rows.map(parseRow)`, where parseRow (:298-311) sets `createdAt: row.created_at` (and `description: row.description`, so the message side is populated too). `created_at` is `DATETIME DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` (server/migrations/040-service-table-ownership.js:27), a parseable timestamp string. So the client reads a field the server genuinely sends, and the server formats from a field that genuinely exists. Attribution: commit 0ce73b45 'fix(views): timeline shows the server's timeAgo instead of Invalid Date (F27, item 16 PR1)', found via `git log -S 'a.timeAgo' -- server/views/status.html`.
- ✅ upheld — server/views/status.html:700-713 `renderTimeline` does zero client-side date parsing: `var timeStr = a.timeAgo || '';` at :708, rendered at :710; there is no `new Date(` left in the function. Producer sends it: the page fetches /api/status/activity/timeline (:681) and server/routes/status.js:362-367 maps every row through `timeAgo: formatTimeAgo(activity.createdAt)`. I specifically attacked the obvious survival path — `formatTimeAgo` (status.js:1663-1673) still does `new Date(dateString)` and, on an invalid input, falls through every branch to `date.toLocaleDateString('is-IS')`, which would emit the literal string 'Invalid Date' that the client now prints verbatim. That path is unreachable: `activityLog.search` returns `rows.map(parseRow)` (services/activityLog.js:287) and `parseRow` sets `createdAt: row.created_at` (:309), while the ONLY insert statement (:76-79) lists eight columns and does not include `created_at`, so the `created_at DATETIME DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` default from migration 040-service-table-ownership.js:27 always fires. `createdAt` is therefore never undefined, and even if it somehow were, the worst client-side outcome is the empty-string fallback, not the described 'Invalid Date' prefix. CLOSED.

### `code-37` — CLOSED
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-code-review.md § "Finding details" → "### 37. server/routes/status.js:75 — A status-lookup failure falls back to a cached file with no log line" (Low, downgraded from Medium; PLAUSIBLE, downgraded from CONFIRMED; lens: failloud)
**Original severity:** (unrated)
**Claim:** `getStatusDataFromDb`'s outer `catch { return JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(statusPath, 'utf-8')); }` silently fell back to the on-disk status.json when the pipeline-status DB lookup threw, logging nothing — an observability gap across the six call sites; recommended fix was a single log line in that catch block.
**Current location:** `server/routes/status.js:73-83 (function `getStatusDataFromDb`, the outer catch)`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
The catch now logs before falling back — the exact one-line fix requested, at warn level with the error and both identifiers as structured fields:
`​``js
} catch (err) {
  log.warn(
    { err, bookSlug, chapterNum },
    'Pipeline status DB read failed; serving cached status.json'
  );
  // Fallback: read status.json directly if DB is unavailable
  const chDir = chapterNum === -1 ? 'appendices' : `ch${String(chapterNum).padStart(2, '0')}`;
  const statusPath = path.join(PROJECT_ROOT, 'books', bookSlug, 'chapters', chDir, 'status.json');
  return JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(statusPath, 'utf-8'));
}
`​``
`log` is the shared pino logger (`const log = require('../lib/logger')`, server/routes/status.js:19). The bare `catch {}` that remains at :67-69 is the *inner* metadata read ("No status.json — that's fine, we have DB data"), which is not the claimed defect and is a benign, documented no-op.
```
