## Group: `save-retry-client`
### `code-8` — CLOSED
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-code-review.md — findings table row 8 (line 78) + detail §8 "`server/public/js/saveRetry.js:178` — A successful save doesn't cancel an earlier failed save still queued for retry"; also joint-summary Batch D ("Editor save-path concurrency")
**Original severity:** Medium (CONFIRMED, lens: state)
**Claim:** In saveRetry.js, the `response.ok` success path returned `response.json()` without calling `removeFromQueue(key)` or cancelling the pending retry timer, so an earlier transiently-failed save for the same segment stayed queued and could later replay (timer fire, or automatic queue replay on page reload up to an hour later) and silently overwrite the newer, already-successful save.
**Current location:** `server/public/js/saveRetry.js:196 (call site), :70-76 (`cancelPending` body), :108-123 + :133-134 + :141 + :162 (qid identity guard), :99-103 (one-timer-per-key)`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
Success path now purges — `attempt()` at server/public/js/saveRetry.js:194-198:
`​``js
.then(function (response) {
  if (response.ok) {
    cancelPending(key);
    return response.json();
  }
`​``
and `cancelPending` kills BOTH replay vectors the review named — the live timer AND the persisted localStorage entry that a page reload would replay — at :70-76:
`​``js
function cancelPending(key) {
  if (activeTimers[key] !== undefined) {
    clearTimeoutFn(activeTimers[key]);
    delete activeTimers[key];
  }
  removeFromQueue(key);
}
`​``
The review's suggested "stamp queue entries with a sequence number" was also implemented, as `qid`, and is re-checked at three points — at timer-fire (:133-134 `const stored = currentEntry(queueItem.key, queueItem.qid); if (!stored) return;`) and again after the fetch settles in BOTH continuations (:141 and :162 `if (!currentEntry(stored.key, stored.qid)) return;`), with `currentEntry` at :118-123:
`​``js
function currentEntry(key, qid) {
  const live = loadQueue().find(function (q) { return q.key === key; });
  return live && live.qid === qid ? live : null;
}
`​``
`queueForRetry` at :99-103 additionally enforces exactly one live timer per key. Corroborated by a BEHAVIORAL (not static-pin) test: server/__tests__/saveRetryQueue.test.js:107 `it('a successful save purges an earlier failed save queued under the same key', ...)` asserting `expect(h.queue()).toEqual([])`, plus :242 `// A late success on the stale retry must not purge the newer save.`
```
**Notes:** Attribution: commit e73f164d `fix(item13): saveRetry — success purges queue, qid identity check, one timer per key (finding 8)` plus follow-up 231869f1 `fix(item13): saveRetry re-checks qid identity after fetch settles (finding B)`, both merged in PR #299 (merge commit 278443dd, `fix/item13-concurrent-edit-lost-updates`) — the register at docs/plans/2026-07-11-pre-semester-coding-campaign.md:99 (item 13, Batch 6) names this fix explicitly. Line 178 today is the unrelated closing `}, delay);` of `executeRetry` — pure line drift, the finding was re-located by symbol (`response.ok` success path in `attempt`). TWO REGISTER-TRACKED RESIDUALS live in this same module and are NOT this claim (this claim was same-key), so no ledger rows for them: I13-R1 `[residual]` cross-key stale-batch replay (a queued `loc-auto:` per-module batch can still replay one stale segment after a newer per-segment `loc:` save succeeded — different retry keys, cancellation cannot see across them) and I13-R3 `[latent]` double-scheduled `processQueue` timers descending from one entry, which share one qid and therefore both pass the qid guard — both at docs/plans/2026-07-11-pre-semester-coding-campaign.md:131,133.
**Adversarial verdicts (1/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — Cannot refute — the defect is unreachable in current code, verified independently of the verifier's evidence. The review's fix ("remove any queued retry entry for that same segment and cancel its timer; consider stamping queue entries with a sequence number") is fully implemented. In server/public/js/saveRetry.js, `attempt()`'s success branch is no longer `if (response.ok) { return response.json(); }` — at :195-198 it calls `cancelPending(key)` BEFORE `return response.json()`, and `cancelPending` at :70-76 does both halves: `clearTimeoutFn(activeTimers[key]) / delete activeTimers[key]` and `removeFromQueue(key)` (which filters localStorage's `saveRetryQueue` by key at :62-68). I traced both replay vectors the finding names and both die: (a) TIMER FIRE — `activeTimers[key]` is cleared, and in the window where the timer had already fired and deleted its own entry (:129), the fire-time guard `const stored = currentEntry(queueItem.key, queueItem.qid); if (!stored) return;` (:133-134) plus the post-fetch re-checks at :141 and :162 abort, since `currentEntry` (:118-123) returns null once the entry is gone; (b) PAGE-RELOAD REPLAY — `processQueue()` (:273-300) reads `loadQueue()`, and the purged entry is simply absent, so the hour-long `EXPIRY_MS` window has nothing to replay. Key identity is what makes the purge land on the right entry, and I checked the real call sites rather than assuming: segment-editor.js:1379 uses `seg:${currentBook}/${currentChapter}/${currentModuleId}:${segmentId}` — deterministic per segment, so the failed save and the later successful save collide on the identical key, and `addToQueue` (:52-60) dedupes by key so at most one entry exists to remove. The `qid` sequence stamp (:213, :261) is the review's optional suggestion, also implemented. Notably the finding's own aggravating premise — "the server-side conflict check doesn't apply here, because both saves came from the same editor" — is still true (`checkEditConflict` in server/services/segmentEditorService.js:57-75 filters `editor_id != ?`), so the client-side purge is genuinely load-bearing, and it is present. Only one file named saveRetry.js exists under server/ (no bundled/minified shadow copy), and it is the file served via `<script src="/js/saveRetry.js">` from segment-editor.html:1614 and localization-editor.html:2074. Residual races I deliberately did NOT count as survival of this claim, since the finding scopes itself to "stayed queued and could later replay (timer fire … page reload)": a retry fetch already on the wire when the newer save succeeds (unfixable client-side; the stale continuation is muted by the :141/:162 qid guards), and cross-key non-purge between `seg:` and `acc:` (or `loc:` and `loc-auto:`), which are different operations, not the same segment save.
- ✅ upheld — CANNOT REFUTE — the described failure is mechanically impossible on main. I traced the review's exact sequence step by step.

LOAD-BEARING PRECONDITION I VERIFIED INDEPENDENTLY (the verifier did not): both saves must share the retry key for cancelPending(key) to reach the earlier entry. server/public/js/segment-editor.js:1380 builds it as `seg:${currentBook}/${currentChapter}/${currentModuleId}:${segmentId}` — deterministic per segment, no timestamp/nonce — so the newer save's purge targets exactly the earlier failed save's entry.

TRACE OF THE REVIEW'S SCENARIO:
(1) transient 500 -> queueForRetry -> addToQueue + executeRetry schedules activeTimers[key] (saveRetry.js:104-105, :128). Still happens.
(2) newer edit saves successfully -> attempt() success path at saveRetry.js:194-198 now calls `cancelPending(key)` BEFORE `return response.json()`.
(3) 'the old retry timer fires anyway' -> DEAD. cancelPending (:70-76) does `clearTimeoutFn(activeTimers[key]); delete activeTimers[key];`.
(4) 'the page reloads and the queue is replayed up to an hour later' -> DEAD. cancelPending also calls `removeFromQueue(key)` (:75), which rewrites STORAGE_KEY 'saveRetryQueue' filtered of that key (:62-68); processQueue (:273-274) then reads an empty queue on reload.
The review's sequence stops at step 3 — both named replay vectors are severed by one call.

TWO EXTRA CONFIRMATIONS I BANKED: (a) `find` shows exactly ONE saveRetry file outside node_modules (server/public/js/saveRetry.js) and it is served by `app.use(express.static(publicPath))` at server/index.js:170 — no stale bundled/minified second copy is what the browser actually loads; (b) a reload-scheduled retry is equally cancellable, because processQueue routes through the same `executeRetry` that populates `activeTimers[key]` (:128), the very slot cancelPending clears.

Corroborating (NOT my proof — cited to avoid circularity): behavioral tests at server/__tests__/saveRetryQueue.test.js:107 assert `h.queue()).toEqual([])` AND `h.timers.size).toBe(0)` then fire all timers and confirm the OLD body never replays.

RESIDUALS I FOUND BUT DELIBERATELY DID NOT STRETCH THE CLAIM TO COVER (all narrower mechanisms than the missing purge the review named, and all already logged): cross-key replay (a queued `seg:` edit survives a successful `acc:` accept — different keys, and the review's own prescribed fix `removeFromQueue(key)` was key-scoped too) is register item I13-R1; processQueue bypassing queueForRetry's one-timer-per-key is I13-R3 (dead today — both views load saveRetry.js as a sync <script>); and the in-flight race (timer fires mid-flight so the old body is already on the wire, with the qid guard at :141/:162 suppressing only client-side toast/queue effects) is a different mechanism from the one the review described.
- ❌ REFUTED — REFUTED — the fix is real but SAME-KEY-ONLY, and the finding's failure scenario survives across the sibling key namespaces that write the same content.

What IS closed (conceded): the exact literal site. `attempt()` success path now purges — server/public/js/saveRetry.js:194-198 `if (response.ok) { cancelPending(key); return response.json(); }` with `cancelPending` at :70-76 clearing `activeTimers[key]` AND calling `removeFromQueue(key)`; plus the qid identity guard (:118-123, :133-134, :141, :162) and one-timer-per-key (:99-103). If the newer successful save carries the SAME key, the stale entry cannot replay.

What SURVIVES: `cancelPending(key)` purges only an exact key match, but the same module content is saved under DISJOINT keys by sibling call sites of the same shared `attempt()`:
- server/public/js/localization-editor.js:182 `'loc-auto:' + book/chapter/module` (60s autosave → POST .../save-all, multi-segment batch)
- localization-editor.js:1029 `'loc:' + book/chapter/module + ':' + segmentId` (explicit single save → .../save)
- localization-editor.js:1147 `'loc-all:' + book/chapter/module` (Vista allt → .../save-all)
- segment-editor.js:1077 `acc:...:segmentId` vs :1379 `seg:...:segmentId` (accept vs edit-save of the same segment)
So: autosave of segment X fails 5xx → queued under `loc-auto:B/C/M` with X=v1 serialized in the body; editor re-edits X to v2 and the explicit save succeeds → `cancelPending('loc:B/C/M:X')` purges nothing; the `loc-auto` timer (or `processQueue()` on reload, up to EXPIRY_MS=1h, saveRetry.js:26/273-300) replays v1 over v2. That is verbatim the review's sentence: a successful save does not cancel the earlier failed save's queue entry, and the stale version replays over the newer one.

The project's OWN artifacts bound the fix to same-key: (a) the corroborating test the verifier cites is titled, at server/__tests__/saveRetryQueue.test.js:107, `it('a successful save purges an earlier failed save queued under the same key')` — and no test in that file exercises a cross-key purge (all cases at :78/:87/:97/:121/:134/:149/:180/:212/:231 are single-key); (b) the residual is registered as I13-R1 at docs/plans/2026-07-11-pre-semester-coding-campaign.md:131 — "cross-key stale-batch replay: a queued `loc-auto:` per-module batch can replay one stale segment after a newer per-segment (`loc:`) save succeeded — different retry keys, cancellation can't see across them" — logged in the SAME batch (#299) that shipped the success-purge fix. So this is OPEN-TRACKED (I13-R1, campaign batch 6), not untracked.

The register's named backstop does NOT hold in a reachable case. It claims "review-OFF replays carry stale `lastModified` → existing mtime 409". But on a module with no localized file yet, `loadModuleForLocalization` returns `lastModified = null` (server/services/segmentParser.js ~:287-293: `let lastModified = null; if (hasLocalized) { ... }`) → localization-editor.js:491 `edLastModified = edModuleData.lastModified || null` → the queued body omits the token entirely (`if (edLastModified != null) saveBody.lastModified = edLastModified;` at :173/:1020/:1138) → the server guard `if (lastModified != null)` (server/routes/localization-editor.js:322 and :495) never runs → the stale `loc-auto` batch is written unconditionally over the newer save, silently. Secondary weakening: even when the token is present the comparison tolerates a 1s window (`Math.abs(currentMtime - lastModified) > 1`, :328/:501).

Uncertainty resolved against CLOSED: I cannot point at any code that makes the described overwrite impossible on the cross-key path — the project itself documents it as live.

### `code-24` — CLOSED
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-code-review.md — findings table row 24 (line 94) + detail §24 "`server/public/js/saveRetry.js:209` — A conflict error drops its own explanation text"; also joint-summary Batch D
**Original severity:** Low (CONFIRMED, lens: contract)
**Claim:** The non-retryable error path built `new Error(data.error || 'Villa ' + response.status)`, reading only the machine-readable code; since a 409 returns `{error: 'conflict', message: <Icelandic explanation>}`, the user saw the bare English word "conflict" and the caller's own Icelandic fallback never fired either (the code checks "do we have a message" first). Fix: check the explanation field first, falling back to the short code.
**Current location:** `server/public/js/saveRetry.js:233-244 (message-first + the load-bearing two-arg `.then`), :246-267 (the trailing catch that must not swallow it)`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
Message-first ordering is exactly the review's prescribed fix, at server/public/js/saveRetry.js:233-238:
`​``js
return response.json().then(
  function (data) {
    const err = new Error(data.message || data.error || 'Villa ' + response.status);
    err.status = response.status;
    return Promise.reject(err);
  },
`​``
The user-visible half of the claim needed a second fix: the crafted rejection has to survive to the caller. The TWO-ARGUMENT `.then(onFulfilled, onRejected)` form is what makes it do so, and the code comment at :223-232 documents precisely the failure mode the register calls "the first fix was dead code":
`​``js
// Non-retryable — parse error and reject. Two-argument .then(onFulfilled,
// onRejected) — NOT a single-argument .then(onFulfilled) chained into a
// separate catch handler — is load-bearing here: ... a trailing catch would
// intercept the deliberate Promise.reject(err) crafted below (with the
// Icelandic conflict message from data.message/data.error) and silently
// replace it with the generic 'Villa <status>' text
`​``
Trace to the caller confirms it: the 409 sets `err.status = 409` (:236), so the trailing `.catch` at :246-248 evaluates `isRetryableError(err) && !err.status` = false and falls through to `return Promise.reject(err);` (:266) untouched — the Icelandic `data.message` reaches the editor. Corroborated (as a STATIC presence pin, not behavior) by server/__tests__/clientMessageContracts.test.js:19 `it('reads data.message before data.error', ...)`, whose comment at :29 restates the swallow hazard.
```
**Notes:** Attribution: PR #270 (merge commit b758dff6, `fix/segment-edit-exit-path`), across two commits — f7fe8ba0 `fix(ui): surface dropped server messages — conflict text + pipeline confirmation handshake` introduced `data.message || data.error`, and 94ac094e `fix(editor): final-review behavior fixes — live conflict message, ...` supplied the behavioral half (its own message says "saveRetry's crafted rejection was swallowed by its own .catch (two-arg .then now)"). I verified the two-arg shape separately via `git log -S 'response.json().then('` and `-S 'load-bearing here'` rather than trusting the string-presence commit, because the register at docs/plans/2026-07-11-pre-semester-coding-campaign.md:13 warns "the first fix was dead code, caught by adversarial verification". Re-verified independently and recorded as closed by item 13's Part D at docs/plans/2026-07-11-pre-semester-coding-campaign.md:99 ("Finding 24 ... verified already closed by #270 ... no code"). Line 209 today is the unrelated `credentials: options.credentials,` inside a queue item — pure line drift; re-located by symbol (`new Error(data.… || 'Villa ' + response.status)`). Out-of-scope observation, not a ledger row: the register's SR4-F3 notes `err.data.violations` from a 400 still never reaches the editor panes — `attempt` propagates only the Error, never attaching the parsed body — which is adjacent to but distinct from this claim.
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — Cannot refute — the exact prescribed fix is present and I traced the message end-to-end to the user without relying on the verifier's reasoning. The review's fix was "check the explanation field first, falling back to the short code"; server/public/js/saveRetry.js:235 now reads `const err = new Error(data.message || data.error || 'Villa ' + response.status);` — `data.message` first, so the finding's literal defect (`new Error(data.error || ...)` yielding the bare English word "conflict") no longer exists. I then verified the finding's actual user-visible failure scenario, which needs three more steps to survive: (1) the 409 body really does carry both fields — server/routes/segment-editor.js:476 `return res.status(409).json({ error: 'conflict', message: err.message })`, where `err.message` is the Icelandic `Annar yfirlesari (${conflict.editor_username}) hefur breytt þessum bút. Endurhlaðið til að sjá nýjustu útgáfu.` set in server/services/segmentEditorService.js:70-73; (2) the crafted rejection must reach the caller — the two-argument `response.json().then(onFulfilled, onRejected)` form at :233-244 means the second callback fires only if JSON parsing itself fails, and the deliberate `Promise.reject(err)` at :237 propagates to the outer `.catch` at :246, where `isRetryableError(err) && !err.status` evaluates false (err.status = 409 set at :236; 409 is neither a TypeError nor 5xx per :82-88), so it falls to `return Promise.reject(err)` at :266 with the message intact; (3) the caller must display it — segment-editor.js:1418 `alert(err.message || 'Einingin hefur verið breytt af öðrum notanda. Endurhleð...')`, so the Icelandic explanation is what the editor sees. The finding's specific complaint that the caller's own fallback "never gets a chance to display" is now moot rather than defective: `err.message` is non-empty but is itself the Icelandic explanation. I also checked the adjacent `/accept` path that shares `saveRetry.attempt` (key `acc:…`, segment-editor.js:1077, caller alert at :1096) in case the finding's structure recurred there — server/routes/segment-editor.js:545 likewise sends `{ error: err.code, message: err.message }` on its STALE_CONTENT/EDIT_EXISTS/DISCUSS_OPEN/HUMAN_CONTENT 409s, so no bare machine code surfaces there either. The localization-editor 409s (routes/localization-editor.js:330, :503) also include an Icelandic `message`, and its client handler at localization-editor.js:1064-1072 uses its own hardcoded Icelandic string regardless. No path remaining where the raw code is shown in place of the explanation.
- ✅ upheld — CANNOT REFUTE — the message-first fix is present AND I verified the full round trip, which is what makes it closed rather than merely plausible.

PRODUCER: server/routes/segment-editor.js:476 `return res.status(409).json({ error: 'conflict', message: err.message });` where err.message is the Icelandic string built in checkEditConflict at server/services/segmentEditorService.js:69-71 — `Annar yfirlesari (${conflict.editor_username}) hefur breytt þessum bút. Endurhlaðið til að sjá nýjustu útgáfu.` So the `message` field the review said was being dropped is genuinely populated.

CLIENT CONSTRUCTION: saveRetry.js:235 `const err = new Error(data.message || data.error || 'Villa ' + response.status);` — message-first, exactly the review's prescribed ordering, so the bare English word 'conflict' is no longer selected.

SURVIVAL TO THE CALLER (the half the original fix got wrong — the register calls it 'the first fix was dead code'): the crafted rejection is built inside the TWO-ARGUMENT `response.json().then(onFulfilled, onRejected)` at :233-244. A single-arg `.then(fn).catch(fn2)` would route the deliberate `Promise.reject(err)` into fn2 and overwrite the message with the generic 'Villa 409'; the two-arg second callback (:239-243) fires only when json() itself fails to parse. Then the outer `.catch` at :246-248 evaluates `isRetryableError(err) && !err.status` — err is not a TypeError and 409 is not 5xx, and `err.status = 409` was set at :236, so the guard is false twice over — falling to `return Promise.reject(err)` at :266, re-rejecting the SAME object with message identity intact (no re-queue either, correctly).

CONSUMER: server/public/js/segment-editor.js:1416-1419 `if (err.status === 409) { alert(err.message || 'Einingin hefur verið breytt...'); }` — err.message now holds the Icelandic explanation, so the user sees it; the same pattern at :1094 on the accept path. The review's stated end result ('the user sees the literal English word conflict with no context') can no longer occur.

Also confirmed the sibling 409s carry a message field, so no surviving instance of the same symptom on the paths that alert err.message: localization-editor.js:329-333 and :502-506 both include an Icelandic `message`, and the accept route at segment-editor.js:545 returns `{ error: err.code, message: err.message }` for STALE_CONTENT/EDIT_EXISTS/DISCUSS_OPEN.

Corroborating only (a STATIC presence pin, not behavior, and not my proof): server/__tests__/clientMessageContracts.test.js:19 'reads data.message before data.error'. NOTE (out of scope, notes-level, does not affect this verdict): some non-editor 409s remain English-only — pipeline.js:77/:127/:177 and segment-editor.js:1309/:1319 — but those are not the edit-conflict path the review cited.
- ✅ upheld — NOT REFUTED — I enumerated every site the claim covers and each one delivers the Icelandic explanation.

(1) Construction: saveRetry.js:235 is the ONLY error-construction site on the non-retryable response path — `const err = new Error(data.message || data.error || 'Villa ' + response.status); err.status = response.status;` — message-first, exactly the prescribed fix. Its sibling at :240 is the json-parse-failure fallback (`'Villa ' + status`), which has no message to read.
(2) Propagation: the two-argument `.then(onFulfilled, onRejected)` at :233-244 keeps the deliberate `Promise.reject(err)` out of a same-chain handler; the trailing `.catch` at :246-267 evaluates `isRetryableError(err) && !err.status` = false for a 409 (err.status=409, not 5xx) and falls through to `return Promise.reject(err);` at :266 — the crafted message is not rewritten anywhere between construction and caller.
(3) Producers: every responder that can emit the bare code carries a `message` — server/routes/segment-editor.js:476 `res.status(409).json({ error: 'conflict', message: err.message })`, :545 `{ error: err.code, message: err.message }`, and routes/localization-editor.js:329-333 / :502-506 `{ error: 'conflict', message: 'Einingin hefur verið breytt af öðrum notanda…' }`. A repo-wide grep for `error: 'conflict'` returns only those three sites; none is reached via the reversed-order utility `htmlUtils.js:45` (`data.error || data.message`) — no conflict-producing endpoint routes through `fetchJson`.
(4) Consumers: segment-editor.js:1417 `alert(err.message || 'Einingin hefur verið breytt…')` and acceptSegment's `alert(err.message || UI.acceptance.conflict)` display the server message; the localization editor's three 409 handlers (localization-editor.js:~230, :1065-1077, :1195-1211) show their own Icelandic confirm and never render `err.message`, so the bare English word "conflict" is not producible there either.

One scope gap I examined and it does not flip the verdict: `executeRetry`'s terminal branch (saveRetry.js:149-158 and :167-176) never calls `response.json()`, so a REPLAYED request that 409s toasts the generic 'Vista mistókst eftir N tilraunir…' and discards the Icelandic `message`. It constructs no Error object at all, so the finding's prescription ("check the explanation field first") has no site there, and it is a background replay rather than the interactive conflict-alert path the finding described. saveRetry.js is the only copy served (server/views/segment-editor.html:1614, localization-editor.html:2074 → /js/saveRetry.js).


## Group: `localization-review-svc`
### `code-3` — CLOSED
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-code-review.md § "Finding details" #3 (`server/services/localizationReviewService.js:212`), ranked-findings table row 3, and Batch B; cross-referenced as theme 5 in docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-review-joint-summary.md (no extra dimension added there)
**Original severity:** Medium (downgraded from High), CONFIRMED, lens: integrity
**Claim:** `approveAndApply` marks a localization pending edit `status='approved'` BEFORE writing the localized file, and is not wrapped in a transaction, so a throw from `loadModuleForLocalization`/save (e.g. a missing faithful file, as really happened on m68700) strands the row as "approved" but never applied — permanently un-actionable through the API, since both approve and reject only operate on `status='pending'` rows.
**Current location:** `server/services/localizationReviewService.js:237-293 (function `approveAndApply`, declared at :215)`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
The order is now explicitly write-first, and the status change is transactional:

:237-242 — `// 1. Apply to the localized file FIRST (snapshot-before-save handled by .bak).` / `// The file write is the one step a SQLite transaction cannot roll back, so it` / `// runs before any status change: if it throws, the edit stays 'pending' —` / `// visible in the queue, retryable, rejectable. If the write succeeds but the` / `// status update below fails, the edit also stays 'pending' and a retry` / `// rewrites identical bytes, so no state is stranded either way.`

:243-259 — `const data = segmentParser.loadModuleForLocalization(edit.book, edit.chapter, edit.module_id);` … `const { savedPath } = contentVersionService.saveLocalizedWithSnapshot(edit.book, edit.chapter, edit.module_id, segments, reviewerUsername);`

:265-274 — only AFTER the write: `conn.transaction(() => { conn.prepare(\`UPDATE localization_pending_edits SET status = 'approved', reviewer_id = ?, … applied_at = CURRENT_TIMESTAMP WHERE id = ?\`).run(…) …})();`

Behaviour is pinned by two named tests: server/__tests__/localizationReviewService.test.js:212 `it('a failed file write leaves the edit pending and retryable (F3 write-then-mark)')` and server/__tests__/locApproveSnapshot.test.js:100 `it('write-succeeds-DB-throws: file updated, row stays pending, retry succeeds')` (describe block at :89 is titled `approveAndApply snapshots + write-then-mark (I12-M3)`).
```
**Notes:** Fixing commit verified, not guessed: `git log -S "The file write is the one step a SQLite transaction cannot roll back" -- server/services/localizationReviewService.js` → 109f3f0e `fix(item12): localization approve writes file before marking approved (F3)`, first reachable via merge 615a7462 = PR #298 (`fix/item12-apply-job-version-integrity`). The evidence answers the review's NARROWED second-pass claim ("the original stranded database row itself stays permanently un-actionable through the API"), not the superseded High-severity framing: no row can now reach approved-without-write at all, because status is written only after `saveLocalizedWithSnapshot` returns, and the two status UPDATEs are inside one `conn.transaction`. No bypass path exists — `grep -rn "localization_pending_edits" server/routes/ server/services/` returns hits ONLY in localizationReviewService.js, and routes/localization-editor.js reaches it exclusively via `approveAndApply` (:149) / `rejectEdit` (:183) / `submitEdit` (:383, :588). Observation only, NOT a ledger row and not a data-op recommendation: the fix prevents NEW strandings; migration 041 rebuilds the table with `INSERT … SELECT … status …`, so any pre-item-12 `approved`-with-`applied_at IS NULL` row would have survived the rebuild un-actionable — almost certainly zero rows, since `enforce_localization_review` defaults OFF (`isReviewEnabled` :52-57 returns `!!(row && row.enforce_localization_review)`).
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — Independently confirmed CLOSED. In server/services/localizationReviewService.js, approveAndApply (declared :215) performs the file write BEFORE any status mutation: segmentParser.loadModuleForLocalization(edit.book, edit.chapter, edit.module_id) at :243 and contentVersionService.saveLocalizedWithSnapshot(...) at :253-259 — which reaches the actual disk write via segmentParser.saveLocalizedSegments at contentVersionService.js:397. The ONLY `SET status = 'approved'` statement in the whole file is at :268-274, inside conn.transaction() opened at :265, i.e. strictly after the write. I grepped the entire tree for other writers of this table (grep -rn 'localization_pending_edits|approveAndApply' over server/ tools/ scripts/): the only non-test, non-migration callers are routes/localization-editor.js:149 (approve route), which merely calls approveAndApply and does no status write of its own. So the review's exact scenario — a missing faithful file on m68700 making loadModuleForLocalization throw — now throws at :243 with the row untouched at 'pending', and both approveAndApply (:219 `if (edit.status !== 'pending') throw`) and rejectEdit (:308, same guard) still accept it. The 'stranded approved-but-never-applied, un-actionable through the API' outcome is unreachable. The non-transactional aspect is also moot: if the write succeeds and the transaction at :265 fails, the row stays 'pending' and a retry rewrites identical bytes. Attribution: commit 109f3f0e 'fix(item12): localization approve writes file before marking approved (F3)'. server/__tests__/localizationReviewService.test.js: 12 passed.
- ✅ upheld — Scope attack finds no surviving site. Enumerated every producer of an 'approved' status and every localized-file writer. (a) Single approve path: routes/localization-editor.js:149 `localizationReview.approveAndApply(...)` is the ONLY caller (grep of `approveAndApply` across server/ excluding tests returns just the declaration at localizationReviewService.js:215 and that one route); there is no bulk/apply-all localization approve. (b) Single status writer: the only `SET status = 'approved'` in the tree is localizationReviewService.js:268-274, and it is now positioned AFTER the file write and inside `conn.transaction(() => {...})()` at :265, setting `applied_at = CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` in the SAME UPDATE — so 'approved with applied_at NULL' has no producer at all. (c) The exact m68700 trigger step in the original scenario now precedes any mutation: `segmentParser.loadModuleForLocalization(edit.book, edit.chapter, edit.module_id)` at :243 and `contentVersionService.saveLocalizedWithSnapshot(...)` at :253 run first; everything before them (the `SELECT * ... WHERE id = ?` at :217, the status!=='pending' guard, the sibling-pending SELECT at :226) is read-only, so a missing faithful file throws with zero rows mutated and the edit stays 'pending' — i.e. still approvable AND rejectable, killing the un-actionable-row failure. (d) The remaining partial-failure path is covered too: contentVersionService.saveLocalizedWithSnapshot (:377-399) swallows a failed snapshot (`log.error(... 'save proceeds')`) and only then calls saveLocalizedSegments, and if the post-write transaction throws the row stays 'pending' and a retry rewrites identical bytes. Assertions (not just test names) verified: localizationReviewService.test.js:212 rmSync's the faithful file and asserts `edit.status === 'pending'`; locApproveSnapshot.test.js:100 installs a BEFORE UPDATE RAISE(ABORT) trigger and asserts file updated + row still 'pending' + retry reaches 'approved'. Two caveats that do not resurrect the claim: item 13 added a third terminal status 'superseded' (:278-290) which approve/reject also refuse, but it is a deliberate resolution carrying a reviewer_note with content preserved, not a silent strand; and pre-existing stranded rows are healed only by the deploy-time rider I12-R3 (`SELECT COUNT(*) ... status='approved' AND applied_at IS NULL`) in docs/plans/2026-07-21-post-item17-followup-campaign.md P0-1 — a data op, not the code defect. Fixing commit 109f3f0e 'fix(item12): localization approve writes file before marking approved (F3)'.
- ✅ upheld — MECHANISM TRACE HOLDS — the described failure cannot complete. Original scenario: `approveAndApply` set status='approved' first, then `loadModuleForLocalization` threw on a missing faithful file, stranding an 'approved'-but-never-applied row that neither approve nor reject (both `if (edit.status !== 'pending') throw`) could touch. Today server/services/localizationReviewService.js:243 calls `segmentParser.loadModuleForLocalization(edit.book, edit.chapter, edit.module_id)` and :253-259 `contentVersionService.saveLocalizedWithSnapshot(...)` BEFORE any status write; the status change is at :265-293 inside `conn.transaction(() => {...})()`, and the single UPDATE at :268-274 sets `status = 'approved'` and `applied_at = CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` together — so 'approved' now IMPLIES applied_at is set; the stranded (approved, applied_at NULL) state is unreachable. Traced the throw path: `saveLocalizedWithSnapshot` (contentVersionService.js:377-399) swallows only the pre-write SNAPSHOT failure in its try/catch at :386-395; the actual write `segmentParser.saveLocalizedSegments(...)` at :397 is OUTSIDE that catch, so a write failure propagates uncaught to the caller and the status update at :265 is never reached. Route server/routes/localization-editor.js:149-171 just maps the throw to a 4xx and does no compensating status write. Confirmed there is no second writer of the table: `grep -rn localization_pending_edits server/ scripts/ tools/` (excluding tests and migrations) returns hits ONLY inside localizationReviewService.js — no route, script, or batch service sets status='approved' anywhere else. Behaviour is pinned by server/__tests__/localizationReviewService.test.js:212 ('a failed file write leaves the edit pending and retryable (F3 write-then-mark)', which physically rmSync's the faithful file and asserts status 'pending' + applied_at null + retry succeeds) and locApproveSnapshot.test.js:100 (write-succeeds/DB-throws → row stays pending). One honest caveat that does NOT flip the verdict: no data migration un-strands pre-existing (approved, applied_at NULL) rows — migration 041 copies `status` verbatim — but none can exist, because `isReviewEnabled` (:52-57) returns false absent a `book_settings` row, so the tier was never on to produce them. CLOSED.

### `code-7` — CLOSED
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-code-review.md § "Finding details" #7 (`server/services/localizationReviewService.js:92`), ranked-findings table row 7, and Batch D; cross-referenced as theme 6 in docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-review-joint-summary.md (no extra dimension added there)
**Original severity:** Medium, CONFIRMED, lens: state
**Claim:** `submitEdit`'s "is there already a pending edit" lookup selects on `book/module_id/segment_id` with `status='pending'` and NO `editor_id` predicate, then UPDATEs that row in place — so when the review tier is enabled, a second editor's submission silently overwrites a first editor's still-pending edit (content, author and timestamp), with no conflict warning and no record of what was lost; the file-mtime conflict check cannot catch it because review-tier submissions only touch the DB.
**Current location:** `server/services/localizationReviewService.js:97-103 (function `submitEdit`, declared at :85); backing index at server/migrations/041-localization-pending-per-editor.js:76-78`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
The lookup is now scoped by editor:

:97-103 — `const existing = conn.prepare(\`SELECT id FROM localization_pending_edits WHERE book = ? AND module_id = ? AND segment_id = ? AND editor_id = ? AND status = 'pending'\`).get(book, moduleId, segmentId, String(editorId));` — so `existing` can only ever be the SUBMITTING editor's own row; a different editor falls through to the INSERT at :124-141 and gets their own row.

Documented at :78-83 — `Keeps one open (pending) edit per segment PER EDITOR (post-041): a re-submit by the same editor updates their pending row in place; a different editor's submit creates their own row — it can no longer overwrite someone else's pending work (finding 7). The partial unique index idx_loc_pending_one_per_editor backs the invariant.`

Enforced at the schema layer, migrations/041-localization-pending-per-editor.js:76-78 — `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_loc_pending_one_per_editor ON localization_pending_edits(book, module_id, segment_id, editor_id) WHERE status = 'pending';`

Pinned by server/__tests__/localizationReviewService.test.js:322 `it('cross-editor submit preserves both pending rows (finding 7)')` and :352 `it('same-editor re-submit still updates in place after per-editor scoping')`.
```
**Notes:** Fixing commit verified, not guessed: `git log -S "AND editor_id = ? AND status = 'pending'" -- server/services/localizationReviewService.js` → a2230ac5 `fix(item13): scope loc pending-edit upsert by editor (finding 7)`, first reachable via merge 278443dd = PR #299 (`fix/item13-concurrent-edit-lost-updates`). The delivered fix is STRONGER than any of the three options the review offered (scope by editor ID / return a conflict / at minimum log the overwritten content): the invariant is enforced at two layers (query predicate + partial unique index), and no editor's content is destroyed at all — `approveAndApply` :221-235 additionally refuses with `err.code = 'PENDING_EXISTS'` when a NEWER sibling pending exists (`if (siblingPendings.some((s) => isNewer(s, edit)))`), and :276-292 marks strictly-older siblings `status = 'superseded'` with the note `'Leyst úr gildi af nýrri samþykktri breytingu'` inside the same transaction, so the losing editor's work becomes auditable history rather than a silent clobber or a stuck queue item. Pinned by :378 `it('approve refuses (PENDING_EXISTS) when a newer pending exists on the segment')` and :420 `it('approving the newest pending supersedes older pendings on the segment')`. Same bypass check as code-3 applies: all writes to `localization_pending_edits` funnel through this service.
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — Independently confirmed CLOSED. submitEdit (declared server/services/localizationReviewService.js:85) now scopes the pending lookup by editor: :97-103 `SELECT id FROM localization_pending_edits WHERE book = ? AND module_id = ? AND segment_id = ? AND editor_id = ? AND status = 'pending'` bound with .get(book, moduleId, segmentId, String(editorId)). Therefore `existing` at :105 can only ever be the SUBMITTING editor's own row, and the in-place UPDATE at :106-120 can no longer touch another editor's content/author/timestamp; a different editor falls through to the INSERT at :124-141 and gets a distinct row. I checked for a schema-level blocker that would turn the second editor's INSERT into a constraint error: migration 034-localization-review.js creates only NON-unique indexes on this table (idx_loc_pending_module/_status/_segment, :43-47), and 041-localization-pending-per-editor.js:76-78 adds `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX ... idx_loc_pending_one_per_editor ON localization_pending_edits(book, module_id, segment_id, editor_id) WHERE status = 'pending'` — per-editor, so cross-editor INSERTs are legal. I also traced every submitEdit call site rather than trusting the verifier: routes/localization-editor.js:383 (single save) passes editorId: req.user.id (:391) and :588 (batch save) passes e.editorId built from String(req.user.id) at :551 — both authenticated, so there is no String(undefined) identity collapse that could re-merge two editors onto one row. Attribution: commit a2230ac5 'fix(item13): scope loc pending-edit upsert by editor (finding 7)'. Tests pass (12/12).
- ✅ upheld — Scope attack finds no surviving site. (a) Single upsert: `submitEdit` (localizationReviewService.js:85) is the only writer of a pending row, and its lookup at :97-103 now reads `WHERE book = ? AND module_id = ? AND segment_id = ? AND editor_id = ? AND status = 'pending'` bound with `String(editorId)` — the same normalization the INSERT at :126-141 uses — so a second editor can no longer reach the in-place UPDATE branch at :108-121 at all; they fall through to INSERT and get their own row. (b) Both call sites checked, not just the common one: routes/localization-editor.js:383 (single-segment save, passes `editorId: req.user.id`) and :588 (the BATCH save loop, passes `editorId: e.editorId` = `String(req.user.id)` built at ~:544) — both funnel through the fixed function and both pass the acting user, so the batch path is not a sibling that kept the defect. (c) Writer set is closed repo-wide: an UNFILTERED `grep -rni localization_pending_edits` (excluding node_modules/.git) returns only localizationReviewService.js, migrations 034/041, server/__tests__/*, and docs — no scripts/ backfill, no tools/, no e2e seed writes this table with the old un-scoped predicate. (d) Schema backing exists and is actually wired: migrations/041-localization-pending-per-editor.js:76-78 creates `UNIQUE INDEX idx_loc_pending_one_per_editor ON localization_pending_edits(book, module_id, segment_id, editor_id) WHERE status = 'pending'`, and 041 is registered in the runner's explicit list (server/services/migrationRunner.js:72 `require('../migrations/041-localization-pending-per-editor')`), so prod boot applies it. (e) The only other UPDATE that matches on segment identity without editor_id is approveAndApply's supersede at :278-290, which sets status='superseded' with a reviewer_note and PRESERVES edited_content — it resolves the loser as history rather than silently destroying it, which is the opposite of the finding's failure. Assertions verified in the source, not the titles: localizationReviewService.test.js:322 asserts `second.updated === false` and two pending rows keyed by editor_username with both contents intact; :352 asserts a same-editor re-submit still returns the same id with one row. Fixing commits a2230ac5 'fix(item13): scope loc pending-edit upsert by editor (finding 7)' and bd4a7c13 (migration 041).
- ✅ upheld — MECHANISM TRACE HOLDS — the cross-editor overwrite is blocked at the exact step the review described. Original scenario: `submitEdit`'s lookup matched any pending row on (book, module_id, segment_id) with no editor predicate, then UPDATEd it in place, replacing editor B's row content/author/timestamp over editor A's. Today server/services/localizationReviewService.js:97-103 reads `SELECT id FROM localization_pending_edits WHERE book = ? AND module_id = ? AND segment_id = ? AND editor_id = ? AND status = 'pending'` bound with `String(editorId)`, so `existing` can only ever be the SUBMITTING editor's own row; the in-place UPDATE at :106-120 uses `WHERE id = existing.id`, i.e. the submitter's own row, and a different editor falls through to the INSERT at :124-141 and gets a distinct row. Verified editorId is a real per-user value, not a constant that would collapse the scoping: both call sites pass it (routes/localization-editor.js:391 `editorId: req.user.id` and :596 `editorId: e.editorId` sourced from :551 `String(req.user.id)`), and middleware/requireAuth.js:52-53 sets `req.user = { id: decoded.sub, ... }` from the JWT subject. Verified there is no alternate door: `grep -rn localization_pending_edits server/ scripts/ tools/` (excluding tests/migrations) shows every UPDATE of `edited_content` lives inside localizationReviewService.js — no raw-SQL route or script updates the table scoped only by book/module/segment. Verified the loser's work is preserved rather than destroyed downstream: approveAndApply:224-235 refuses with PENDING_EXISTS (409) if a strictly newer sibling pending exists, and :276-292 marks strictly-older siblings `status='superseded'` inside the same transaction — history, not silent loss. Defence-in-depth confirmed present on fresh DBs (checked the migration-041 early-return the verifier glossed over): 034-localization-review.js:29-31 CHECKs only ('pending','approved','rejected') and creates no per-editor index, so `if (tableInfo.sql.includes("'superseded'")) return;` never short-circuits on a new database and 041:76-78 `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX ... idx_loc_pending_one_per_editor ON (book, module_id, segment_id, editor_id) WHERE status='pending'` does get created; 041 is wired into services/migrationRunner.js:73. Pinned by localizationReviewService.test.js:322 (cross-editor submit → `second.updated === false`, two pending rows with both editors' distinct content) and :352 (same-editor re-submit still updates in place). CLOSED.
