**Notes:** CALIBRATION item — confirmed CLOSED, matches the hand-verified expectation. Attribution: commit 366206b5 "fix(authz): book-scope pipeline inject/render/run (review finding 1)", merged via PR #268 (merge 41105021, branch fix/authz-book-scope-sweep).

IMPORTANT nuance for future readers: the blanket router-level gate the review quoted is STILL present verbatim at server/routes/pipeline.js:30 (`router.use(requireAuth, requireRole(ROLES.HEAD_EDITOR));`). The fix ADDED per-route `requireHeadEditorFor` on the three mutating routes rather than replacing the global gate — so do not read line 30 as evidence the finding is open. The consequence is that the two READ routes, `GET /jobs` (pipeline.js:218) and `GET /jobs/:jobId` (pipeline.js:236), inherit only the global role check and remain un-book-scoped. That read-path sibling is out of scope for this claim (whose failure scenario is mutation, and mutation is now scoped) and is separately tracked as C5 · B1-F5 / I12-R1 in docs/plans/2026-07-21-post-item17-followup-campaign.md:86 and efni issue #328 — including the amended fix shape (DELETE `GET /jobs`, which has zero callers; scope `/jobs/:jobId` on `job.book` with a 404).
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — Scope-enumerated and confirmed CLOSED. The router exposes exactly three mutating endpoints and all three carry the book-scoped guard resolved from the body: server/routes/pipeline.js:67-70 `router.post('/inject', requireHeadEditorFor((req) => req.body?.book), ...)`, :118-121 for '/render', :168-171 for '/run'. The guard genuinely compares against assignments and fail-closes at server/middleware/requireRole.js:131-144 — `if (!book) return res.status(404)...` then `if (req.user.books && req.user.books.includes(book)) return next();` else 403 (a user with no `books` array falls through to 403, not through). Body is parsed before the router (server/index.js:216-217 express.json/urlencoded; mounted at :248), and the guard reads the same `req.body.book` the handler later validates against VALID_BOOKS, so there is no TOCTOU. Attacking the scope: the only sibling call sites into the same service are server/routes/segment-editor.js:1296-1299 `POST /:book/:chapter/:moduleId/apply-and-render` guarded by `requireHeadEditor()` (default bookParam 'book', requireRole.js:56) and server/routes/publication.js:135/190/246 guarded by `requireHeadEditor('bookSlug')` — both book-scoped, so the described failure (a chemistry head-editor regenerating biology's published HTML) has no surviving route. The residual global `router.use(requireAuth, requireRole(ROLES.HEAD_EDITOR))` at :30 is now only a floor beneath the per-route guard. Fixing commit 366206b5 'fix(authz): book-scope pipeline inject/render/run (review finding 1)'. (Out of scope of this finding, and tracked as issue #328: GET /jobs and GET /jobs/:jobId are still not book-scoped — read-only, not the mutate/publish scenario.)
- ✅ upheld — Attacked the mechanism end-to-end and it holds. (a) The guard is on all three mutating routes and resolves the book from the same place the handler reads it: server/routes/pipeline.js:69/:120/:170 `requireHeadEditorFor((req) => req.body?.book)`, while validateParams (pipeline.js:36) reads the same `req.body.book` — no window for the checked value to differ from the used value. (b) The guard is genuinely assignment-aware and fail-closed: server/middleware/requireRole.js:135-144 `if (req.user.books && req.user.books.includes(book)) return next(); ... return res.status(403)`; a falsy/missing book → 404 at :131-133, a throwing resolver → 404 at :126-129. (c) I chased the one bypass that would have kept the failure alive — `req.user.books` being populated with every book. It is not: server/middleware/requireAuth.js:58 `books: decoded.books || []`, minted from server/services/auth.js:213 `books: headEditorBooks` ← userService.js:522-526 `getHeadEditorBooks` = `user.bookAccess.filter(ba => ba.role === HEAD_EDITOR).map(ba => ba.book)`, i.e. per-book grants only (admins get `books: []` and pass via the explicit ADMIN bypass at requireRole.js:119-121, the intended behaviour). So the review's concrete scenario — chemistry-only head-editor POSTs `/api/pipeline/render` with `book: 'liffraedi-2e'` — now terminates at 403 before pipelineService is reached; `confirmed: true` no longer helps because authz runs ahead of the confirmation branch. Behaviour-pinned by server/__tests__/crossBookAuthz.test.js:264-277 (all three routes: other book → 403; owning head-editor → later 400, never 401/403) plus the /render discrimination case at :314. Attribution: commit 366206b5 'fix(authz): book-scope pipeline inject/render/run (review finding 1)'. (Out of claim scope: GET /api/pipeline/jobs at pipeline.js:218 is still unscoped — that is issue #328 / C5, not this finding.)
- ✅ upheld — I could not reach the defect. Independent trace of the DEFECT path: a head-editor of efnafraedi-2e POSTing {book:'liffraedi-2e'} to /api/pipeline/render. The blanket gate at server/routes/pipeline.js:30 (`router.use(requireAuth, requireRole(ROLES.HEAD_EDITOR))`) is still there, but it is no longer the last word — each of the three mutating routes now carries a per-request book guard AHEAD of the handler: pipeline.js:67-70 (`router.post('/inject', requireHeadEditorFor((req) => req.body?.book), ...)`), :118-120 (/render), :168-170 (/run). The guard itself compares against the caller's assignments at server/middleware/requireRole.js:135-144 — `if (req.user.books && req.user.books.includes(book)) return next();` else `res.status(403)` — i.e. it fails CLOSED, not open, when `books` is empty or lacks the slug. `req.user.books` is always an array (server/middleware/requireAuth.js:58 and :89, `books: decoded.books || []`), so the `includes` never short-circuits into a pass. Bypass attempts I checked and that fail: (a) `req.body` is parsed before the router (server/index.js:216 `app.use(express.json(...))` vs :248 `app.use('/api/pipeline', pipelineRoutes)`), so the resolver is never reading an undefined body — and an undefined book 404s at requireRole.js:131-133 anyway; (b) the handler acts on the SAME string the guard checked (`validateParams` reads `req.body.book` at pipeline.js:36 and `runInject/runRender/runPipeline` are called with `params.book`, pipeline.js:99/149/199), so there is no check-here-use-there split to exploit; (c) a non-string `book` (array/object) fails `includes` by SameValueZero → 403. The review's sub-clause 'even the confirmation step can be skipped by sending confirmed:true' is moot for the same ordering reason: the guard runs before the handler ever reads `req.body.confirmed` (pipeline.js:84). ADMIN bypass at requireRole.js:119-121 is the stated intended behaviour. Behaviourally pinned (not regex-pinned) by server/__tests__/crossBookAuthz.test.js:264-320, which asserts 403 for a head-editor of another book on all three routes, 400 (i.e. authz cleared, then chapter 9999 rejected) for the owning head-editor and for admin, and — the resolver-vs-hardcoded-constant discrimination — 400 for HE_A on their OWN book on both /inject and /render. Attribution: server/routes/pipeline.js commit 366206b5 'fix(authz): book-scope pipeline inject/render/run (review finding 1)'. Matches the task's own calibration (code-1 = CLOSED).

### `code-14` — CLOSED
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-code-review.md — Findings table row 14 + "Finding details" §14 (`server/routes/pipeline.js:83`)
**Original severity:** Medium, CONFIRMED (contract)
**Claim:** The pipeline's 409 "requiresConfirmation" handshake has no client half: no browser code checks the confirmation flag or resends with `confirmed: true`, and because the 409 body carries neither `error` nor `message`, the client's fallback shows the bare text "Villa: HTTP 409" and the warning is thrown away.
**Current location:** `server/routes/pipeline.js:87-95 (+ :137-145, :187-195); server/public/js/segment-editor.js:1960-1985; server/public/js/htmlUtils.js:34-55`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
Both halves the review said were missing now exist.

(a) Server — all three 409s now carry the generic fields the client's fallback reads. server/routes/pipeline.js:87-94:
        return res.status(409).json({
          requiresConfirmation: true,
          error: 'Staðfestingar krafist',
          message:
            'Extraction has not been run for this chapter. Inject may fail without extracted segments.',
          warning:
            'Extraction has not been run for this chapter. Inject may fail without extracted segments.',
        });
(identical shape at :137-144 for /render and :187-194 for /run).

(b) Client — `runPipelineAction` implements confirm-and-resend. server/public/js/segment-editor.js:1960-1976:
      if (err.data && err.data.requiresConfirmation) {
        if (window.confirm(err.data.warning || err.message)) {
          try {
            const data = await fetchJson(`/api/pipeline/${action}`, {
              method: 'POST',
              ...
              body: JSON.stringify({ book: currentBook, chapter: currentChapter, moduleId: ..., track, confirmed: true }),
            });
            pollJobStatus(data.jobId);
            return;
plus an explicit user-declined branch at :1980-1988 that resets to a neutral idle state (`UI.pipeline.cancelled`) instead of the red failure badge.

(c) The connecting link — `err.data` really carries the parsed 409 body, so the branch is live rather than dead. server/public/js/htmlUtils.js:34-53:
    async function fetchJson(url, options) { ... if (!res.ok) { let msg = 'Villa: HTTP ' + res.status; let data; try { data = await res.json(); msg = data.error || data.message || msg; } catch {} const err = new Error(msg); err.status = res.status; err.data = data; throw err; }
`segment-editor.js` defines no local `fetchJson` (grep for `function fetchJson|const fetchJson` in that file returns nothing), and `server/views/segment-editor.html:1613` loads `<script src="/js/htmlUtils.js"></script>` ahead of it, so it is this global.
```
**Notes:** Attribution: commit f7fe8ba0 "fix(ui): surface dropped server messages — conflict text + pipeline confirmation handshake", merged via PR #270 (merge b758dff6). The same commit also addressed the sibling finding 24 (saveRetry.js conflict text) — not my group.

I deliberately did not rest this disposition on the static pins in server/__tests__/clientMessageContracts.test.js:62-78 (which regex `requiresConfirmation`/`confirmed:\s*true` against file bytes); those pins exist and are useful as regression guards, but the behavioural evidence above is the `err.data` assignment in htmlUtils.js plus the script-tag load order, which is what makes the handshake branch reachable.
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — Both halves verified, and the client half is the ONLY client. Server: all three 409s now carry the generic fields the fallback reads — pipeline.js:87-94 `requiresConfirmation: true, error: 'Staðfestingar krafist', message: 'Extraction has not been run…', warning: …`, identical at :137-144 (render) and :187-194 (run); `if (!req.body.confirmed)` at :84/:134/:184 means the resend actually proceeds. Client: server/public/js/segment-editor.js:1960-1976 `if (err.data && err.data.requiresConfirmation) { if (window.confirm(err.data.warning || err.message)) { … confirmed: true … pollJobStatus(data.jobId); return; } }`, with a declined branch at :1980-1988 resetting to `UI.pipeline.cancelled` (defined, ui-strings.js:164). The link is live: `fetchJson` is defined once in the tree (server/public/js/htmlUtils.js:34) and sets `err.data = data` at :51 after parsing the JSON body; segment-editor.html:1613 loads htmlUtils.js before segment-editor.js at :1621 and segment-editor.js defines no local shadow. Scope attack fails: grepping server/public + server/views for 'api/pipeline' finds only segment-editor.js:1945/1963 issuing the POSTs (both wired to the real buttons at :1919-1929 btn-inject/btn-render/btn-pipeline); books.html:2222 and admin.html:1021 only poll /jobs/:jobId and never receive a requiresConfirmation body. The other producer of the flag, server/routes/admin.js:369, already has its own handler at server/views/admin.html:1072 (the 'other part of the admin interface' the review itself cited as correct). Pinned by server/__tests__/clientMessageContracts.test.js:63-79. Fixing commit f7fe8ba0.
- ✅ upheld — Both halves the review said were missing now exist, and I traced the failure step by step through today's code without it completing. Server: all three 409s now carry the generic fields the client's fallback reads — server/routes/pipeline.js:87-94 (`requiresConfirmation: true, error: 'Staðfestingar krafist', message: …, warning: …`), identical at :137-144 (/render) and :187-194 (/run). Client: server/public/js/segment-editor.js:1958-1988 — `if (err.data && err.data.requiresConfirmation) { if (window.confirm(err.data.warning || err.message)) { … body: JSON.stringify({ …, confirmed: true }) … pollJobStatus(data.jobId); return; } else { … output.textContent = UI.pipeline.cancelled; … return; } }`. The connecting link is live, not dead code: server/public/js/htmlUtils.js:34-53 `fetchJson` does `data = await res.json(); msg = data.error || data.message || msg; … err.status = res.status; err.data = data; throw err;`, it is the ONLY definition of fetchJson in the tree (`grep -rn 'function fetchJson|fetchJson =' public/js views` → htmlUtils.js:34 alone), and server/views/segment-editor.html:1613 loads it before segment-editor.js:1621. The handlers really fire: btn-inject/btn-render/btn-pipeline exist at segment-editor.html:1458/:1461/:1464 inside `#pipeline-panel` (:1449) and are bound at segment-editor.js:1919-1928. `UI.pipeline.cancelled` is defined (public/js/ui-strings.js:164 'Hætt við.'), so the declined branch shows real text, not 'undefined'. Server-side the retry actually proceeds: the prerequisite block is gated by `if (!req.body.confirmed)` (pipeline.js:84/:134/:184) and validateParams ignores the extra field. The old outcome — bare 'Villa: HTTP 409' with the warning discarded and no way to proceed — cannot be reproduced. Attribution: commit f7fe8ba0 'fix(ui): surface dropped server messages — conflict text + pipeline confirmation handshake'.
- ✅ upheld — Two independent kills; I could not construct the described failure. The claim has two halves and both are dead. (1) SERVER half — the 409 body no longer lacks the generic fields. server/routes/pipeline.js:87-94 now emits `{requiresConfirmation:true, error:'Staðfestingar krafist', message:'Extraction has not been run...', warning:'Extraction has not been run...'}`, identically shaped at :137-144 (/render, 'Injection has not been run...') and :187-194 (/run). Because server/public/js/htmlUtils.js:36-47 builds its message as `let msg = 'Villa: HTTP ' + res.status; ... msg = data.error || data.message || msg;`, a body carrying `error` can no longer fall through to the bare status string — for ANY client, which is what makes this half client-independent. (2) CLIENT half — the confirm-and-resend handshake now exists in the pipeline panel's own handler. server/public/js/segment-editor.js:1958-1979: `if (err.data && err.data.requiresConfirmation) { if (window.confirm(err.data.warning || err.message)) { ... body: JSON.stringify({..., confirmed: true}) ... pollJobStatus(data.jobId); return; }`, wired to #btn-inject/#btn-render/#btn-pipeline at :1919-1929, with a declined branch at :1981-1988 resetting to `UI.pipeline.cancelled` (which really exists — server/public/js/ui-strings.js, `pipeline: { ... cancelled: 'Hætt við.' }`). The connecting link is live, not dead code: htmlUtils.js:45 does `err.data = data;` before throwing, htmlUtils.js is loaded ahead of segment-editor.js (server/views/segment-editor.html:1613 vs :1621), and segment-editor.js defines no shadowing local `fetchJson` (its first use is a call at :156). The server's `if (!req.body.confirmed)` at pipeline.js:84/134/184 means the resent request actually proceeds, so the 'no way to proceed' symptom is gone too. I re-enumerated clients repo-wide rather than trusting the verifier's directory-scoped grep — `grep -rn 'pipeline/(inject|render|run)' --include=*.js --include=*.html` over the whole tree (minus node_modules/docs) returns only crossBookAuthz.test.js, pipeline.js's own header comment, and two display strings in server/routes/status.js:1637/1643; the only browser poster is segment-editor.js via the template literal `/api/pipeline/${action}`. Attribution: commit f7fe8ba0 'fix(ui): surface dropped server messages — conflict text + pipeline confirmation handshake'.

### `code-23` — CLOSED
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-code-review.md — Findings table row 23 + "Finding details" §23 (`server/routes/pipeline.js:43`)
**Original severity:** Low, CONFIRMED (contract)
**Claim:** The pipeline route's own inline chapter validation (`if (isNaN(chapterNum) || chapterNum < 1 || chapterNum > MAX_CHAPTERS)`) rejects -1, the internal code for the appendices chapter that the segment editor itself offers as selectable — so Inject/Render/Run on Viðaukar returns a flat "Invalid chapter number"; and even where -1 is accepted elsewhere it reaches the CLI tool as the literal text "-1", which that tool's formatting logic does not recognise.
**Current location:** `server/routes/pipeline.js:43-47 (validation); server/services/pipelineService.js:66-67, :201-202, :239-240 (CLI boundary)`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
(a) The inline validation now routes through the canonical normalizer and carries the -1 exception. server/routes/pipeline.js:43-47:
  const chapterNum = normalizeChapter(chapter);
  if (chapterNum === null || (chapterNum !== -1 && (chapterNum < 1 || chapterNum > MAX_CHAPTERS))) {
    res.status(400).json({ error: 'Invalid chapter number' });
    return null;
  }
imported at :27 — `const { normalizeChapter } = require('../lib/chapterLabel');` — and server/lib/chapterLabel.js:24-25 maps both dialects: `if (value === 'appendices') return -1;` / integers pass through.

(b) The "second, deeper issue" the review flagged for THIS path is also closed: the spawn boundary emits the word, not "-1". server/services/pipelineService.js:23 `const { cliChapterArg, chapterFromDir } = require('../lib/chapterLabel');` and at :66-67 / :201-202 / :239-240:
    '--chapter',
    cliChapterArg(chapter),
with server/lib/chapterLabel.js:48-50:
  function cliChapterArg(chapter) {
    return chapter === -1 ? 'appendices' : String(chapter);
  }
Behaviour-pinned (not just regex-pinned) by server/__tests__/pipelineSpawnArgs.test.js:61-82, which spies on the spawned argv: "runInject emits --chapter appendices for -1 and keeps job.chapter = -1" → `expect(chapterArgOf(spawnedCalls[0])).toBe('appendices')`, repeated for runRender and runExtract. The validation half is pinned by server/__tests__/pipelineValidateParams.test.js ("appendices acceptance matrix (item 14, finding 23 first half)") via the `router._validateParams` seam exposed at pipeline.js:247.
```
**Notes:** Attribution: commit 9e38df15 "fix(item14): pipeline route accepts appendices (-1) — panel inject/render/run no longer 400s", merged via PR #300 (merge 533316ec, branch fix/item14-appendices-label).

Scope precision on the "deeper issue": what is closed is the appendices dialect on the PIPELINE route's own downstream (route → pipelineService → CLI argv). The analogous -1 mishandling on the PUBLISH write-path is a different code path and is still open — `tools/validate-chapter.js` parseArgs drops `-1` as a flag and builds `ch-1`, and `publicationService.validateBeforePublish` uses raw `String(-1)` instead of `cliChapterArg`. That is tracked as campaign item C1d (docs/plans/2026-07-21-post-item17-followup-campaign.md; spec/plan at docs/superpowers/{specs,plans}/2026-07-22-c1d-appendix-writepath-publish*, and flagged in CLAUDE.md's C1a block). Closed here, open there, different path.

Out-of-scope observation, not a row (per SCOPE DISCIPLINE): the retained lower bound `chapterNum < 1` at pipeline.js:44 also rejects chapter 0, even though chapterLabel.js:10-12 explicitly documents that "chapter 0 (front-matter, ch00) is real". That was not part of finding 23 and I did not check whether ch00 is ever a legitimate pipeline target.
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — Both halves of the finding are closed, at every site the claim covers. (a) Validation: pipeline.js:43-44 `const chapterNum = normalizeChapter(chapter); if (chapterNum === null || (chapterNum !== -1 && (chapterNum < 1 || chapterNum > MAX_CHAPTERS)))` — the -1 exception is explicit, and normalizeChapter (server/lib/chapterLabel.js:24-30) maps both dialects ('appendices' → -1, '-1'/-1 → -1). This is a single shared function called by all three routes (:71, :122, :173), so there is no per-route gap. (b) The 'second, deeper issue' (literal '-1' on argv): every spawn site formats through cliChapterArg — pipelineService.js:66-67 (runExtract), :201-202 (runInject), :239-240 (runRender) — with chapterLabel.js:48-50 `return chapter === -1 ? 'appendices' : String(chapter);`. runPipeline (:272-320) does not spawn itself; it awaits runInject then runRender, so the /run route inherits the fix. Downstream the word is accepted: tools/lib/parseArgs.js:65 `parse: (val) => (val === 'appendices' ? 'appendices' : parseInt(val, 10))` (CHAPTER_OPTION is used by both cnxml-inject.js:109 and cnxml-render.js:471), and both tools special-case it (cnxml-inject.js:84-88 `if (chapter === 'appendices') return 'appendices';`, cnxml-render.js:273). The pre-spawn confirmation lookup also survives -1: pipelineService.js:704 getStageStatus normalizes rather than padStarts. Behaviour-pinned by server/__tests__/pipelineSpawnArgs.test.js:60-88 (spies on argv, asserts 'appendices' for runInject/runRender/runExtract and '3' for a normal chapter) and server/__tests__/pipelineValidateParams.test.js:60/71. Fixing commit 9e38df15. Residual padStart sites (computeSourceHash:603, runProtect:111, runUnprotect:153, runGenerateTm:867) are off the inject/render/run path — computeSourceHash only runs in runExtract's post-hook and returns null for a nonexistent ch-1 dir; protect/unprotect have no non-test callers — so none of them revives the described failure.
- ✅ upheld — I traced BOTH halves of the finding — the flat 400 and the 'deeper issue' the review warned would remain — and neither survives. (a) Validation: server/routes/pipeline.js:43-44 `const chapterNum = normalizeChapter(chapter); if (chapterNum === null || (chapterNum !== -1 && (chapterNum < 1 || chapterNum > MAX_CHAPTERS)))` — the `chapterNum !== -1` clause is exactly the missing exception; normalizeChapter (server/lib/chapterLabel.js:24-31) accepts every dialect the client can send ('appendices' → -1; '-1' → -1; -1 → -1). The client sends the numeric dialect (`currentChapter = parseInt(chapterSelect.value, 10)` at public/js/segment-editor.js:206, with the deep-link alias `if (chapter === 'appendices') chapter = '-1'` at :2833), so the Viðaukar selection maps to -1 and clears the guard. Bounds still bite for real junk (-2 → 400). (b) The deeper issue — the CLI receiving the literal '-1' — is closed at the spawn boundary, not merely at the route: server/services/pipelineService.js:66-67 (runExtract), :201-202 (runInject), :239-240 (runRender) all emit `'--chapter', cliChapterArg(chapter)` with chapterLabel.js:49-51 `return chapter === -1 ? 'appendices' : String(chapter);` (runPipeline delegates to runInject/runRender at :304/:319, so it inherits this). I then verified the receiving end actually recognises that word rather than assuming it: tools/lib/parseArgs.js:60-66 CHAPTER_OPTION `parse: (val) => (val === 'appendices' ? 'appendices' : parseInt(val, 10))`, imported by tools/cnxml-inject.js:44/:107-109 and tools/cnxml-render.js:41/:469-471, and consumed by tools/cnxml-inject.js:84-88 `formatChapter` (`if (chapter === 'appendices') return 'appendices'`) and tools/cnxml-render.js:273/:285 — so the strict `=== 'appendices'` comparisons match. Intermediate steps also survive -1: hasRunningJob does strict equality on the number (pipelineService.js:469-473) and getStageStatus (:702-705) passes -1 through, worst case falling back to `{}` → the (now-working) confirmation handshake. Pinned by server/__tests__/pipelineSpawnArgs.test.js (spies on spawned argv) and pipelineValidateParams.test.js via the `router._validateParams` seam (pipeline.js:247). Attribution: commit 9e38df15 'fix(item14): pipeline route accepts appendices (-1) — panel inject/render/run no longer 400s'. (The still-open appendix write-path work in memory, C1d, is publicationService/tools/validate-chapter.js — a different route family, not this finding's inject/render/run path.)
- ✅ upheld — Both halves of the claim are dead; I walked the full Viðaukar → CLI chain looking for a surviving break. (a) VALIDATION half: the inline `isNaN(chapterNum) || chapterNum < 1` test no longer exists. server/routes/pipeline.js:43-47 is now `const chapterNum = normalizeChapter(chapter); if (chapterNum === null || (chapterNum !== -1 && (chapterNum < 1 || chapterNum > MAX_CHAPTERS))) { res.status(400).json({error:'Invalid chapter number'}); return null; }` — an explicit -1 exception — with `normalizeChapter` imported at :27 from server/lib/chapterLabel.js, whose :25 `if (value === 'appendices') return -1;` plus the `/^-?\d+$/` branch at :27-29 accepts all three dialects the editor can send. That matters because the editor really does send `'-1'` as a string: server/public/js/segment-editor.js:2833 `if (chapter === 'appendices') chapter = '-1';`. Nothing downstream in the route re-rejects -1: `getStageStatus` (server/services/pipelineService.js:702-719) special-cases `chapter === 'appendices' ? -1 : Number(chapter)` and is wrapped in a try/catch returning `{}`, so the worst case is the (now working) confirmation handshake, never a 400 or 500. (b) CLI half — the string-identity chain the original claim said was broken now joins end to end: pipelineService.js:66-67, :201-202, :239-240 all push `'--chapter', cliChapterArg(chapter)`; chapterLabel.js:49-51 `return chapter === -1 ? 'appendices' : String(chapter);`; tools/lib/parseArgs.js:60-66 `CHAPTER_OPTION.parse: (val) => (val === 'appendices' ? 'appendices' : parseInt(val, 10))` passes the word through UNCHANGED; and both consuming tools compare against exactly that word — tools/cnxml-inject.js:84-88 `formatChapter` (`if (chapter === 'appendices')`) and tools/cnxml-render.js:272-276 `formatChapterDir` / :284-288 `formatChapterOutput` — with both tools using CHAPTER_OPTION (cnxml-inject.js:44/109, cnxml-render.js:41/471). Pinned behaviourally, not by regex: server/__tests__/pipelineSpawnArgs.test.js:60-88 spies on the spawned argv (`expect(chapterArgOf(spawnedCalls[0])).toBe('appendices')` for runInject/runRender/runExtract, and `'3'` for a normal chapter), and server/__tests__/pipelineValidateParams.test.js:55-71 asserts the acceptance matrix (`'appendices'`, `'-1'`, `-1` all → -1; `'0'`, `'100'`, `'chappendices'`, `''` → 400) through the `router._validateParams` seam at pipeline.js:247. Attribution: commit 9e38df15 'fix(item14): pipeline route accepts appendices (-1) — panel inject/render/run no longer 400s'. Caveat that does NOT flip the verdict, noted for completeness: server/services/pipelineService.js:866 and :902 (`runGenerateTm`) still do `String(chapter).padStart(2,'0')` / `ch${chapterStr}` / `'--chapter', String(chapter)` — same family of appendix residue — but that function has no HTTP caller anywhere in server/routes (the live TM route, server/routes/tm.js:53, does its own `chapter === -1 ? 'appendices' : chapter` conversion), so it is not on the Inject/Render/Run path this claim names.


## Group: `terminology-glob`
### `code-9` — OPEN-TRACKED
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-code-review.md — findings table row "| 9 | 14 | server/services/terminologyService.js:1438 | Medium | CONFIRMED | deadcode |" (:79), detail section (:259-264), prioritization note (:740); reaffirmed in docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-review-joint-summary.md:34 ("Code Batch H (findings 9, 32, 33, 34, 35, 36)")
**Original severity:** Medium (review severity column); joint summary ranks the batch as M but flags this specific item as first-to-fix for real breakage risk
**Claim:** The terminology key-terms import feature does `require('glob')`, but `glob` is not a declared dependency of server/package.json (or its lockfile) anywhere — it resolves only by accident today via an unrelated package's transitive dependency, so a clean install can break it outright and any head-editor importing key terms with a chapter number specified gets a server error.
**Current location:** `server/services/terminologyService.js:1876 (require); server/package.json:14-19 (overrides — the ONLY glob entry), :20-37 (dependencies — glob absent), :49-51 (devDependencies — glob absent)`
**Register item:** item 22 · Batch 9 — docs/plans/2026-07-21-post-item17-followup-campaign.md:111, under "## P3 — Opportunistic / polish · [CODE], no deadline": "**item 22 · Batch 9** — undeclared glob · dead-code · SR-OOS-1 · absorbs B4-F6." Predecessor register corroborates: docs/plans/2026-07-11-pre-semester-coding-campaign.md:240 "22. Batch 9 (undeclared `glob` first, then dead-code deletion; SR-OOS-1 archiver skew)."
**Evidence:**

```
Re-located by symbol, not line (review said :1438; now :1876).

server/services/terminologyService.js:1875-1882 —
`​``
function findFiles(pattern) {
  const glob = require('glob');
  try {
    return glob.sync(pattern);
  } catch {
    return [];
  }
}
`​``
Note the `require` on :1876 is OUTSIDE the try that opens on :1877 → a missing `glob` throws an UNCAUGHT MODULE_NOT_FOUND, exactly the 500 the review describes.

Only the chapter-number branch reaches it — server/services/terminologyService.js:1218-1227 —
`​``
  if (chapterNum) {
    const chDir = `ch${String(chapterNum).padStart(2, '0')}`;
    const pattern = path.join(pubDir, 'faithful', 'chapters', chDir, '*-key-terms.md');
    keyTermsFiles = findFiles(pattern);
    if (keyTermsFiles.length === 0) {
      const mtPattern = path.join(pubDir, 'mt-preview', 'chapters', chDir, '*-key-terms.md');
      keyTermsFiles = findFiles(mtPattern);
    }
  } else {
    keyTermsFiles = findFilesRecursive(pubDir, '-key-terms.md');
  }
`​``
matching the review's "with a chapter number specified" qualifier; `findFilesRecursive` (:1884) is the review's suggested in-file replacement helper and is already used on the else branch.

Declaration state — server/package.json:14-19 —
`​``
  "overrides": {
    "glob": ">=11.0.0",
    "minimatch": ">=10.2.3",
    "brace-expansion": ">=5.0.8",
    "body-parser": ">=2.3.0"
  },
`​``
…and glob appears in NEITHER `"dependencies"` (:20-37: archiver, better-sqlite3, cookie-parser, cors, csv-parse, dotenv, express, express-rate-limit, helmet, jsonwebtoken, multer, nodemailer, pino, pino-pretty, uuid, xlsx) NOR `"devDependencies"` (:49-51: only @playwright/test). An `overrides` entry constrains a transitive version; it does not install anything.

Resolution today (verified, not inferred): `ls -d server/node_modules/glob` → "No such file or directory"; `grep '"node_modules/glob"' server/package-lock.json` → no match (only `readdir-glob`). `require.resolve('glob', {paths:['server/services']})` → `/home/siggi/dev/repos/namsbokasafn-efni/node_modules/glob/dist/commonjs/index.min.js` — i.e. it walks up to the REPO-ROOT tree. Root package-lock.json:1274-1290 has `"node_modules/glob": {"version": "13.0.6"}` with no `"dev": true`, arriving via `@mathjax/src` → `mj-context-menu` → `rimraf` (root package.json dependencies are only @mathjax/src, @xmldom/xmldom, mammoth).
```
**Notes:** Matches the calibration item exactly (OPEN); tracked, so OPEN-TRACKED rather than OPEN-UNTRACKED. CLAUDE.md and MEMORY.md contain no glob entry (their only "glob" hits are the unrelated word "global"/"globals"); the two plan registers carry it.

No refutation exists: neither the joint summary nor the editorial-workflow review walks it back — the joint summary (:34) reaffirms it as the first item of Code Batch H, and the code review itself (:740) calls it "the one item here with real breakage risk".

ATTRIBUTION: not fixed; no closing commit. `git log -S"require('glob')" -- server/services/terminologyService.js` yields only `b0307ab5` (the commit that introduced it). ⚠️ DO NOT read `e4ac93bc` "chore: override deprecated glob in server dependencies" as a fix — that commit ADDED the `overrides` entry, which is precisely why `grep glob server/package.json` now returns a hit while the package remains undeclared and uninstalled in the server tree. A future reader could close this finding by mistake on that grep.

SHARPENED vs the original claim: the review guessed the accidental resolution came from a transitive dep inside server/node_modules. Current state is worse and differently shaped — glob is absent from server/node_modules AND from server/package-lock.json entirely; it resolves only by Node walking up to the repo-root tree, where it exists as a MathJax transitive (@mathjax/src → mj-context-menu → rimraf → glob@13.0.6). It is not dev-flagged there and `.github/workflows/deploy.yml:53` runs `npm ci --omit=dev` at the repo root before `cd server && npm ci --omit=dev` (:54), so prod does have it — today, by accident, through a MathJax chain that has nothing to do with terminology import.

OUT-OF-SCOPE OBSERVATION (note only, not a ledger row): because the surviving resolution path is `@mathjax/src` → `mj-context-menu` → `rimraf` → `glob`, a routine MathJax version bump that drops rimraf would silently break a server feature with no signal in server/package.json — a fragility the review did not anticipate and one the `overrides` entry actively camouflages.


## Group: `admin-fabricated-zeros`
### `code-22` — CLOSED
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-code-review.md § "22. server/routes/admin.js:397 — The admin book list hides real failures behind fabricated zeros" (summary table row 22; Batch E — Fail-loud sweep). Also cited in docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-review-joint-summary.md §(b).
**Original severity:** Low, CONFIRMED (lens: failloud). Second-pass verification: "Confirmed directly by reading the surrounding code and the sibling catch block that does this correctly. Rating unchanged."
**Claim:** In GET /api/admin/books, the per-book editorial-progress computation was wrapped in a bare `catch { book.editorialProgress = { percent: 0, approvedSegments: 0, totalSegments: 0 }; }` with no log line, so a genuine DB/filesystem failure produced output byte-for-byte identical to a legitimately untouched book; the fix asked for logging the real error AND representing the value as explicitly unavailable rather than a fabricated zero.
**Current location:** `server/routes/admin.js:409-413 (the catch block); consumer at server/views/books.html:1663. The review's `:397` is stale line-drift — re-located by symbol (`getEditorialProgress` / `book.editorialProgress`) inside the `router.get('/books', requireAuth, requireRole(ROLES.EDITOR), ...)` handler, which now begins at server/routes/admin.js:396.`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
Server half — server/routes/admin.js:402-413:
`​``js
        const progress = segmentEditorService.getEditorialProgress(book.slug);
        book.editorialProgress = {
          percent: progress.summary.percentComplete,
          approvedSegments: progress.summary.approvedSegments,
          totalSegments: progress.summary.totalSegments,
        };
      } catch (err) {
        log.error({ err, book: book.slug }, 'Editorial progress failed for book list');
        book.editorialProgress = null;
        book.editorialProgressUnavailable = true;
      }
`​``
Consumer half (the "visually distinguishable" clause) — server/views/books.html:1661-1664:
`​``js
                var ep = book.editorialProgress;
                var pct = ep && typeof ep.percent === 'number' ? ep.percent : 0;
                var pctLabel = book.editorialProgressUnavailable
                  ? 'Framvinda ótiltæk'
                  : pct + '% lokið';
`​``
Durability guard — server/__tests__/adminBooksHonesty.test.js:98-104 drops the `segment_edits` table to force the throw and asserts `editorialProgress === null`, `editorialProgressUnavailable === true`, and that `logger.error` was called with `{ book: 'prufubok' }` + a message containing 'Editorial progress failed'.
```
**Notes:** Attribution: commit fd7360b3 (2026-07-12) "fix(admin): progress failures log + render 'Framvinda ótiltæk' instead of fabricated zeros (finding 22)", merged to main via PR #271 (febebbcc, branch fix/fail-loud-sweep) — the earliest merge on the ancestry path fd7360b3..HEAD, and the branch name matches the review's own Batch E ("Fail-loud sweep"). Found with `git log -S "editorialProgressUnavailable" -- server/routes/admin.js`.

NOT a residual: books.html:1662 still coerces a missing percent to `0`, but that 0 only feeds the progress *bar* width; the *label* is unavailable-guarded, so an admin can tell a broken pipeline from an untouched book — exactly what the finding asked for, and what the commit message calls "keeping the progress bar honest (empty, not fabricated)". Do not reopen on that line alone.

Out of scope for this row (context only): the same commit also fixed four sibling unlogged catches in admin.js (VALID_BOOKS refresh, assignments dashboard, manual-migration loop, validate-pipeline's getStageData) — those belong to findings 20/21/37 in Batch E, not to finding 22.

No new problems observed in the audited code path.
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — Attacked the defect, not the fix, and it does not survive. Re-located by symbol (`getEditorialProgress` / `book.editorialProgress`) inside the single `router.get('/books', requireAuth, requireRole(ROLES.EDITOR), ...)` handler in server/routes/admin.js (only one such route in the file; mounted once at server/index.js:241 `app.use('/api/admin', adminRoutes)`), so there is no shadowing handler. FIXING CODE, server/routes/admin.js:409-413: `} catch (err) { log.error({ err, book: book.slug }, 'Editorial progress failed for book list'); book.editorialProgress = null; book.editorialProgressUnavailable = true; }` — the bare unlogged catch and its fabricated `{percent:0, approvedSegments:0, totalSegments:0}` triple are gone; `log` is real pino (`const log = require('../lib/logger')` at admin.js:17, logger.js wraps pino). Both halves of the requested fix are present: the real error is logged with book context, and the value is explicitly unavailable (null + flag) rather than a fabricated zero. The flag reaches the client — `listRegisteredBooks` returns plain object literals (bookRegistration.js:693-709) serialized via `res.json({books, total})` — and the consumer distinguishes the states at server/views/books.html:1661-1664 (`pctLabel = book.editorialProgressUnavailable ? 'Framvinda ótiltæk' : pct + '% lokið'`); the bar width falling back to 0 is not indistinguishable because the sibling label differs. NON-THROWING PATH CONSIDERED AND DISPOSED OF: `getEditorialProgress` can return all-zeros WITHOUT throwing, because `segmentParser.listChapters` returns [] when `books/<slug>/02-for-mt` is absent (segmentParser.js:379-381) and `countModuleSegments` returns 0 on a missing EN file (segmentParser.js:438), so the outer catch never fires. That reproduces the claim's SYMPTOM by a different mechanism, but it does not resurrect this finding: (1) the finding names the mechanism — the bare catch and its exact fabricated triple in the /books handler — which is gone; (2) that existsSync-guarded quiet-zero path predates the review baseline and is unchanged, so it is not what the review described; (3) in this repo a missing 02-for-mt/chNN is a legitimate state (only 13 of 259 biology modules are extracted), so returning 0 there is correct, not fabrication. Genuine I/O failures inside those helpers (readdirSync/readFileSync on an existing-but-unreadable path) still throw and hit the logging catch. Durability guard (secondary evidence, not the basis): server/__tests__/adminBooksHonesty.test.js:98-104 drops `segment_edits` to force the throw and asserts null + flag + logged error.
- ✅ upheld — Could not refute for the claim as written, but with a boundary. SERVER HALF: server/routes/admin.js:409-413 inside `router.get('/books', requireAuth, requireRole(ROLES.EDITOR) …)` now reads `catch (err) { log.error({ err, book: book.slug }, 'Editorial progress failed for book list'); book.editorialProgress = null; book.editorialProgressUnavailable = true; }` — both halves of the requested fix (log the real error; represent as explicitly unavailable, not a fabricated zero). `log` is the pino instance (`const log = require('../lib/logger')`, admin.js:17). CONSUMER SCOPE: I enumerated every caller of `/api/admin/books` — books.html:1642 (list), admin.html:679 `usersLoadBooks`, server/public/js/localization-editor.js:1723 `rvLoadBooks`. Only books.html renders progress, and it distinguishes at books.html:1661-1665 (`book.editorialProgressUnavailable ? 'Framvinda ótiltæk' : pct + '% lokið'`) — distinguishable by LABEL, not by bar (the bar still renders `width:0%` because `pct` falls back to 0 at :1662). No other producer of `book.editorialProgress` exists (grep across server/ finds only admin.js:404/411). SIBLING CALL SITES of `getEditorialProgress`: status.js:939 is unwrapped and fails loud with a 500; admin.js:1007 (assignments dashboard) now logs (`'Editorial progress failed for assignments dashboard'`). All from one commit, fd7360b3 ('fix(admin): progress failures log + render Framvinda ótiltæk instead of fabricated zeros (finding 22)'), which also logged three further silent catches in the same file. Durability: server/__tests__/adminBooksHonesty.test.js DROPs `segment_edits` to force the throw and asserts null + flag + the log call. BOUNDARY (why this is not a clean 'both halves fixed'): the finding named two triggers — 'a database error, or a filesystem permissions issue'. Only the THROW path is closed. `segmentParser.listChapters` (server/services/segmentParser.js:378-381) uses `fs.existsSync(mtDir)`, which returns false on EACCES/EPERM, so a permissions failure returns [] → totalSegments 0 → percentComplete 0 on the SUCCESS path; the catch never fires, nothing is logged, and books.html shows '0% lokið' byte-identically to an untouched book. That is a different symbol in a different file never cited by the review, so per scope discipline it is a note rather than a ledger reversal — but the row should not be read as closing the filesystem-permissions trigger.
- ✅ upheld — Cannot refute — the described failure no longer completes. (1) Locus re-located by symbol, not line: the bare catch is inside `router.get('/books', requireAuth, requireRole(ROLES.EDITOR), ...)` in server/routes/admin.js, and `git log -L 396,415:server/routes/admin.js` shows commit fd7360b3 (2026-07-12, on main — `git merge-base --is-ancestor fd7360b3 main` passes) replacing the exact cited line `catch { book.editorialProgress = { percent: 0, approvedSegments: 0, totalSegments: 0 }; }` with server/routes/admin.js:409-413 `catch (err) { log.error({ err, book: book.slug }, 'Editorial progress failed for book list'); book.editorialProgress = null; book.editorialProgressUnavailable = true; }` (`log` is the real pino logger, admin.js:17 `require('../lib/logger')`). (2) Mechanism traced step-by-step, not assumed: the discriminating question is whether `segmentEditorService.getEditorialProgress` actually THROWS on a genuine DB failure rather than returning zeros — settled empirically by server/__tests__/adminBooksHonesty.test.js:63 `db.exec('DROP TABLE segment_edits'); // force getEditorialProgress to throw`, then asserting (:98-104) `editorialProgress === null`, `editorialProgressUnavailable === true`, and `logger.error` called with `{book:'prufubok'}` + a message containing 'Editorial progress failed'. It throws, the catch fires, the error is logged. (3) The one way the fabricated zero could reconstitute INSIDE the fixed expression — `progress.summary.percentComplete` coming back non-numeric, which would make books.html's `typeof ep.percent === 'number' ? ep.percent : 0` render '0% lokið' with the flag falsy and nothing logged — is impossible: segmentEditorService.js:1481-1483 returns `percentComplete: totalSegments > 0 ? Math.round((totalApproved / totalSegments) * 1000) / 10 : 0`, unconditionally a number. (4) The 'visually distinguishable' half of the fix is real and is the ONLY consumer: exhaustive `grep -rn editorialProgress server/ --include=*.js --include=*.html` returns only admin.js, the test, and server/views/books.html:1661-1665, where `pctLabel = book.editorialProgressUnavailable ? 'Framvinda ótiltæk' : pct + '% lokið'` is emitted into the book card's stats span — so a broken pipeline reads 'Framvinda ótiltæk' where an untouched book reads '0% lokið'. The other two `/api/admin/books` callers (views/admin.html:679 user-book list, public/js/localization-editor.js:1723 book <select>) never touch `editorialProgress`, so `null` breaks nothing. No `percent: 0` or bare `catch {` fabrication remains anywhere in routes/admin.js; the sibling site at admin.js:1006-1012 (assignments dashboard) logs too.
