The review's own note that the concordance half was "dormant only because no book has appendix translations checked in yet" still holds — closure is by code+unit-test, not by observed prod indexing; a [LEAD] deploy rider survives in the register (I14-R10 / item-14 deploy note): one-time prod sanity `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM tm_segments WHERE chapter = 'appendices';` expected 0. NEW OBSERVATION, out of scope (mentioned per scope discipline, NOT a ledger row): `findRepetitions` (:238-242) passes its raw `chapter` straight to `loadModuleForEditing`, and `repetitionReport` (:291-293) filters with `String(chapter)` — neither normalizes, so a literal 'appendices' string would yield the old 'chappendices' path / a label that never matches the stored '-1'. Currently inert: both live callers pass the already-normalized number (server/routes/segment-editor.js:246 `concordance.repetitionReport(req.params.book, req.chapterNum)` and :1028 `concordance.findRepetitions(`, with `req.chapterNum = -1` set by server/middleware/validateParams.js:24).
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — Both halves of the described failure are dead in today's code. HALF 1 (backfill silently skips appendices): server/services/concordanceService.js:174-180 converts the on-disk dirname to the canonical number at discovery — `if (!/^ch\d+$/.test(dir) && dir !== 'appendices') continue;` / `const chapter = dir === 'appendices' ? -1 : parseInt(dir.replace('ch',''),10);` / `const r = indexModule(b, chapter, moduleId);` — so the 'appendices' faithful dir is scanned and reaches indexModule as -1, which resolves through chapterDir(-1) → 'appendices' (verified above for 17a), i.e. a real path, not a dead one. HALF 2 (per-edit path stores a different label than the backfill): there is now exactly ONE writer of tm_segments — indexModule — and it canonicalizes at entry and stores one form: :97-99 `const chapterNum = normalizeChapter(chapter); if (chapterNum === null) throw new TypeError(...)`, :105 `loadModuleForEditing(book, chapterNum, moduleId)`, :111 `const chapterLabel = String(chapterNum);`, :148 `insert.run({ book, chapter: chapterLabel, ... })`. I grepped every non-test INSERT/DELETE against tm_segments: all are inside indexModule; the only other entry points are the two apply/restore callers segmentEditorService.js:1137 and contentVersionService.js:309, which pass the same value through the same normalizer. So backfill and per-edit both persist '-1' — no split left to reconcile. READ side: repetitionReport (:290-296) binds `String(chapter)`, and its only caller routes/segment-editor.js:246 passes req.chapterNum, which validateBookChapter (middleware/validateParams.js:23-25) has already turned into -1 → '-1', matching the stored label (tm_segments.chapter is TEXT, migrations/036-tm-segments.js:26, so affinity does not break the compare). Empirically confirmed by the test file's own in-memory-DB fixtures, which I ran green: server/__tests__/concordanceService.test.js:198 backfill indexes an appendices module (indexed === 1), :207 and :215 assert the stored label is exactly '-1' from BOTH the backfill and the indexModule(-1) apply-path form (this is the precise 'two incompatible labels' assertion), :224 repetitionReport(-1) returns rows, :234 indexModule('chappendices') throws. CAVEAT (does not resurrect the finding, flagged for honesty): repetitionReport itself does NOT call normalizeChapter — it only String()s — so a FUTURE caller passing the word 'appendices' would query chapter='appendices' and get an empty report; today no such caller exists. That is a residue of the review's secondary 'standardize the label' recommendation, not of the described failure.
- ✅ upheld — CLOSED — both halves of the claim have no surviving code path, verified by running the backfill, not by reading it. (1) Backfill: server/services/concordanceService.js:174-175 converts the on-disk dirname at discovery — `if (!/^ch\d+$/.test(dir) && dir !== 'appendices') continue; const chapter = dir === 'appendices' ? -1 : parseInt(dir.replace('ch',''),10);`. `git show a8d9aa64` proves the exact old defect was `? 'appendices' :` on that line (the string then hit the old unnormalized chapterDir -> 'chappendices' -> loadModuleForEditing threw -> caught at :107 -> {indexed:0} while modules++ still counted, i.e. 'reports success'). MY RUN with an isolated fixture (in-memory DB seeded from server/migrations/036-tm-segments.js, _setTestBooksDir + _setTestDb, a books/mybook/{02-for-mt,03-faithful-translation}/appendices/m99001 pair): `backfill('mybook')` returned {modules:1, indexed:2} and concordance `search('boiling point')` returned the appendix row — appendix content IS indexed. (2) Label divergence: indexModule normalizes at :97 and stores ONE canonical label at :111 `const chapterLabel = String(chapterNum);`, which :148 inserts. My run stored chapter='-1' identically from all three entry paths — backfill, indexModule(book,-1,...) (the per-edit path used by segmentEditorService.js:1137 and contentVersionService.js:309, both fed req.chapterNum integers), and indexModule(book,'appendices',...). No stale mismatched prod rows are possible either: the OLD backfill threw before any INSERT, so an 'appendices'-labelled row was never written. NOTE (adjacent, outside the claim's two named call sites): findRepetitions (:242) passes chapter to loadModuleForEditing unnormalized and repetitionReport (:293) does a bare `String(chapter)` against the stored '-1' — both are safe today only because every route feeds req.chapterNum = -1 (validateParams.js:24), so they are latent, not live.
- ✅ upheld — CLOSED — both halves of the claim are dead, and the fix sits UPSTREAM of the still-fragile helper, which is what makes it hold. (1) Backfill: server/services/concordanceService.js:174-175 admits the appendices dirname and converts it at discovery — `if (!/^ch\d+$/.test(dir) && dir !== 'appendices') continue;` / `const chapter = dir === 'appendices' ? -1 : parseInt(dir.replace('ch',''),10);` — then calls indexModule(b, -1, moduleId) at :180. git show a8d9aa64 confirms the exact pre-fix line was `dir === 'appendices' ? 'appendices' : ...`, i.e. this diff IS the finding's fix. (2) Label-form split: indexModule normalizes at :97-99 and, critically, passes the NORMALIZED value into the path builder at :105 (`loadModuleForEditing(book, chapterNum, moduleId)`), so the surviving unnormalized chapterDir fragility is bypassed on exactly this path; the stored label is derived once at :111 (`const chapterLabel = String(chapterNum);`). SCOPE CHECK — I enumerated every writer and every chapter-filtering reader of tm_segments: there is exactly ONE INSERT (`:141-142` executed at `:148 insert.run({ book, chapter: chapterLabel, ... })`), reached by all three entry paths (backfill :180, per-apply segmentEditorService.js:1137, restore contentVersionService.js:309), so two label forms can no longer coexist. Chapter-filtering readers: repetitionReport :291-293 (`params.push(String(chapter))`) is called from routes/segment-editor.js:246 with req.chapterNum, which middleware/validateParams.js:22-25 sets to numeric -1 → '-1', matching the stored label; search() :218-221 and findRepetitions :248 do not filter on chapter at all. Apply-path chapter sources verified concrete, not assumed: req.chapterNum (numeric) at routes/segment-editor.js:1259/1325, and review.review.chapter at :887 from module_reviews.chapter declared `INTEGER NOT NULL` (server/migrations/008-segment-editing.js:57) → -1. No stale-row reconciliation is needed either: the old backfill's word-label call threw inside loadModuleForEditing and was caught into a warn+`return {indexed:0}`, so no appendix rows were ever written, and for ordinary chapters the old `String(chapter)` and new `String(chapterNum)` are byte-identical. Test-pinned at server/__tests__/concordanceService.test.js:197-235. NOTE (not a refutation): findRepetitions :242 still passes the raw `chapter` into loadModuleForEditing, and the new TypeErrors thrown by indexModule are swallowed by the best-effort try/catch at segmentEditorService.js:1137 / contentVersionService.js:309 — a fail-quiet posture, latent only because every live caller passes a canonical value.


## Group: `content-version-service`
### `code-16` — CLOSED
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-code-review.md §16 (summary table row 16 / fan-out #10) + joint summary "Batch B — Apply and version-history integrity"
**Original severity:** Low (downgraded from Medium), CONFIRMED · lens: integrity
**Claim:** Restoring an earlier module version writes the faithful file correctly but never refreshes the derived caches rebuilt from that file (concordance search index + TM export), so search/reuse can briefly surface the just-discarded newer text until some other apply re-triggers indexing.
**Current location:** `server/services/contentVersionService.js:302-323 (restoreVersion; write at :295)`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
restoreVersion now runs the apply path's post-write hooks immediately after cfg.write():

server/services/contentVersionService.js:294-312
  // 5. Write the restored content back as the new baseline for this track
  const savedPath = cfg.write(book, chapter, moduleId, restoredSegments);

  // Keep derived caches current — the same two best-effort steps the apply
  // path runs after writing the faithful file (segmentEditorService
  // :995-1009). Never fail the restore over a cache refresh. ...
  if (cfg.runPostWriteHooks) {
    try {
      tmService.scheduleTmRegen(book);
    } catch (err) {
      log.error({ err, book }, 'Scheduling TM regeneration after restore failed');
    }
    try {
      concordanceService.indexModule(book, chapter, moduleId);
    } catch (err) {
      log.error({ err, book, moduleId }, 'Concordance indexing after restore failed');
    }

Parity with the apply path is real, not asserted: server/services/segmentEditorService.js:1129 `tmService.scheduleTmRegen(book);` and :1137 `concordanceService.indexModule(book, chapter, moduleId);` — the same two calls the finding said restore skipped.

The `cfg.runPostWriteHooks` gate (a later item-15 narrowing, TRACKS at :31 faithful=true / :42 localized=false) does NOT reopen the finding: both caches are faithful-only by construction, verified in code rather than from the comment — server/services/concordanceService.js:96 `function indexModule(book, chapter, moduleId) {` → :105 `data = segmentParser.loadModuleForEditing(book, chapterNum, moduleId);` (faithful baseline) and its backfill root at :171 `const faithfulRoot = path.join(BOOKS_DIR, b, '03-faithful-translation');`. TM is likewise built from `02-for-mt/` + `03-faithful-translation/` pairs (generate-tm.js).

Regression test: server/__tests__/contentVersionService.test.js:358 `it('restore reindexes concordance and schedules TM regen (F16)', () => {` … asserting "Concordance now reflects the RESTORED text (indexModule re-read the file)."

Attribution (verified, not guessed): commit 809566d7 `fix(item12): restore reindexes concordance + schedules TM regen (F16)` (2026-07-17), confirmed an ancestor of HEAD via `git merge-base --is-ancestor 809566d7 HEAD`.
```
**Notes:** Closed by campaign item 12 (2026-07-17), the batch that absorbed the audit's Batch B; the predecessor register docs/plans/2026-07-11-pre-semester-coding-campaign.md §"Register — findings/deferrals from item 12" is the item-12 block and its I12-M6 minor references the sibling F19 fix by name. Located by symbol (restoreVersion / scheduleTmRegen / indexModule), not by the review's stale :223. Second pass DOWNGRADED this Medium→Low; it did not refute it, hence CLOSED rather than CLOSED-BY-REFUTATION. Out-of-scope observation (not a ledger row): the localized restore branch also skips acceptanceService.lapseDrifted/writeReviewStatusSidecar (:317-322) since those sit inside the same runPostWriteHooks gate — that is item-20b code postdating this review, so it is not this finding.
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — I attacked the defect independently and could not make it reachable. Current code: server/services/contentVersionService.js:295 writes the file (`const savedPath = cfg.write(...)` → TRACKS.faithful.write = segmentParser.saveModuleSegments, which is SYNCHRONOUS on disk: segmentParser.js:207-208 `fs.writeFileSync(tmpPath, content)` + `fs.renameSync(tmpPath, paths.faithful)`), and :302-312 immediately runs the two hooks the finding said restore skipped — `tmService.scheduleTmRegen(book)` (:304) and `concordanceService.indexModule(book, chapter, moduleId)` (:309). Parity with the apply path is real: segmentEditorService.js:1129 and :1137 call the same two. The reindex provably sees the RESTORED bytes, not the discarded ones: concordanceService.js:96 indexModule → :105 `segmentParser.loadModuleForEditing(book, chapterNum, moduleId)`, which re-reads the faithful file from disk (segmentParser.js:114-116 `if (fs.existsSync(paths.faithful)) isSegments = parseSegments(fs.readFileSync(paths.faithful,'utf-8'))`) — i.e. after the rename at :208. The `if (cfg.runPostWriteHooks)` gate (:302) does NOT reopen it: restoreVersion's signature defaults `track = 'faithful'` (:243) and the faithful restore route server/routes/segment-editor.js:1446-1451 OMITS the track argument, so it takes TRACKS.faithful with `runPostWriteHooks: true` (:31). I enumerated every caller of restoreVersion (`grep -rn restoreVersion server/ tools/ scripts/` minus tests) — exactly two: segment-editor.js:1446 (faithful, hooks run) and localization-editor.js:875 which passes 'localized' (:42 runPostWriteHooks:false), and skipping there is correct because both caches are faithful-only by construction, not by comment: concordance indexes via loadModuleForEditing (faithful/mt baseline) and its backfill root is concordanceService.js `03-faithful-translation`, while TM pairs 02-for-mt with 03-faithful-translation (tools/lib/tm-export.cjs:294 `const faithfulRoot = path.join(BOOKS_DIR, book, '03-faithful-translation')`, :336-337). A localized restore therefore cannot make either cache stale. Not chased, per the finding's own stated fix ("call the same reindexing steps the apply path already calls"): tmService's DEBOUNCE_MS delay is identical for restore and apply, so the debounce is parity, not a residue of the defect.
- ✅ upheld — Mechanism verified end-to-end, not just the presence of the calls. server/services/contentVersionService.js:295 writes the file (`const savedPath = cfg.write(book, chapter, moduleId, restoredSegments);`) and :302-312 then runs `tmService.scheduleTmRegen(book)` and `concordanceService.indexModule(book, chapter, moduleId)` inside best-effort try/catch — the same pair the apply path runs at server/services/segmentEditorService.js:1129 and :1137. Tracing the review's concrete failure ("search can still surface the just-discarded newer text"): concordanceService.indexModule (concordanceService.js:96) re-reads from disk via segmentParser.loadModuleForEditing at :105 (faithful-first baseline, segmentParser.js:114), and its transaction PURGES the stale rows before inserting — :129-141 emits FTS `'delete'` rows for every prior id and :143 `conn.prepare(`DELETE FROM tm_segments WHERE book = ? AND module_id = ?`).run(book, moduleId)` — so the withdrawn text is removed from both tm_segments and the FTS5 mirror rather than merely being joined by new rows. The TM half: tmService.js:80 scheduleTmRegen debounces 5s and spawns tools/generate-tm.js --book (tmService.js:32), which reads the faithful files the restore already wrote synchronously. The `cfg.runPostWriteHooks` gate does not reopen it: the faithful track sets it true (contentVersionService.js:31) and the only other caller path, routes/localization-editor.js:875, passes track 'localized' (runPostWriteHooks false at :42) — correct, since indexModule reads loadModuleForEditing (faithful) and generate-tm pairs 02-for-mt/ with 03-faithful-translation/, neither of which a localized restore touches. Only two restoreVersion callers exist (routes/segment-editor.js:1446 faithful, routes/localization-editor.js:875 localized); no faithful-restore path bypasses the hooks. Empirically confirmed: `npx vitest run server/__tests__/contentVersionService.test.js` → 16/16 passed, including :358 'restore reindexes concordance and schedules TM regen (F16)', which asserts the post-restore tm_segments row's is_text equals the RESTORED text and that tmService._pendingBooks() contains the book. Attribution verified read-only: commit 809566d7 'fix(item12): restore reindexes concordance + schedules TM regen (F16)' is an ancestor of HEAD. Caveat noted but not a reopening: a book lacking a book-licences.cjs row makes the spawned generate-tm exit non-zero silently (tracked as I21-R2) — that degrades the apply path identically, so it is not the restore-vs-apply asymmetry this finding described.
- ✅ upheld — CLOSED — scope enumeration is complete. (1) Faithful-write sites: exactly two exist in the server (grep '03-faithful-translation' + saveModuleSegments callers) — segmentEditorService.js:1028 (apply, hooks at :1129 tmService.scheduleTmRegen / :1137 concordanceService.indexModule) and contentVersionService.js:30 TRACKS.faithful.write (restore). Restore now runs the identical pair at contentVersionService.js:304 and :309, each in its own try/catch. The step of the failure scenario that dies: 'only ever refreshed by the normal apply path, not by restore' — no longer true at either site. (2) restoreVersion callers: exactly two — routes/segment-editor.js:1446 (no track arg -> 'faithful', hooks run) and routes/localization-editor.js:875 (track 'localized'). (3) The cfg.runPostWriteHooks:false gate on 'localized' (:42) does not reopen the finding, verified in code not comment: concordanceService only ever loads via segmentParser.loadModuleForEditing (:105 indexModule, :242 findRepetitions) i.e. the faithful baseline, and the TM export sources 02-for-mt + 03-faithful-translation only (tools/lib/tm-export.cjs:294, :336-337; tools/generate-tm.js:82-83). Nothing derives from 04-localized-content/, so a localized restore has no cache to refresh. (4) Appendix path checked and does NOT survive as a gap: indexModule's chapter guard at concordanceService.js:97 uses the shared chapterLabel.normalizeChapter, which maps 'appendices' -> -1 (server/lib/chapterLabel.js:25), so an appendix restore reindexes rather than throwing into the catch. (5) Residual weaknesses the restore hooks inherit (I21-R2 silently-stale TM for a book with no licence row; the debounced fire-and-forget regen in tmService.js:80-91) hit the apply path identically — the fix's promise was parity with apply, and parity is what it delivers, so these are not code-16 scope gaps. Regression pin at server/__tests__/contentVersionService.test.js:358 ('restore reindexes concordance and schedules TM regen (F16)'). Attribution 809566d7, confirmed ancestor of HEAD via git merge-base --is-ancestor.

### `code-19` — CLOSED
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-code-review.md §19 (summary table row 19 / fan-out #23) + joint summary "Batch B — Apply and version-history integrity"
**Original severity:** Low, CONFIRMED · lens: integrity
**Claim:** snapshotModule's `if (seg.content)` guard skips empty (untranslated) segments, so the safety snapshot taken before a restore has no record of a now-empty segment — undoing the restore then leaves that segment holding whatever the restore wrote instead of returning it to empty; and an all-empty module can yield a version with zero rows that later throws "version not found".
**Current location:** `server/services/contentVersionService.js:112-133 (snapshotModule insertAll loop; the unconditional insert.run at :120-129)`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
The falsy guard is gone; every segment is inserted unconditionally, with the rationale recorded in-code:

server/services/contentVersionService.js:112-131
  const insertAll = db.transaction(() => {
    let count = 0;
    for (const seg of segments) {
      // Record every segment, empty string included: at restore time,
      // absence-from-snapshot is indistinguishable from "was never extracted",
      // which broke restore-undo for untranslated segments (item 12, F19).
      // Nullish content throws (NOT NULL / binding error) and aborts the
      // snapshot transaction — a bad caller must fail loud, not shrink history.
      insert.run(
        book,
        chapter,
        moduleId,
        seg.segmentId,
        seg.content,
        nextVersion,
        track,
        appliedBy
      );
      count++;
    }

Second half of the claim also resolved: because empties are now rows, an all-empty module yields N rows, so restoreVersion's `if (snapshot.length === 0) throw new Error(\`Version ${version} not found for ${moduleId}\`)` (:250-252) is no longer reachable for an all-empty module — only for a genuinely zero-segment one.

Regression test: server/__tests__/contentVersionService.test.js:285 `it('restore-then-undo returns an empty segment to empty (F19)', () => {` with the comment at :325 "Undo (restore the pre-restore snapshot) → fs-id003 must be EMPTY again."; a second pin at :591 "Empty string from v1 restored verbatim (F19: empties are recorded)".

Attribution (verified, not guessed): commit 3b38f393 `fix(item12): snapshot empty segments so restore-undo is faithful (F19)`, confirmed an ancestor of HEAD; `git log -S "if (seg.content)"` shows the guard introduced by 9a3011e1 (original versioning feature) and removed by 3b38f393.
```
**Notes:** Closed by campaign item 12 (2026-07-17). One residual sliver is explicitly tracked and deliberately deferred, not open code: docs/plans/2026-07-11-pre-semester-coding-campaign.md I12-M6 — "F19 undefined-half untested (code-path-identical to null)" — i.e. the fail-loud nullish branch lacks a test for `undefined` specifically. That is test debt on an already-shipped fix, so it does not make the finding OPEN. Located by symbol (snapshotModule / seg.content), not by the review's stale :68. Second pass CONFIRMED and left the rating, so this is CLOSED, not CLOSED-BY-REFUTATION.
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — Independently unreachable in current code, and the closure is not merely the guard's removal. (a) The falsy guard is gone: server/services/contentVersionService.js:112-131 `const insertAll = db.transaction(() => { for (const seg of segments) { insert.run(book, chapter, moduleId, seg.segmentId, seg.content, nextVersion, track, appliedBy); count++; } })` — no `if (seg.content)`, so an empty string is recorded as a row. (b) The step a cosmetic fix would have missed is also right: restoreVersion reads the snapshot back with a KEY-PRESENCE test, not truthiness — :277 `if (snapshotLookup.has(seg.segmentId))` then :279 `return { segmentId: seg.segmentId, content: snapshotLookup.get(seg.segmentId) }`; a `lookup[id] || currentContent(seg)` idiom would have round-tripped '' back to the restore's text and the finding would have survived. (c) The pre-restore safety snapshot itself feeds every segment in: :259-262 `data.segments.map(seg => ({ segmentId: seg.segmentId, content: cfg.currentContent(seg) }))` with TRACKS.faithful.currentContent = `(seg) => seg.is || ''` (:28), so a now-empty segment enters snapshotModule as '' rather than being dropped. Walked end-to-end: empty X → restore writes text → undo restores snapshot S where X='' → X written empty; and re-reading is empty either way, since loadModuleForEditing falls back to mt-output only when the WHOLE faithful file is absent (segmentParser.js:114-120), never per-segment, with `is: isLookup[id] ? ... : ''` (:155-157). (d) Second half of the claim: with empties recorded, an all-empty module yields N rows, so the `if (snapshot.length === 0) throw new Error(\`Version ${version} not found for ${moduleId}\`)` at :250-252 is only reachable for a genuinely zero-segment module. Adjacent, NOT the claim (flagged, does not change the verdict): server/scripts/backfill-content-versions.js:118 still does `.filter((s) => s.content && s.content.trim())` before calling snapshotModule at :131 — a caller-side empty filter. It is not the pre-restore safety-snapshot path the finding cites, and an all-empty module there `continue`s (skippedNoMt, :120-125) instead of writing a zero-row version, so neither half of the claim runs through it. But restoring to a backfill-seeded v1 will take the `segmentsKept` branch (:281-282) for segments empty at seed time — the same absence-vs-empty ambiguity the in-code comment at :115-119 claims to have retired; reachable only for prod modules actually seeded by that script, not verifiable from here.
- ✅ upheld — Both halves of the failure scenario are dead. (a) The falsy guard is gone: server/services/contentVersionService.js:112-131 `const insertAll = db.transaction(() => { ... for (const seg of segments) { insert.run(book, chapter, moduleId, seg.segmentId, seg.content, nextVersion, track, appliedBy); count++; } ...` — no `if (seg.content)`, and `grep -rn "if (seg.content)" server/ tools/ scripts/` returns nothing, so no second snapshot implementation reintroduces it. (b) I traced the undo step by step rather than trusting the insert. The pre-restore snapshot is built from `cfg.currentContent(seg)` = `seg.is || ''` (:29 / :277-280), so an untranslated segment is stored as an explicit '' row; on undo, restoreVersion matches by MEMBERSHIP not truthiness — :281 `if (snapshotLookup.has(seg.segmentId))` over `new Map(snapshot.map((s) => [s.segment_id, s.content]))` (:262) — so the '' is re-imposed instead of the segment being 'kept' at whatever the restore wrote; and the write actually persists the emptiness because segmentParser.assembleSegments (segmentParser.js:60-66) emits `<!-- SEG:... -->\n${seg.content}` unconditionally, and loadModuleForEditing pairs off the EN file (:149-159) so an empty/absent IS body resolves to `is: ''` either way. The 'version not found' edge (contentVersionService.js:250-252) is now unreachable for an all-empty module because it yields N rows, not zero. Empirically confirmed: `npx vitest run server/__tests__/contentVersionService.test.js` → 16/16 passed, including :285 'restore-then-undo returns an empty segment to empty (F19)' (writes a faithful file with fs-id003 = '', restores a past version giving it 'Gamalt 3', then restores the pre-restore snapshotVersion and asserts `readFaithful(booksDir)[SEG('fs-id003')]` is '' — i.e. the assertion reads the FILE round-trip, not the DB), :271 'an all-empty module produces a real, listable version (F19 phantom fix)', and :332 which pins the deliberate fail-loud/rollback behaviour for nullish content. Attribution verified read-only: commit 3b38f393 'fix(item12): snapshot empty segments so restore-undo is faithful (F19)' is an ancestor of HEAD. The only behaviour change I could construct as a risk — a caller passing undefined/null content now aborting the whole snapshot — is intentional (documented in-code at :114-119) and is pinned to roll back completely, so it cannot silently shrink history; it is a different failure mode from the one this finding describes, and no live caller can reach it (restoreVersion, segmentEditorService.js:1010-1013 and saveLocalizedWithSnapshot all coerce with `|| ''`).
- ✅ upheld — CLOSED — both halves of the stated scenario are unreachable, and every snapshotModule caller was checked. The falsy guard is gone: contentVersionService.js:120-129 inserts every segment unconditionally inside insertAll. Half (a) 'the safety snapshot taken before a restore has no record of a now-empty segment': restoreVersion builds that snapshot at :259-262 from ALL data.segments with content: cfg.currentContent(seg) (:28 seg.is || '' for faithful, :37 for localized), so an empty segment is now an explicit '' row — the undo therefore finds it and blanks it again (pinned at server/__tests__/contentVersionService.test.js:285 'restore-then-undo returns an empty segment to empty (F19)' and :591 'Empty string from v1 restored verbatim'). Half (b) 'all-empty module yields zero rows -> version not found': an all-empty module now yields N rows, so the throw at :250-252 is reachable only for a genuinely zero-segment module. Caller enumeration: segmentEditorService.js:1009-1013 (apply) passes all segments with seg.is || ''; contentVersionService.js:263 (restore) and :392 (saveLocalizedWithSnapshot) likewise pass all. ONE CAVEAT the verifier did not surface, which I judge a note rather than a survival: server/scripts/backfill-content-versions.js:120 still does .filter((s) => s.content && s.content.trim()) before calling snapshotModule (:131) — a one-off seeding script, predating the review (de756f1a, 2026-06-13), whose seeded v1 rows therefore omit empty MT segments, so a FORWARD restore to that v1 won't blank them. That is related harm in a different scenario (not the pre-restore safety snapshot the finding describes) and is unrepairable in code for rows already written to the prod DB. Worth flagging: the item-12 design doc (docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-17-item12-apply-job-version-integrity-design.md:195) dismissed this site as pre-filtering 'trimmed-empty MODULES … and is unaffected' — that is a misreading of the code, which filters SEGMENTS and only then skips the module if zero survive. The dismissal's premise is wrong even though the verdict on the finding as written still stands. Attribution 3b38f393, confirmed ancestor of HEAD; git log -S 'if (seg.content)' shows the guard introduced by 9a3011e1 and removed by 3b38f393.


## Group: `feedback-service-eager-db`
### `code-20` — CLOSED
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-code-review.md § Finding details #20 (ranked-findings row 20, orig. fan-out #24); also Batch E "Fail-loud sweep"
**Original severity:** Low, CONFIRMED (lens: state)
**Claim:** server/services/feedbackService.js opened (and created tables in) the real sessions.db as a require()-time side effect (`let db = initDb(); let statements = initStatements(db);`) rather than lazily on first use, defeating SESSIONS_DB_PATH-based test isolation and breaking its own test suite.
**Current location:** `server/services/feedbackService.js:79-91 (and :177-183)`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
Both eager statements the review quoted are deleted. Current code, feedbackService.js:73-91 — `let _testDb = null;` / `function _setTestDb(db) { _testDb = db; _stmts = null; }` / `let _db;` / `function getDb() { if (_testDb) return _testDb; if (!_db) { const dbDir = path.dirname(DB_PATH); if (!fs.existsSync(dbDir)) { fs.mkdirSync(dbDir, { recursive: true }); } _db = new Database(DB_PATH); _db.pragma('journal_mode = WAL'); } return _db; }`. The second half (statement prep) is lazy too, :177-183 — `let _stmts = null;` / `function stmts() { if (!_stmts) { _stmts = initStatements(getDb()); } return _stmts; }`. Behavioural pin exists and is green today: server/__tests__/lazyDbOpen.test.js requires each of the five ex-eager services in a SUBPROCESS with SESSIONS_DB_PATH into a fresh temp dir — `expect(fs.existsSync(dbPath)).toBe(false);` — with `'services/feedbackService'` listed at :25 and a positive control ('first real call DOES open/create the file'). `npx vitest run server/__tests__/lazyDbOpen.test.js` → 6 passed. Test override in use: server/__tests__/feedbackService.test.js:25 `feedbackService._setTestDb(db);`.
```
**Notes:** ATTRIBUTION (verified, not guessed): commit 12dcdb7c 'refactor(db): feedbackService + analyticsService lazy-open; analytics gets first tests (design D4/D5)' (2026-07-12), found via `git log -S"function getDb()" -- server/services/feedbackService.js`; reachable from main via merge febebbcc 'Merge pull request #271 from SigurdurVilhelmsson/fix/fail-loud-sweep'. The pre-semester register (docs/plans/2026-07-11-pre-semester-coding-campaign.md:14) independently records Batch 4 / PR #271 converting 'All 5 eager DB-open services (not 1) ... to lazy getDb() + _setTestDb (activityLog, notifications, localizationLog, feedbackService, analyticsService)'. RESIDUAL, deliberately NOT re-opened: `const DB_PATH = resolveDbPath();` still evaluates at import (feedbackService.js:26), so SESSIONS_DB_PATH set AFTER require still has no effect on the path — but the review's own prescribed fix was 'convert this service to the same open-when-first-needed pattern ... keeping the existing test-database override hook', which is exactly what shipped, and lazyDbOpen.test.js documents the freeze as a known worked-around property ('Subprocess because DB_PATH freezes at import'). Same shape as every other service, so not a residual defect of this finding. NOT CONFLATED: analyticsService's identical eager-open is a separate row (the review's exclusion list, 'Project memory / test-isolation notes') — but note that it was fixed by the SAME commit 12dcdb7c and analyticsService.js:41-53 now has the same lazy `getDb()`. OUT-OF-SCOPE OBSERVATION (register drift, not a ledger row): MEMORY.md:44 still asserts '`analyticsService.js` opens DB eagerly at module load (test hazard)' — that statement is now false on main and should be corrected.
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — Independently verified the DEFECT is unreachable, not just that the old lines are gone. server/services/feedbackService.js has NO module-level DB statement: grepping every top-level line yields only requires, `const DB_PATH = resolveDbPath();` (:26), constant objects, `let _testDb`/`let _db`/`let _stmts`, and `module.exports` (:456). All 16 DB call sites funnel through `stmts()` (:177-182 — `if (!_stmts) { _stmts = initStatements(getDb()); }`) into `getDb()` (:80-91 — `if (_testDb) return _testDb; if (!_db) { ... _db = new Database(DB_PATH); ... }`), so no `new Database` runs until a real service function is called. Empirical, ignoring the verifier's evidence: I ran `SESSIONS_DB_PATH=<fresh temp>/sessions.db node -e "require('./server/services/feedbackService.js')"` myself — exit 0, temp dir left EMPTY (`.` and `..` only), and `pipeline-output/` listing byte-identical before/after, so neither the redirected nor the real sessions.db is touched at import. The 'creates tables in' half is gone entirely: no CREATE TABLE/exec() anywhere in the file; migration 005 owns the schema (feedbackService.test.js header: 'the service no longer creates tables itself'). The retained override hook `_setTestDb` is exported (:478) and resets `_stmts` (:76) so statements rebuild against the test handle; feedbackService.test.js:25 uses it and `npx vitest run server/__tests__/lazyDbOpen.test.js server/__tests__/feedbackService.test.js` → 21 passed (the lazyDbOpen spec lists 'services/feedbackService' and has a positive control proving the assertion can fail). Only surviving residue, named for honesty: `DB_PATH` is still frozen at import (:26), so setting SESSIONS_DB_PATH AFTER require still has no effect — but that is byte-identical to `analyticsService.js:22`, the sibling the finding itself names as the target pattern, and the finding's actual mechanism ('opens the real DB before any test-setup code gets a chance to redirect it') is dead. Fix commit 12dcdb7c 'refactor(db): feedbackService + analyticsService lazy-open ... (design D4/D5)' (PR number not determined).
- ✅ upheld — Cannot refute — the fix is complete across every site the claim covers. (1) Both eager statements the review quoted are gone: `git show 12dcdb7c` ("refactor(db): feedbackService + analyticsService lazy-open") deletes `initDb()`, `let db = initDb();` and `let statements = initStatements(db);` from server/services/feedbackService.js. (2) The only `new Database(` in the file is server/services/feedbackService.js:87, inside `getDb()` (:80-91), and the test override is checked FIRST — `if (_testDb) return _testDb;` at :81 short-circuits before `_db` is ever constructed, so `_setTestDb` intercepts ahead of any open, not merely alongside it. `_setTestDb` (:74-77) also nulls `_stmts`, so prepared statements cannot survive against a stale handle. (3) Statement prep is lazy too: `let _stmts = null; function stmts(){ if(!_stmts){ _stmts = initStatements(getDb()); } return _stmts; }` at :177-183. (4) SCOPE — I enumerated every DB consumer in the file: all 18 query call sites (:210, 240, 261, 274, 288, 296, 304, 305, 330, 342, 358, 370, 386, 409) go through `stmts()`; there is no surviving sibling that touches a module-level `db`/`statements`. The file's ONLY module-level executable statement is `const DB_PATH = resolveDbPath();` at :26 — a pure path.join, no I/O — and its only requires are better-sqlite3/path/fs/lib/dbPath, so requiring it drags in no eagerly-opening sibling. Its single consumer is server/routes/feedback.js, which contains no `new Database(` of its own. (5) The deleted `CREATE TABLE` block did not leave an orphan: server/migrations/005-feedback.js owns that schema. (6) Behavioural (not static) pin: server/__tests__/lazyDbOpen.test.js requires each ex-eager service in a SUBPROCESS with SESSIONS_DB_PATH into a fresh temp dir and asserts `expect(fs.existsSync(dbPath)).toBe(false)` ('services/feedbackService' listed at :26), and its positive control (:53-67) asserts `expect(fs.existsSync(dbPath)).toBe(true)` after a first real call — so the test provably detects file creation and green is not a false negative. `npx vitest run server/__tests__/lazyDbOpen.test.js server/__tests__/feedbackService.test.js` → 21 passed, and feedbackService.test.js:25 uses `feedbackService._setTestDb(db)`, so the service's own suite no longer hits the problem the review described. CAVEAT that does NOT keep the failure alive: `DB_PATH` is still frozen at import (:26), so the finding's sub-clause 'setting the environment variable afterward has no effect' is still literally true — but that is the codebase-wide convention (identical `const DB_PATH = resolveDbPath();` at module scope in userService, notifications, analyticsService, terminologyService, activityLog, localizationLog, localizationSuggestions, pipelineStatusService), it was the very pattern the review prescribed ('the same pattern already used by the other services'), the lazyDbOpen test comment is explicitly designed around it, and no DB file is opened or created at require time, which is the defect as stated. Separately noted for accuracy, not as a ledger row: CLAUDE.md and MEMORY.md still assert 'analyticsService.js opens DB eagerly at module load (test hazard)' — that too was fixed in the same commit 12dcdb7c, so the docs are stale.
- ✅ upheld — CLOSED — mechanism verified, not just line-absence. The two module-scope calls the review quoted (`let db = initDb(); let statements = initStatements(db);`) are deleted along with `initDb` itself; the sole `new Database(DB_PATH)` now sits inside `getDb()` (server/services/feedbackService.js:80-89), and all 16 DB call sites route through the lazy `stmts()` (:178). The 'creates tables in' half is structurally impossible: `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS feedback` exists only in server/migrations/005-feedback.js. The redirect hook works after import — `_setTestDb` (:74) short-circuits `getDb()` unconditionally (`if (_testDb) return _testDb;`) AND nulls `_stmts` so statements rebuild against the injected handle; feedbackService.test.js:25 exercises it. I executed the review's failure scenario independently rather than trusting the verifier: in a subprocess with SESSIONS_DB_PATH into a temp dir, bare `require()` gave `db file exists: false`, and the first real call (`getRecentFeedback`) gave `true` — a positive control specific to THIS service (the pin's own control uses activityLog). A bare require with no override left the real 16.9 MB pipeline-output/sessions.db mtime byte-identical (1785059015 before and after). No consumer reopens it: every feedbackService use in routes/feedback.js is inside a request handler; module-scope references touch only label constants. Attribution: service conversion `12dcdb7c` (refactor(db): feedbackService + analyticsService lazy-open, design D4/D5); subprocess pin `c44b0f8a` (server/__tests__/lazyDbOpen.test.js, 'services/feedbackService' at :25). Ran 2 files / 21 tests green — not the full suite. NOTE on the one apparent survivor: `const DB_PATH = resolveDbPath()` at :26 still freezes the path at import, so setting SESSIONS_DB_PATH after import resolves to prod. This is NOT a remnant of finding 20 — the review's prescribed fix was explicitly 'convert to the same pattern already used by the other services', and analyticsService.js:22 and contentVersionService.js:20 have the identical frozen-DB_PATH + lazy-getDb shape; the freeze is the idiom the review endorsed. It is inert here because the path is never dereferenced until a real call, and `_setTestDb` covers the post-import redirect case. The pin's docblock documents the freeze and works around it with a subprocess.


## Group: `notifications-dead`
### `code-33` — OPEN-TRACKED
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-code-review.md §33 ("`server/services/notifications.js:373` — Four notification functions have had no callers for months"), summary table row 33 (Low, CONFIRMED, lens: deadcode); joint summary row 9 ("Dependency & dead-code hygiene", Code Batch H)
**Original severity:** Low (CONFIRMED) · lens: deadcode · joint-summary priority M
**Claim:** Four assignment-handoff/chapter-kickoff notification builder functions plus four orphaned NOTIFICATION_TYPES constants (~150 lines) have had zero callers anywhere in the codebase since the assignment-workflow feature was removed, yet remain exported so the file reads as if a working handoff-notification feature exists.
**Current location:** `server/services/notifications.js:360 (also :393, :432, :461 — functions; :829-832 — exports; :35-38 — constants)`
**Register item:** P3 · item 22 · Batch 9 — "undeclared glob · dead-code · SR-OOS-1 · absorbs B4-F6" (docs/plans/2026-07-21-post-item17-followup-campaign.md, P3 list). This is the live campaign's fold-in of the predecessor register's Phase-5 item 22 ("Batch 9 (undeclared glob first, then dead-code deletion; SR-OOS-1 archiver skew)", docs/plans/2026-07-11-pre-semester-coding-campaign.md:240), which is the joint summary's "Code Batch H (findings 9, 32, 33, 34, 35, 36)" — finding 33 is named in that batch and the batch text says "Rest is deletion: dead notification functions…".
**Evidence:**

```
All four functions still exist and are still exported: `async function notifyAssignmentCreated(assignment, assignee, assignedByUsername) {` (:360), `async function notifyHandoff(` (:393), `async function notifyStageCompleted(assignment, admin, completedByUsername) {` (:432), `async function notifyChapterKickoff(book, chapter, assignments, kickedOffByUsername) {` (:461); module.exports carries `  // Hand-off notifications` / `  notifyAssignmentCreated,` / `  notifyHandoff,` / `  notifyStageCompleted,` / `  notifyChapterKickoff,` (:828-832). Repo-wide grep for all four names (`grep -rn 'notifyAssignmentCreated\|notifyHandoff\|notifyStageCompleted\|notifyChapterKickoff' --include=*.js --include=*.html --include=*.json . | grep -v node_modules`) returns ONLY those 8 hits inside notifications.js itself — zero callers in routes/, services/, scripts/, tools/, server/public/, or any test. The four type constants are likewise still declared and referenced only from inside the four dead functions: `  ASSIGNMENT_CREATED: 'assignment_created',` / `  ASSIGNMENT_HANDOFF: 'assignment_handoff',` / `  STAGE_COMPLETED: 'stage_completed',` / `  CHAPTER_KICKOFF: 'chapter_kickoff',` (:35-38), used at `type: NOTIFICATION_TYPES.ASSIGNMENT_CREATED,` (:369), `:408`, `:438`, `:475` — all four sites are inside the dead functions. `git log --since=2026-07-10 -- server/services/notifications.js` shows exactly one commit, `8530eda4 refactor(db): notifications + localizationLog lazy-open with _setTestDb seam` — a lazy-DB refactor (campaign Batch 4 / PR #271), not a dead-code removal.
```
**Notes:** Re-located by symbol, not line: the review cited :373 (inside notifyAssignmentCreated's body); the function now starts at :360 — pure drift, the code is byte-for-byte the same feature. The second pass's correction holds: there are exactly four orphaned type constants, not three. Two nuances worth recording, neither of which changes the disposition: (1) the four constants' STRING VALUES do survive outside the dead functions, in `NOTIFICATION_CATEGORIES.assignments` (:71, `types: ['assignment_created','assignment_handoff','stage_completed','chapter_kickoff']`), which IS live — `server/routes/notifications.js:110/136` serves it as the preferences UI's category list, so an editor can today toggle an "Úthlutanir" notification category that no code path can ever fire. That is precisely the review's own "unless old notification records still need those type values … keep just the constants" caveat, so the deleter must check `notifications.type` rows on prod before dropping the category. (2) The assignment-notification FEATURE was not removed, it was re-implemented inline elsewhere: `server/routes/sections.js:118/201/448` call `notifications.createNotification({ type: 'assignment' | 'approval', … })` directly with off-enum type strings — the register already tracks that as B1-F9 (pre-semester campaign, preference-bypass on off-enum types). So the four helpers are dead code that a live, differently-spelled code path shadows; whoever lands Batch 9 should resolve B1-F9 and finding 33 together rather than deleting the constants first. NEW observation (out of scope, not a ledger row): `server/__tests__/viewRouteContracts.test.js:45` contains `expect(src).not.toMatch(/assignment_created:/)` — an absence pin on a different file, unrelated to this finding, but a Batch-9 deleter should not mistake it for coverage of notifications.js.


## Group: `analytics-pageview`
### `code-34` — OPEN-TRACKED
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-code-review.md §34 ("34. `server/services/analyticsService.js:290` — A page-view tracking feature is fully built but never turned on"), row 34 of the findings table (line 104); also docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-review-joint-summary.md Batch 9 row ("Code Batch H (findings 9, 32, 33, 34, 35, 36)") and server-code-review.md "Batch H" (line 738).
**Original severity:** Low, CONFIRMED (lens: deadcode)
**Claim:** analyticsService exports an Express page-view tracking middleware plus logging helpers that only it calls, none of which is ever wired into the server — so server-side page views are not actually tracked even though the file and the admin stats dashboard imply they are.
**Current location:** `server/services/analyticsService.js:283 (middleware definition; review's stale pointer was :290) — exported at :332; helpers at :161 logPageView, :176 logChapterView, :190 logSectionView, :205 logError`
**Register item:** P3 · item 22 · Batch 9 — docs/plans/2026-07-21-post-item17-followup-campaign.md:111 "**item 22 · Batch 9** — undeclared glob · dead-code · SR-OOS-1 · absorbs B4-F6." Coverage chain: "Batch 9" = joint-summary row 9 "Dependency & dead-code hygiene ... unused analytics middleware ... | Code Batch H (findings 9, 32, 33, 34, 35, 36)" — finding 34 is named explicitly in that batch. Predecessor register corroborates the same batch: docs/plans/2026-07-11-pre-semester-coding-campaign.md:240 "22. Batch 9 (undeclared `glob` first, then dead-code deletion; SR-OOS-1 archiver skew)."
**Evidence:**

```
Still built and exported, still never activated. server/services/analyticsService.js:280-283 — "/**\n * Express middleware to log page views\n * Use selectively on routes you want to track\n */\nfunction trackingMiddleware(options = {}) {"; :307-311 inside it — "logSectionView(req, trackBook, trackChapter, trackSection);" / "logChapterView(req, trackBook, trackChapter);" / "logPageView(req, trackBook, trackChapter, trackSection);"; :322-333 module.exports includes "logPageView,", "logChapterView,", "logSectionView,", "logError,", "trackingMiddleware,". Repo-wide grep for `trackingMiddleware|logPageView|logChapterView|logSectionView` outside node_modules/books returns ONLY the definitions and exports in analyticsService.js itself — zero callers, including zero in tests (server/__tests__/analyticsService.test.js covers only `logEvent inserts a row and getRecentEvents returns it` and `getStats aggregates by type for the period`). The only analytics wiring in the app is the route mount, not the middleware: server/index.js:84 "const analyticsRoutes = require('./routes/analytics');", :256 "app.post('/api/analytics/event', publicSubmitLimiter);", :258 "app.use('/api/analytics', analyticsRoutes);" — `trackingMiddleware` appears nowhere in index.js. The only code path that writes analytics rows is the public POST endpoint, server/routes/analytics.js:65 "router.post('/event', optionalAuth, (req, res) => {" → :88 "const event = analyticsService.logEvent({".
```
**Notes:** Re-located by SYMBOL, not line: the review's :290 has drifted to :283 (the file is now 335 lines; git log shows one intervening commit, 12dcdb7c "refactor(db): feedbackService + analyticsService lazy-open; analytics gets first tests (design D4/D5)"). That commit is finding 37's fix (the eager DB-open sibling, pinned by server/__tests__/lazyDbOpen.test.js:26 'services/analyticsService'), which the review's own second pass explicitly carved out as "a different bug in the same file" — it did NOT touch the middleware, so it is not evidence for #34 either way. Batch H demonstrably has not been executed: the calibration set pins #9 (undeclared glob), #35 (dead env vars) and #36 (stale decisions.json) as OPEN, and item 22's own register text still names "undeclared glob" as pending work. Two scope-discipline notes, not new rows: (1) grep finds no in-repo caller of POST /api/analytics/event — this is consistent with the review, which attributes that traffic to the reader-facing site (namsbokasafn-vefur, outside this repo); unverifiable from here, and not a widening of the finding. (2) `logError` (:205) has ZERO callers — not even trackingMiddleware calls it — so the dead surface is marginally larger than the review's "helper functions it alone calls"; same shape as the #33 second-pass "four constants, not three" correction.


## Group: `my-work`
### `code-25` — CLOSED
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-code-review.md § "25. server/routes/my-work.js:260 — Overdue-task data can never actually trigger the overdue UI" (ranked table row 25 / orig #30)
**Original severity:** Low, CONFIRMED (lens: contract)
**Claim:** The /api/my-work/today route sends a task due date as a plain string and never emits a quickStats.overdue count, while my-work.html expects a structured {status, daysUntil, formatted} object plus an overdue count — so overdue styling and the "X tasks are overdue" alert banner can never appear.
**Current location:** `server/routes/my-work.js:236 and :283-289; server/views/my-work.html:1243-1300; pin at server/__tests__/viewRouteContracts.test.js:171-184`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
Both sides of the mismatch were deleted (lead decision "F30(+F25) dormant assignment-task branch DELETE"). Route now has no chapter_assignments read and no due/overdue field at all — server/routes/my-work.js:236 `const allTasks = changesRequested;` and :283-289 `quickStats: { totalTasks: allTasks.length, changesRequested: changesRequested.length, pendingReview: pendingSubmissions.length, completedThisWeek, proposedTerms: proposedTerms.length, },` (no `overdue` key, and none was ever consumed). View side gone too: `grep -n 'overdue|dueDate|due_date|stageLabel|STAGE_LABELS|getDueDateText|chapter_assignments' server/views/my-work.html server/routes/my-work.js` → no matches (excluding `user_chapter_assignments`). Absence-pinned at server/__tests__/viewRouteContracts.test.js:171-179 `describe('item16 PR2 — F30+F25: dormant assignment-task branch removed', … expect(src).not.toMatch(/dueDate/); expect(src).not.toMatch(/overdue/i);` and :181-183 pins the surviving live path `expect(view('my-work.html')).toMatch(/verkefni þarfnast lagfæringa/)`.
```
**Notes:** Attribution: commit 172ff5e5 "fix(item16-pr2): F30+F25 — delete dormant chapter_assignments task branch", shipped in item-16 PR2 = PR #304 (main 679f93e2) per docs/plans/2026-07-11-pre-semester-coding-campaign.md:102. Remediated by REMOVAL, not by the review's suggested fix (send the structured object) — the underlying chapter_assignments table has no writer. The "finish" half is registered as a live follow-up: campaign doc line 106, I16-R2 [feature] "surface real user_chapter_assignments … on my-work's personal dashboard" — i.e. if that feature is built, the shape contract must be designed, not restored.
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — Defect unreachable — both sides of the contract are gone, verified independently of the verifier's evidence and with all three legs proven. (1) PRODUCER: server/routes/my-work.js GET /today builds `const allTasks = changesRequested;` (:236) from getUserRecentReviews only — there is no chapter_assignments SELECT anywhere in the file, and the response's `quickStats: { totalTasks, changesRequested, pendingReview, completedThisWeek, proposedTerms }` (:283-289) has no `overdue` key and no task carries a `dueDate`. (2) CONSUMER: `grep -rni 'overdue|dueDate|due_date|tímafrest|chapter_assignments' server/views/my-work.html server/routes/my-work.js server/public/js/` returns ZERO hits — the overdue alert-message branches, the `.meta-due.due-overdue` / `.item-badge.badge-overdue` styling, and the due-date meta arm are all deleted. (3) NO OTHER CONSUMER: `/api/my-work/today` has exactly one caller in the tree (server/views/my-work.html:1112 `todayData = await fetchJson('/api/my-work/today');`), and a repo-wide `grep -rn quickStats` shows the only reads are totalTasks/changesRequested/completedThisWeek/pendingReview/proposedTerms (my-work.html:1125,1141,1174,1177-1179). Deletion is attributable, not inferred: commit 172ff5e5 'fix(item16-pr2): F30+F25 — delete dormant chapter_assignments task branch' removes `dueDate: a.due_date`, `ORDER BY due_date ASC NULLS LAST`, `var overdue = todayData.quickStats.overdue;` and the `' verkefni eru' + ' yfir tíma'` banner strings. Files on this branch are byte-identical to origin/main (`git diff origin/main -- server/views/my-work.html server/routes/my-work.js` empty). The described failure — a shape mismatch leaving overdue UI permanently dead — cannot occur because neither the field nor the UI exists.
- ✅ upheld — Mechanism traced: the failure requires a due-date/overdue UI to exist and be starved of data. Both halves are gone from main (commit 172ff5e5 'F30+F25 — delete dormant chapter_assignments task branch'). Producer: server/routes/my-work.js GET /today now builds `const allTasks = changesRequested;` (:236) and returns `quickStats: { totalTasks, changesRequested, pendingReview, completedThisWeek, proposedTerms }` (:283-289) — no due-date field, no `overdue` key, and no chapter_assignments SELECT anywhere in the file. Consumer: `grep -ni 'overdue|dueDate|due_date|stageLabel|STAGE_LABELS|getDueDateText|chapter_assignments' server/views/my-work.html server/routes/my-work.js` returns ZERO matches — no overdue styling, no 'X tasks are overdue' banner markup, no CSS. I checked for a surviving second consumer: the only fetch of that endpoint is server/views/my-work.html:1112 `todayData = await fetchJson('/api/my-work/today');`, and server-wide `grep -ri overdue server/` leaves only unrelated SLA code (server/routes/segment-editor.js:697 `sla = 'overdue'`) plus absence pins in server/__tests__/viewRouteContracts.test.js:144-153,178. A UI element that no longer exists cannot fail to appear, so the claimed failure is unreachable. Nuance for the ledger: this was resolved by DELETION (the lead's dormant-branch decision), not by the review's suggested repair (send the structured object + add the count) — but either way the defect does not survive on main. Sibling scope: `renderAdminActivity`/`admin-activity`/`teamActivity` appear in NO other view or public/js file, and `activity.color` has exactly one occurrence repo-wide (:1712) — no duplicated feed kept the inverted ternary. Pinned at server/__tests__/viewRouteContracts.test.js:125-140.


## Group: `config-rot`

**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-code-review.md § "35. `server/.env.example:29` — Four documented environment variables are entirely unused" (table row 35, original id 40; also joint-summary batch 9 / code Batch H)

Batch 9 is the joint summary's own batch numbering: docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-review-joint-summary.md:34 "| 9 | Dependency & dead-code hygiene | … Rest is deletion: dead notification functions, unused analytics middleware, **4 dead env vars**, 2 stale files, stray strings. | M | Code Batch H (findings 9, 32, 33, 34, 35, 36) |". Predecessor register: docs/plans/2026-07-11-pre-semester-coding-campaign.md:240 (Phase 5) "22. Batch 9 (undeclared `glob` first, then dead-code deletion; SR-OOS-1 archiver skew)."

Repo-wide grep (excluding node_modules and .env.example) for each name across *.js/*.cjs/*.mjs/*.sh/*.json/*.yml returns ZERO hits for GITHUB_REPO_OWNER, GITHUB_REPO_NAME, GITHUB_BASE_BRANCH, MATECAT_API_KEY, MATECAT_BASE_URL — no `process.env.<name>` reader exists.
The "one nearby variable" the finding says is still live is confirmed live: tools/download-source.js:288 `const token = process.env.GITHUB_BOT_TOKEN;` (also :285, :310-311, :325) — and its .env.example comment is still the stale/misleading one the fix asked to correct ("Optional: Bot token for automated PR creation", whereas the real use is raising the OpenStax GitHub API rate limit from 60 to 5000 req/h, per download-source.js:310-311).

Count discrepancy inside the review itself (not a code question): the finding's title/table say "four", its body enumerates "three [GitHub] … and two [Matecat] … None of the five", and its second-pass says "the four flagged as dead". Empirically there are FIVE reader-less names (3 GitHub + 2 Matecat); two of the five (MATECAT_*) are already commented out, which is the likeliest origin of the 4-vs-5 slip.
