### `code-36` — OPEN-TRACKED
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-code-review.md § "36. `server/data/decisions.json:1` — Two stale placeholder files are still committed and processed at every boot" (table row 36, original id 41; also joint-summary batch 9 / code Batch H)
**Original severity:** (unrated)
**Claim:** server/data/decisions.json — a file containing nothing but an empty list — is still committed and is read and parsed by the server's data loader at every boot (and on some routine requests), only to be discarded because it lacks the field the loader looks for; the consistency-checking script has to skip it by name; and a second stale file, an empty zero-byte database placeholder with a name similar to the real DB, sits beside it and is still referenced in the architecture docs.
**Current location:** `server/data/decisions.json:1 (3 bytes, content `[]`) and server/data/workflow.db (0 bytes); loader at server/services/bookDataLoader.js:14-26; skip-by-name at tools/validate-pipeline-consistency.js:37 and :226; stale doc reference at docs/technical/architecture.md:377`
**Register item:** P3 · item 22 · Batch 9 — docs/plans/2026-07-21-post-item17-followup-campaign.md:111 "**item 22 · Batch 9** — undeclared glob · dead-code · SR-OOS-1 · absorbs B4-F6.", where Batch 9 = docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-review-joint-summary.md:34 "| 9 | Dependency & dead-code hygiene | … 4 dead env vars, **2 stale files**, stray strings. | M | Code Batch H (findings 9, 32, 33, 34, 35, 36) |". Predecessor: docs/plans/2026-07-11-pre-semester-coding-campaign.md:240 item 22. Independently corroborated as OPEN in docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-26-closure-audit-design.md:30 "| #36 stale `server/data/decisions.json` | 9 | **OPEN** (3 bytes, still committed) |".
**Evidence:**

```
Both files are still present AND still git-tracked — `git ls-files server/data/` lists `server/data/decisions.json` and `server/data/workflow.db`; `ls -la` shows `decisions.json` = 3 bytes and `workflow.db` = 0 bytes; `cat server/data/decisions.json` → `[]`.
Still read-and-discarded at boot — server/services/bookDataLoader.js:14-26:
  14: const dataDir = path.join(__dirname, '..', 'data');
  17: for (const file of fs.readdirSync(dataDir).filter((f) => f.endsWith('.json'))) {
  19:     const data = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(path.join(dataDir, file), 'utf8'));
  20:     if (data.slug) {
  21:       cache[data.slug] = data;
The filter is `.endsWith('.json')` with no exclusion, so decisions.json is read + JSON.parsed, then dropped because an array has no `.slug`. The loop is at module scope ("Builds a slug-indexed cache at require time", file header line 7), and the module is required at boot: server/routes/segment-editor.js:64 and server/routes/localization-editor.js:74 both `require('../services/bookDataLoader')`, and server/index.js:62-63 requires both routers. So: boot still processes it.
The skip-by-name the finding cites still exists TWICE — tools/validate-pipeline-consistency.js:37 and :226, both identical:
    .filter((f) => f.endsWith('.json') && f !== 'decisions.json');
The stale architecture reference is also still there — docs/technical/architecture.md:377: `DATABASE_PATH=./data/workflow.db`.
Git history shows no remediation touch: `git log --oneline -- server/data/decisions.json` → single commit 36ad779c; `-- server/data/workflow.db` → single commit da8090c0. Both pre-date the review.
```
**Notes:** Matches the hand-verified calibration (OPEN). Every one of the finding's four sub-claims independently re-verified present today: (1) decisions.json committed, (2) parsed-and-discarded at boot by bookDataLoader.js, (3) two by-name exclusions in validate-pipeline-consistency.js, (4) zero-byte workflow.db + its stale architecture.md:377 reference.
The second stale file the brief asked me to identify is server/data/workflow.db (0 bytes, tracked). Nothing in the tree reads `workflow.db`: the only reference outside the audit docs is the architecture.md:377 line, and the real DB path resolves through server/lib/dbPath.js / SESSIONS_DB_PATH (pipeline-output/sessions.db) — consistent with the finding's "could plausibly confuse someone into thinking it's the actual database".
Caveat on the finding's own wording, carried forward for whoever executes Batch 9: the OTHER readdir of server/data/ — tools/validate-pipeline-consistency.js loadBookCatalog:33-40 — would THROW on a non-book JSON rather than discard it, which is why the by-name skip exists there; deleting decisions.json makes both exclusions removable exactly as the fix describes. The finding's prerequisite "after confirming the production server doesn't depend on either one being present" is not checkable from this box (prod filesystem) — but nothing in the source tree reads either file's contents meaningfully, so the risk is a readdir of a then-absent file, not a missing datum.


## Group: `segment-editor-service`
### `code-15` — CLOSED
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-code-review.md §"15. server/services/segmentEditorService.js:697 — Same-second approvals can unpredictably discard the newer edit" (summary table row 15; also cross-listed at line 702 as a Low/CONFIRMED integrity finding)
**Original severity:** Low (downgraded from Medium), CONFIRMED — lens: integrity
**Claim:** applyApprovedEdits picked the winning approved edit with `ORDER BY reviewed_at DESC` and no id tie-break; since SQLite CURRENT_TIMESTAMP is 1-second granular, two same-second approvals for the same segment resolved arbitrarily and the loser was permanently marked superseded — and because the preview path (buildEffectiveSegments) already broke ties by id, preview and publish could disagree about which edit won.
**Current location:** `server/services/segmentEditorService.js:966 (+ server/lib/editRecency.js:26, server/services/segmentEditorService.js:271)`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
The `ORDER BY reviewed_at DESC` winner-selection is GONE from segmentEditorService.js — `grep -n "reviewed_at DESC" server/services/segmentEditorService.js` returns nothing (the only two survivors repo-wide are `dashboardReadModel.js:124` and `routes/my-work.js:117`, and BOTH already read `ORDER BY reviewed_at DESC, id DESC`). Winner selection is now a shared comparator with an explicit id tie-break. server/lib/editRecency.js:20-27 — `function isNewer(a, b) { const ta = a.created_at || ''; const tb = b.created_at || ''; ... if (ta !== tb) return ta > tb; return a.id > b.id; }` with the file header stating "Newest created_at wins; id breaks same-second ties (CURRENT_TIMESTAMP is 1s-granular)." segmentEditorService.js:956-973 — "// 2. Winner per segment = canonical newest (created_at, id) — the same // rule buildEffectiveSegments uses." then `const winner = pickLatest(list); approvedLookup[segId] = winner; for (const edit of list) { if (edit.id !== winner.id && edit.applied_at === null) { supersededIds.push(edit.id); } }`. The preview side uses the identical comparator — segmentEditorService.js:271 `if (!cur || isNewer(e, cur)) latestBySeg[e.segment_id] = e;` — so the preview/publish divergence the review flagged is closed by the same change. The residual `reviewed_at`-free pre-check query at :839-844 is explicitly documented as ordering-irrelevant: "// Pre-check: any approved edits at all? (ordering is dead weight now that // winners are chosen by pickLatest inside the transaction — this query is // only ever used for its .length)".
```
**Notes:** ATTRIBUTION (all verified ancestors of main via `git merge-base --is-ancestor`): 65e04167 "fix(item12): same-second approval ties break by id, matching preview (F15)" closed the same-second tie itself; 3bd2a226 "feat(item13): shared edit-recency comparator (newest saved wins)" extracted server/lib/editRecency.js; 70f97c51 "fix(item13): preview winner uses shared recency comparator"; 7cb7cc8e "fix(item13): convergent apply — newest approved wins across applies (I12-R5)" removed the reviewed_at ordering from apply entirely. The register (docs/plans/2026-07-11-pre-semester-coding-campaign.md:127, item I12-R5) independently confirms this history: "the F15 fix closed only the same-second tie" — and I12-R5, the cross-second successor defect, is itself recorded closed at line 99 (item 13, PR #299, main 278443dd). THE SHIPPED FIX DIVERGES UPWARD FROM THE RECOMMENDED ONE: the review asked only for "a secondary sort by edit ID" on the reviewed_at ordering; what shipped instead ranks by (created_at, id) — deliberately NOT reviewed_at, because the header comment notes pending rows are UPDATEd in place so created_at refreshes while id does not. Both places the review named (the pre-check query and the in-transaction query) are covered. The harm mechanism is additionally narrowed: :969 now supersedes only losers with `applied_at === null`, so already-applied losers survive as resolved history rather than being marked superseded.
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — I hunted the defect independently and it is not reachable. The described failure needs (a) a winner query with no id tie-break and (b) a preview path that tie-breaks differently. Both legs are gone.

(a) The winner selection inside applyApprovedEdits' IMMEDIATE transaction (server/services/segmentEditorService.js:1030-1032 region, the `allApproved` query at ~:1032) is `SELECT id, segment_id, edited_content, created_at, applied_at FROM segment_edits WHERE book = ? AND module_id = ? AND status = 'approved'` — no ORDER BY at all — followed by `const winner = pickLatest(list); approvedLookup[segId] = winner;` and `if (edit.id !== winner.id && edit.applied_at === null) supersededIds.push(edit.id);` (segmentEditorService.js:966-973). The critical discriminator I checked: `created_at` IS in the SELECT list, so pickLatest does not silently degenerate to id-only. `pickLatest`/`isNewer` (server/lib/editRecency.js:20-27) is `if (ta !== tb) return ta > tb; return a.id > b.id;` — an explicit id tie-break for the 1-second-granular CURRENT_TIMESTAMP, documented as such in the file header (:7). Two same-second approvals therefore resolve deterministically, not arbitrarily.

(b) The preview path uses the SAME comparator, not a private one: buildEffectiveSegments at segmentEditorService.js:271 is `if (!cur || isNewer(e, cur)) latestBySeg[e.segment_id] = e;`, importing isNewer from the same lib (:25). Its input getModuleEdits (:242) is `SELECT * FROM segment_edits ...`, so created_at is present there too. Preview and publish cannot disagree on the tie-break.

I verified no other winner-picker survives in the publish chain: `grep -n "ORDER BY" server/services/segmentEditorService.js` returns 8 hits, none of them a winner selector — :65 is checkEditConflict's conflict probe, :249/:357/:1320 are display lists, :667/:685/:795/:1281 are queue orderings. `reviewed_at DESC` does not appear in the file at all; the two repo-wide survivors (dashboardReadModel.js:124, routes/my-work.js:117) already read `ORDER BY reviewed_at DESC, id DESC` and are read-only listings, not publish. Reinforcing (not load-bearing): approveEdit at :410-414 now refuses the setup up front with `SUPERSEDED_BY_NEWER` when `.some((e) => isNewer(e, edit))` over already-approved rows. Attribution: `pickLatest` entered via 7cb7cc8e/70f97c51, lib added in 3bd2a226.
- ✅ upheld — Scope attack fails: every winner-selection site for segment_edits now shares ONE total order with an explicit id tie-break, so the described nondeterminism is structurally impossible. (a) Apply path, server/services/segmentEditorService.js:956-973: 'const winner = pickLatest(list); approvedLookup[segId] = winner; for (const edit of list) { if (edit.id !== winner.id && edit.applied_at === null) supersededIds.push(edit.id); }' — and the feeding query at :846-852 ('SELECT id, segment_id, edited_content, created_at, applied_at FROM segment_edits WHERE ... status = approved') carries NO ORDER BY at all, so row order can no longer influence the outcome. (b) Preview path, :271: 'if (!cur || isNewer(e, cur)) latestBySeg[e.segment_id] = e;' inside buildEffectiveSegments — identical comparator, so preview and publish cannot disagree about the winner. (c) The comparator, server/lib/editRecency.js:20-27: 'if (ta !== tb) return ta > tb; return a.id > b.id;' — id is unique, so a same-second pair has exactly one deterministic winner. Sibling sites are converted too, not just the two named: approve-time guard segmentEditorService.js:414 '.some((e) => isNewer(e, edit))'; review-status sidecar acceptanceService.js:396 'pickLatest(appliedEditsBySeg[seg.segmentId])'; editor-UI ordering routes/segment-editor.js:293 'sort((a, b) => (isNewer(a, b) ? -1 : isNewer(b, a) ? 1 : 0))'; localization parity localizationReviewService.js:231. A repo-wide reviewed_at sweep (tests/e2e/migrations excluded) leaves exactly two 'ORDER BY reviewed_at DESC' survivors — dashboardReadModel.js:124 and routes/my-work.js:117 — and both read 'ORDER BY reviewed_at DESC, id DESC' and are list/feed reads that select no winner. The remaining pre-check at :839-844 selects only id and is used solely for .length. The one un-tie-broken query left, getSegmentEdits (:351, 'ORDER BY created_at DESC'), has ZERO callers repo-wide outside its own module.exports, so it cannot surface a divergent winner in any UI. Attribution: commits 70f97c51 and 7cb7cc8e (item 13) plus 3bd2a226 which introduced server/lib/editRecency.js.
- ✅ upheld — Genuinely CLOSED. The described failure requires an undefined ordering between two same-second approved-unapplied edits; that step cannot occur now. server/lib/editRecency.js:20-27 isNewer compares created_at lexicographically and on equality falls through to `return a.id > b.id` — id is a unique PK, so the comparator is a TOTAL order (even all-NULL timestamps degrade to the id tie-break via `a.created_at || ''`). Both paths consume it from SELECTs that carry both keys: apply at segmentEditorService.js:951-955 (`SELECT id, segment_id, edited_content, created_at, applied_at`) → :966 `const winner = pickLatest(list)` with losers superseded only when `edit.id !== winner.id && edit.applied_at === null`; preview at :271 `if (!cur || isNewer(e, cur)) latestBySeg[e.segment_id] = e` fed by getModuleEdits' `SELECT *`. So both preview and publish pick the SAME winner among approved rows — the specific divergence the review described (publish by reviewed_at, preview by id) is gone. `grep -rn "reviewed_at DESC" server/ --include=*.js` (non-test) leaves only dashboardReadModel.js:124 and routes/my-work.js:117, both already `ORDER BY reviewed_at DESC, id DESC`; the apply-path ordering was removed in 7cb7cc8e. acceptanceService.js:395 also uses pickLatest, so the sidecar agrees. Two adjacent-but-different mechanisms do NOT rescue the claim: preview still includes pending/discuss edits (documented deliberate at :256-262 — a different divergence than the tie-break), and the reviewed_at→created_at key change alters WHICH edit wins (deliberate, I12-R5) rather than WHETHER the outcome is deterministic.

### `code-18` — CLOSED
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-code-review.md §"18. server/services/segmentEditorService.js:1090 — A progress formula double-counts already-applied work" (summary table row 18; also in the batch listed at line 726)
**Original severity:** Low, CONFIRMED — lens: integrity
**Claim:** Module-completion progress computed `(modEdits.approved || 0) + (modEdits.applied || 0)`, but `applied` is a strict SQL subset of `approved` (applying stamps applied_at without changing status), so every applied segment was counted twice and half-reviewed modules could report as fully reviewed; the identical formula appeared at a second site driving a visible "complete" indicator.
**Current location:** `server/services/segmentEditorService.js:1455 (+ server/routes/status.js:999)`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
Site 1 (the named one, formerly segmentEditorService.js:1090): the addition is gone. segmentEditorService.js:1452-1458 — "// Module is \"complete\" when reviewed (approved ∪ accepted) segments // cover the segment count" then `const reviewedCount = reviewedByModule[mod.moduleId] || 0; if (segCount > 0 && reviewedCount >= segCount) { modulesComplete++; }`. The count is now a DISTINCT-segment UNION, structurally incapable of double-counting — segmentEditorService.js:1363-1380 `getReviewedSegmentsByModule`: `SELECT module_id, COUNT(DISTINCT segment_id) as reviewed FROM ( SELECT module_id, segment_id FROM segment_edits WHERE book = ? AND status = 'approved' UNION SELECT module_id, segment_id FROM segment_acceptances WHERE book = ? AND status = 'active' ) GROUP BY module_id`. Site 2 (the review's "second place", formerly routes/status.js:974 `const approved = edits ? edits.approved + edits.applied : 0;`): also gone — routes/status.js:991 now reads `const approved = edits ? edits.approved : 0;` and the completion test at :999-1003 is `const reviewed = reviewedByModule[mod.moduleId] || 0; ... if (reviewed >= segCount && segCount > 0) { status = 'complete'; ... }`, fed by :953 `const reviewedByModule = segmentEditorService.getReviewedSegmentsByModule(book);`. The review's premise that applied ⊂ approved is confirmed still true in the current SQL — getBookEditsByModule at :1340/:1343 defines `COUNT(CASE WHEN status = 'approved' THEN 1 END) as approved` and `COUNT(CASE WHEN status = 'approved' AND applied_at IS NOT NULL THEN 1 END) as applied`. A repo-wide sweep `grep -rnE "approved[^\n]*\+[^\n]*applied|applied[^\n]*\+[^\n]*approved" server/ --include=*.js --include=*.html` (tests/e2e excluded) returns only three PROSE COMMENTS (localizationReviewService.js:261, public/js/segment-editor.js:820, acceptanceService.js:352) — no surviving third instance of the formula.
```
**Notes:** ATTRIBUTION: f3450ed9 "fix(server): progress formulas stop double-counting applied segments (F18, item 16 PR1)" — verified an ancestor of main; `git log -S` on BOTH literal formulas (`(modEdits.approved || 0) + (modEdits.applied || 0)` and `edits.approved + edits.applied`) returns that same commit as the remover, i.e. one commit closed both sites. The register corroborates: docs/plans/2026-07-11-pre-semester-coding-campaign.md:102 (item 16 PR1 = PR #303, main a1fb853f) lists F18 among the seven mechanical contract repairs and flags "⚠️ F18 fix makes dashboard completion numbers DROP (double-count removed — intended)." The form was later superseded again by item 20b's getReviewedSegmentsByModule UNION (raising the counts back — MTA-R1 comms rider at register line 210), but the double-count itself never returned. OUT-OF-SCOPE OBSERVATION (note only, deliberately not a ledger row): the replacement test `reviewedCount >= segCount` compares DB segment_ids against an on-disk segment count, so approved edits referencing segment ids that no longer exist in the current extraction still count toward "complete" — the code warns about exactly that row class at segmentEditorService.js:977-983. Different mechanism from the claimed double-count; noted for a future review, not audited here.
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — I searched for the additive formula rather than for the fix, including the SQL that a JS regex sweep cannot see, and it does not survive anywhere.

Site 1 (getEditorialProgress, formerly `const approvedRecords = (modEdits.approved || 0) + (modEdits.applied || 0)`): the symbols `modEdits` and `approvedRecords` no longer exist anywhere in server/ (grep returns nothing; `git log -S"approvedRecords"` shows removal in 7109f302 "item20b: metrics redefinition"). Completion is now segmentEditorService.js:1455-1458 `const reviewedCount = reviewedByModule[mod.moduleId] || 0; if (segCount > 0 && reviewedCount >= segCount) { modulesComplete++; }`, fed by getReviewedSegmentsByModule (:1363-1380) `SELECT module_id, COUNT(DISTINCT segment_id) as reviewed FROM ( SELECT module_id, segment_id FROM segment_edits WHERE book = ? AND status = 'approved' UNION SELECT module_id, segment_id FROM segment_acceptances WHERE book = ? AND status = 'active' ) GROUP BY module_id` — a DISTINCT over a deduping UNION, structurally incapable of counting an applied segment twice.

Site 2 (routes/status.js, formerly `const approved = edits ? edits.approved + edits.applied : 0`): now routes/status.js:991 `const approved = edits ? edits.approved : 0;` and that value only feeds the displayed `segmentsApproved`; the completion test at :999-1003 is `const reviewed = reviewedByModule[mod.moduleId] || 0; if (reviewed >= segCount && segCount > 0) { status = 'complete'; ... }` from the same getReviewedSegmentsByModule call at :953.

The premise is still true (so this could not have been closed by redefinition): getBookEditsByModule (:1340,:1343) defines `approved` as `COUNT(CASE WHEN status = 'approved' THEN 1 END)` and `applied` as `COUNT(CASE WHEN status = 'approved' AND applied_at IS NOT NULL THEN 1 END)` — still a strict subset — yet no caller adds them.

On the advisor's flag, I read the block I had skipped (segmentEditorService.js:1402-1422), where `editMap` / `approved_segments` — the numbers behind percentComplete and summary.approvedSegments — is built. Its `UNION ALL` is not a double-count: the two branches are disjoint by construction (`COUNT(DISTINCT segment_id) as edited, 0 as reviewed` vs `0 as edited, COUNT(DISTINCT segment_id) as reviewed`) and summed with SUM per chapter, and the reviewed branch's inner edits∪acceptances join is a deduping `UNION`, not UNION ALL. Sweep `grep -rnE "\.approved[^a-zA-Z_]*\+|\+[^a-zA-Z_]*\.applied|approved \+ applied" server/ tools/ scripts/` returns only prose comments (segment-editor.js:820, localizationReviewService.js:261) and an unrelated `stats.approved++` in tools/merge-glossary.js.
- ✅ upheld — Scope attack fails at both named sites and on a wider sweep than the verifier ran. Site 1 (segmentEditorService, formerly :1090): the addition is gone — :1452-1457 'const reviewedCount = reviewedByModule[mod.moduleId] || 0; if (segCount > 0 && reviewedCount >= segCount) { modulesComplete++; }', fed by :1441 'const reviewedByModule = getReviewedSegmentsByModule(book);'. That source (:1363-1380) is 'SELECT module_id, COUNT(DISTINCT segment_id) as reviewed FROM ( SELECT module_id, segment_id FROM segment_edits WHERE book = ? AND status = approved UNION SELECT module_id, segment_id FROM segment_acceptances WHERE book = ? AND status = active ) GROUP BY module_id' — a DISTINCT UNION over disjoint tables, arithmetically incapable of counting an applied segment twice. Site 2 (routes/status.js, formerly :974): now :991 'const approved = edits ? edits.approved : 0;' with the completion test at :998-1003 'const reviewed = reviewedByModule[mod.moduleId] || 0; ... if (reviewed >= segCount && segCount > 0) { status = complete; chModulesComplete++; }', fed by :953 from the same UNION helper. The review's premise still holds in current SQL (getBookEditsByModule :1340/:1343 define approved as status='approved' and applied as status='approved' AND applied_at IS NOT NULL), so the fix had to be the removal, and it is. Wider sweep for a third site: only two '>= segCount' completion tests exist repo-wide (segmentEditorService.js:1456, routes/status.js:1001), both converted; a non-line-anchored regex for approved/applied addition across server/routes, server/services, server/views, server/public, tools/ and scripts/ (the verifier swept only server/) returns four hits, all PROSE COMMENTS (acceptanceService.js:352, localizationReviewService.js:261, public/js/segment-editor.js:820, scripts/backfill-mt-locks.js:13). Every other applied_at aggregate is either disjoint-union arithmetic (public/js/segment-editor.js:2149 'unapplied_count + unapplied_acceptances', two different tables) or non-additive (getApplyStatus :1157-1162, getGlobalEditStats :1302). Out-of-scope observation, not part of this claim: routes/status.js:1027 still reports per-module 'segmentsApproved: approved' as an edit-RECORD count while views/status.html:597 divides it by segmentCount, so a segment with two approved edits can render >100% — a distinct defect from the approved+applied double-count and not something this finding covered.
- ✅ upheld — Genuinely CLOSED. Traced the review's own scenario through today's code and it dies at the first step: 10 segments, 5 approved+applied, 5 pending → segmentEditorService.js:1363-1380 getReviewedSegmentsByModule returns `COUNT(DISTINCT segment_id)` over `segment_edits status='approved' UNION segment_acceptances status='active'` = 5, and :1456 `if (segCount > 0 && reviewedCount >= segCount)` evaluates 5>=10 = false, so modulesComplete is not incremented. Site 2 reads the same UNION: routes/status.js:953 `const reviewedByModule = segmentEditorService.getReviewedSegmentsByModule(book);` → :999 `if (reviewed >= segCount && segCount > 0) { status = 'complete'; }`. The review's premise applied ⊂ approved is still true in the current SQL (getBookEditsByModule :1340 `COUNT(CASE WHEN status='approved' THEN 1 END) as approved` vs :1343 `COUNT(CASE WHEN status='approved' AND applied_at IS NOT NULL THEN 1 END) as applied`), so the fix is not premise-drift. `git log -S` pins removal of BOTH exact expressions to f3450ed9 ('progress formulas stop double-counting applied segments (F18, item 16 PR1)': `-const approved = edits ? edits.approved + edits.applied : 0;` and `-const approvedRecords = (modEdits.approved || 0) + (modEdits.applied || 0);`), later strengthened to the DISTINCT UNION by item 20b. No third instance survives repo-wide. The surviving edit-record count `edits.approved` at routes/status.js:991 feeds only the display field `segmentsApproved`; percentages come from getEditorialProgress's DISTINCT counts, so it is not a second completion gate.


## Group: `segment-editor-client-strings`
### `code-32` — OPEN-TRACKED
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-code-review.md §32 (`server/public/js/segment-editor.js:1879` — "Several editor messages bypass the shared Icelandic-text file"); summary table row 32/37; Batch H in "Batch recommendations"
**Original severity:** Low, CONFIRMED (lens: contract)
**Claim:** Four categories of editor-facing Icelandic text in segment-editor.js are hardcoded instead of coming from the shared server/public/js/ui-strings.js file — the "file is missing, can be rebuilt" apply-status message (sitting between two branches that do use UI.*), the preview loading/ready badges (written with escaped unicode so a plain-text search for the Icelandic wording misses them), the 409 conflict-error fallback text, and the entire version-history dialog's wording — and the existing ui-strings test only checks that referenced UI.* keys resolve, so it can never catch this.
**Current location:** `server/public/js/segment-editor.js:2161 (primary anchor; finding spans four sites — see evidence). Review's `:1879` is stale line drift.`
**Register item:** P3 · item 22 · Batch 9 — docs/plans/2026-07-21-post-item17-followup-campaign.md:111 `- **item 22 · Batch 9** — undeclared glob · dead-code · SR-OOS-1 · absorbs B4-F6.` The batch-number→finding-number chain is documented, not inferred: that item cites "Batch 9", defined in the predecessor register docs/plans/2026-07-11-pre-semester-coding-campaign.md:240 (Phase 5 — hygiene) as `22. Batch 9 (undeclared glob first, then dead-code deletion; SR-OOS-1 archiver skew)`, which in turn is joint-summary row 9: docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-review-joint-summary.md:34 `| 9 | Dependency & dead-code hygiene | ... 4 dead env vars, 2 stale files, stray strings. | M | Code Batch H (findings 9, 32, 33, 34, 35, 36) |` — naming finding 32 explicitly and calling out "stray strings".
**Evidence:**

```
All four categories are present verbatim on main (9107ed1d). (1) REBUILD MESSAGE — segment-editor.js:2161 `statusEl.textContent = 'Skrá vantar — hægt að endurbyggja úr samþykktum breytingum';` sitting literally between branches that DO use the shared file: :2153-2155 `UI.apply.unappliedCombined(...)` / `UI.apply.unapplied(unapplied_count)` and :2164 `statusEl.textContent = UI.apply.allApplied(total_approved);`. (2) PREVIEW BADGES — :2070 `badge.textContent = 'Hleður...';` and :2091 `badge.textContent = 'Opnað';` (escaped-unicode, exactly the "a text search for the Icelandic wording would miss" phrasing), plus :2089 `win.document.title = 'Forskoðun: ' + currentModuleId;` — while the shared file already defines the identical string twice: ui-strings.js:19 `loading: 'Hleður...',` (common) and ui-strings.js:174 `loading: 'Hleður...',` (apply). (3) CONFLICT FALLBACK — :1419 `alert(err.message || 'Einingin hefur verið breytt af öðrum notanda. Endurhleð...');` while ui-strings.js:61-64 defines `conflictReload: 'Einingu hefur verið breytt af öðrum notanda.\n' + 'Endurhlaða til að sjá nýjustu útgáfu?\n\n' + ...`. (4) VERSION-HISTORY DIALOG — wholly inline: :2322 `list.innerHTML = '<div class="vh-empty">Hleður…</div>';`, :2333 `'<div class="vh-empty">Engar eldri útgáfur. Útgáfur verða til þegar samþykktar breytingar eru vistaðar í skrár.</div>'`, :2361 `'">Færa í þessa útgáfu</button>'`, :2380-2382 `'Færa þessa einingu aftur í útgáfu ' + version + '?\n\nNúverandi efni er fyrst vistað sem ný útgáfa, svo þetta er afturkræft.'`, :2399-2401 `'Fært í útgáfu ' + version + ' (núverandi efni vistað sem útgáfa ' + result.snapshotVersion + ')'`, and the catch at :2374-2375 `'<div class="vh-empty">Villa við að sækja sögu: ' + escapeHtml(err.message) + '</div>'`. TEST CLAIM STILL HOLDS — server/__tests__/ui-strings.test.js:69-79 asserts only `all UI.* references in ${file} have matching definitions` (`for (const ref of refs) { if (!definedKeys.has(ref)) missing.push(...) } expect(missing).toEqual([]);`), i.e. key-existence in the direction that cannot detect a bypass. And no later-added static-pin suite covers these bytes: `grep -rn "endurbyggja úr samþykktum|Hle\\u00f0ur|Opna\\u00f0|öðrum notanda. Endurhleð|Færa í þessa útgáfu|vh-empty" server/__tests__ server/e2e` returns ZERO matches.
```
**Notes:** Attribution: no closing commit exists. `git log -S"hægt að endurbyggja úr samþykktum" -- server/public/js/segment-editor.js` returns only ac5b9c27 "feat(editor): rebuild affordance + header de-jargon (Unit 4 partial)" — that is the commit that INTRODUCED the hardcoded string (origin, not closure). The finding has slightly STRENGTHENED since the baseline: a later-added accept-path conflict handler at segment-editor.js:1094 `alert(err.message || UI.acceptance.conflict);` does use the shared file, making the untouched :1419 hardcode an inconsistency within the same file rather than a uniform omission. Per the review's own second-pass note the four categories are "representative … rather than an exhaustive list", so other inline strings in the same file are in-class but out of this row's scope (scope discipline). Adjacent but DISTINCT register item, not this claim: I18-R4 (pre-semester campaign :162) covers `SUBJECT_NAMES` being hardcoded client-side in segment-editor.js — a data-map drift issue, not the ui-strings bypass. Register shorthand caveat: the live P3 line says only "undeclared glob · dead-code", so a reader of that line alone would not see finding 32; the coverage rests on the batch reference resolving through the joint summary (quoted above). Calibration consistency: findings #9/#35/#36 are members of the same Batch H/Batch 9 and are hand-verified OPEN, matching this row.


## Group: `refuted-four`
### `code-ref-1` — CLOSED-BY-REFUTATION
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-code-review.md § "Refuted on second-pass verification" → `server/services/publicationService.js:247`
**Original severity:** Medium, CONFIRMED (failloud)
**Claim:** Original claim (refuted): a refused pipeline-stage transition is mistaken for a "database unavailable" condition, and the fallback then writes `complete: true` into the on-disk status file, turning a legitimate refusal into a recorded false success. The review refuted it on two legs: (a) the catch is not silent — it logs at error level through the standard logger with the underlying error and the affected publication stage; (b) the fallback only runs when the render job already finished successfully.
**Current location:** `server/services/publicationService.js:235, :248-259 (inside `publishChapter`'s `promise.then()`)`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
Leg (b), guard still gates the fallback — `publicationService.js:235`: `if (job && job.status === 'completed') {`. Leg (a), catch still logs at error level with err + stage — `:248-249`: `// Fall back to direct status.json write if DB transition fails` / `log.error({ err, pubStage }, 'Pipeline status transition failed');`, and only then `:250-258`: `updateChapterStatus(bookSlug, chapterNum, 'publication', { [trackKey]: { complete: true, date: ..., pipeline: 'html', moduleCount: readiness.moduleCount, }, });`
```
**Notes:** Both legs of the refutation re-confirmed verbatim against current main; the block is structurally unchanged since the review baseline. `git log --since=2026-07-10 -- server/services/publicationService.js` shows only appendix read-path work (21559111 "fix(publication): read-path accepts appendices"), nothing touching this catch. No disposition change.
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — Both legs of the refutation still hold at their current locations, and the scope check finds no sibling site with the defect. Leg (b): server/services/publicationService.js:235 `if (job && job.status === 'completed') {` still gates the fallback, and I traced what 'completed' means rather than trusting the name — pipelineService.js runPipeline sets `job.status = 'completed'` at :332 only after BOTH sub-jobs pass the `if (injectJob.status === 'failed') throw` (:308) and `if (renderJob.status === 'failed') throw` (:323) checks, and the sub-job status itself comes from `child.on('close', (code) => { if (code === 0) job.status = 'completed'` (:422-425). So the fallback's `complete: true` (publicationService.js:250-258) can only be written after inject AND render actually exited 0 — a real success recorded through a secondary path, exactly as the refutation argued. Leg (a): the catch is not silent — :249 `log.error({ err, pubStage }, 'Pipeline status transition failed');` fires before the fallback write. I confirmed the refusal path is real (pipelineStatusService.transitionStage:155-158 throws `Cannot complete ${stage}: rendering must be complete first`), so the conflation the finding described does exist, but its worst outcome is the already-logged DB/status.json inconsistency the refutation conceded, not a masked failure. Scope enumeration of every transitionStage caller: pipelineService.js:743 and :760 (advanceChapterStatus/resetChapterStage) catch and only `log.error` — no status.json fallback; routes/pipeline-status.js:110 and :126 have no local catch at all and surface the refusal to the client as `res.status(400).json({ error: err.message })` (:139). publicationService.js:239 is the only site that writes complete:true on a failed transition. No sibling defect.
- ✅ upheld — Refutation still holds; I could not break it. Leg (b) — the fallback in `publishChapter`'s `promise.then()` is still gated by `publicationService.js:235` `if (job && job.status === 'completed')`, and I traced that this is only reachable after BOTH subprocesses exited 0: `pipelineService.runPipeline` throws `Inject failed`/`Render failed` on `injectJob.status === 'failed'` / `renderJob.status === 'failed'` (pipelineService.js:308-327) before `job.status = 'completed'` (:332), and `spawnJob` sets `'failed'` on non-zero exit (:426-428). So the render genuinely produced 05-publication output whenever `complete: true` is written — the 'FALSE' in 'false success' is what the guard kills. Leg (a) — `publicationService.js:249` `log.error({ err, pubStage }, 'Pipeline status transition failed')` precedes the `updateChapterStatus(... complete: true ...)` fallback at :250-258; not silent. OUT-OF-SCOPE OBSERVATIONS (notes, not this row): (i) the catch still cannot discriminate a legitimate prerequisite refusal from DB-unavailability, and I found the refusal path is likely the ROUTINE one, not an edge case — `pipelineStatusService.transitionStage:154-158` refuses `publication.<track>` unless the DB `rendering` row is complete, `rendering` requires `injection`, `injection` requires `tmCreated` (BASE_STAGES, :19-39), `pipelineService.advanceChapterStatus:743-746` swallows its own transition failures, and nothing seeds `chapter_pipeline_status` from status.json (migration 017 only creates the empty table) — so status.json and the authoritative DB routinely disagree and the prerequisite gate is defeated; (ii) if `updateChapterStatus` throws inside the un-`catch`ed `promise.then()` (publicationService.js:250) it is an unhandled rejection. Both are different defects from the one refuted here.
- ✅ upheld — Refutation still holds on essentially unchanged code, and I tested leg (b) rather than restating it. Leg (a): server/services/publicationService.js:249 `log.error({ err, pubStage }, 'Pipeline status transition failed');` sits at the top of the catch, before the fallback write at :250-258 — not silent. Leg (b): the fallback is gated at :235 `if (job && job.status === 'completed') {`, and that status is only reached in pipelineService.runPipeline after BOTH sub-jobs pass their failure checks (`if (injectJob.status === 'failed') throw`, pipelineService.js:308-310; `if (renderJob.status === 'failed') throw`, :323-325) — i.e. inject+render really finished, so `complete: true` records an artifact that genuinely exists on disk, not a masked refusal. Independent residue I confirmed but which does NOT flip the verdict: the catch is still undiscriminating (a plain `catch (err)`), and the refusal is not exceptional but ROUTINE — pipelineStatusService.transitionStage:149-158 refuses any `publication.*` completion unless the DB `rendering` row is complete, and `rendering` is a BASE stage whose own completion (pipelineService.advanceChapterStatus:743) requires the whole BASE_STAGES chain back through `tmCreated`/`linguisticReview` (pipelineStatusService.js:19-39, :159-172). So for an mt-preview publish the DB will normally refuse and the fallback will normally fire. That is exactly the 'minor, already-logged inconsistency between the database and the status file' the review's second pass pre-conceded, not the claimed false success.

### `code-ref-2` — CLOSED-BY-REFUTATION
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-code-review.md § "Refuted on second-pass verification" → `server/routes/books.js:590`
**Original severity:** Medium, CONFIRMED (failloud)
**Claim:** Original claim (refuted): a chapter markdown-import can report success even when registering the imported files in the database fails, leaving files that exist on disk invisible to every database-backed listing. The review refuted it on three legs: (a) the catch logs at error level with the full error object — not silent; (b) the files are already safely on disk and most user-facing views read from disk, so "only two specific database-backed helper functions are actually affected"; (c) the DB-registration step is safely retryable.
**Current location:** `server/routes/books.js:625-639 (POST /:bookId/chapters/:chapter/import), retry path at :263`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
Leg (a) holds — `books.js:634-638`: `chapterFilesService.registerFiles(bookId, chapterNum, filesToRegister, userId);` / `} catch (dbErr) {` / `log.error({ err: dbErr }, 'Failed to register imported files');` / `// Don't fail the request - files are already stored`. Leg (c) holds — a rescan/re-register endpoint still exists at `books.js:263`: `const result = chapterFilesService.scanAndRegisterExistingFiles(bookId, chapterNum, userId);`
```
**Notes:** The refuted core claim was "silent failure", and that refutation holds unchanged — disposition stands. ⚠️ One SECONDARY leg of the refutation no longer matches the tree: the review said "only two specific database-backed helper functions are actually affected"; current `books.js` has at least five DB-backed reads that would miss unregistered files — `getChapterFiles` (:225), `getChapterSectionFiles` (:228), `getGenerationHistory` (:231), `hasRequiredFiles` (:236), `getBookFilesSummary` (:327). That widens the blast radius the refutation used to minimise impact, but does not resurrect the "silent" claim (it is logged) nor the "not retryable" claim (:263). Recorded as an accuracy note, not a reopen. Separately, the route now also carries `requireHeadEditor('bookId')` (:551) — that closed the cross-referenced SA-11 rider (commit 4810320e), which was never part of this refuted group.
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — All three legs hold in current code and the scope sweep finds no unmitigated sibling. Leg (a) at server/routes/books.js:632-637: `chapterFilesService.registerFiles(bookId, chapterNum, filesToRegister, userId);` / `} catch (dbErr) {` / `log.error({ err: dbErr }, 'Failed to register imported files');` / `// Don't fail the request - files are already stored` — logged at error level with the full error, not silent. Leg (c) retry path is live and strictly reachable: `POST /:bookId/chapters/:chapter/files/scan` (books.js:255, `requireAuth, requireEditor()`) calls `chapterFilesService.scanAndRegisterExistingFiles(bookId, chapterNum, userId)` at :263, which readdirs the chapter dir and re-registers everything with a real file type (chapterFilesService.js:428-460) — and its role gate (requireEditor) is BROADER than the import route's (`requireHeadEditor('bookId')`, books.js:550), so anyone who could trigger the failed import can also trigger the repair. Leg (b) impact bound re-verified against current consumers, not taken on trust: I grepped every reader of the DB-backed helpers — getChapterFiles (books.js:225), hasRequiredFiles (books.js:236, itself derived from getChapterFiles at chapterFilesService.js:116) and getBookFilesSummary (books.js:327) are the only ones, all inside read-only informational GET routes; nothing gates a write or a pipeline stage on them. The sibling listing at books.js:228, getChapterSectionFiles, is disk-based (`fs.readdirSync(chapterDir)`, chapterFilesService.js:130), confirming the 'most user-facing views read from disk' leg. registerFiles has exactly two callers (books.js:634 and chapterFilesService.js:455); the second propagates its error to a 500 rather than swallowing it.
- ✅ upheld — Refutation still holds. Leg (a) — the catch around `chapterFilesService.registerFiles(...)` in POST `/:bookId/chapters/:chapter/import` still logs at error level: `books.js:636` `log.error({ err: dbErr }, 'Failed to register imported files');` with the comment `// Don't fail the request - files are already stored`; not silent. Leg (c) — I verified the retry path actually covers the import target rather than merely existing: POST `/:bookId/chapters/:chapter/files/scan` (`books.js:254`) calls `chapterFilesService.scanAndRegisterExistingFiles` (`chapterFilesService.js:428`), which resolves `getChapterDir(bookSlug, chapterNum)` = `books/<slug>/02-for-mt/ch{NN}` (`:70-72`) — the SAME directory the import writes into — readdirs it, types `.en.md` as `FILE_TYPES.EN_MD` via `getFileType` (`:160`) and calls `registerFiles`. So a failed registration is genuinely re-runnable. Leg (b) is imprecise in the review's own prose — the DB-backed helpers affected are `getChapterFiles` (:86), `hasRequiredFiles` (:115) and `getBookFilesSummary` (:398), i.e. three, not 'two', while `getChapterSectionFiles` (:126) reads disk — but that is an impact-magnitude quibble, not a leg that fails. The success response at `books.js:643-651` does still fire after a failed registration exactly as described; the refutation never denied that, only its 'silent'/'invisible everywhere' framing, and that framing is still correctly refuted.
- ✅ upheld — All three refutation legs verified against current code. Leg (a) not silent: server/routes/books.js:634-637 `chapterFilesService.registerFiles(...)` / `} catch (dbErr) { log.error({ err: dbErr }, 'Failed to register imported files'); // Don't fail the request - files are already stored`. Leg (b) impact bounded to two DB-backed helpers: in the GET .../files route the DB read is `chapterFilesService.getChapterFiles(bookId, chapterNum)` (books.js:223) — which is the same function `hasRequiredFiles` delegates to (chapterFilesService.js:115-121) — while `getChapterSectionFiles` (chapterFilesService.js:126-131 `const chapterDir = getChapterDir(...); const files = fs.readdirSync(chapterDir);`) reads disk, so the imported files remain visible there; the files themselves are on disk before the catch (books.js:600 `fs.renameSync(file.path, targetPath);`). Leg (c) retryable AND atomic — POST /:bookId/chapters/:chapter/files/scan (books.js:254, `requireAuth, requireEditor()`) calls `chapterFilesService.scanAndRegisterExistingFiles` (:263), which re-derives the list from `fs.readdirSync(chapterDir)` (chapterFilesService.js:435) and re-registers; and registerFiles wraps its work in `const insertMany = db.transaction((files) => {` (chapterFilesService.js:253) with a per-file_type `UPDATE ... SET superseded_at` before each insert (:241-245, :264), so it cannot half-succeed and a rescan cannot produce duplicate live rows. Adjacent wrinkle, out of scope and not a verdict flip: that supersede is keyed on file_type, not file_path, so a multi-file EN_MD import leaves only the last row non-superseded in `getChapterFiles`' `superseded_at IS NULL` view even when registration fully succeeds.

### `code-ref-3` — OPEN (flipped by skeptics)
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-code-review.md § "Refuted on second-pass verification" → `server/services/segmentEditorService.js:755`
**Original severity:** Medium, PLAUSIBLE (integrity)
**Claim:** Original claim (refuted): the faithful-translation file is overwritten inside a database transaction, so a failure partway through could leave the database rolled back while bad or corrupted content stays on disk. The review refuted it on four legs: (a) the write function never writes partial/corrupt content — timestamped backup plus atomic temp-file-then-rename; (b) the only failures that can happen after a successful write are verification checks that fire when the write already failed; (c) those failures roll back a marker re-checked on the next apply, so affected edits retry automatically; (d) the one theoretical post-successful-write failure is extremely hard to trigger and would still leave the DB consistent with prior content recoverable from the backup.
**Current location:** `server/services/segmentEditorService.js:923 (`applyTransaction`), :1028 (the write), :1030-1036 (verification); the write function itself at server/services/segmentParser.js:187-210`
**⚠️ Verifier said `CLOSED-BY-REFUTATION`; flipped to open by 2/3 skeptics.**
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
Structure unchanged — `segmentEditorService.js:923`: `const applyTransaction = conn.transaction(() => {` … `:1028`: `const savedPath = segmentParser.saveModuleSegments(book, chapter, moduleId, segments);`. Leg (a) holds at `segmentParser.js:196-208`: `// Create backup if file exists` / `fs.copyFileSync(paths.faithful, bakPath);` … `// Atomic write: write to temp file then rename (rename is atomic on Linux within same FS)` / `fs.writeFileSync(tmpPath, content, 'utf-8');` / `fs.renameSync(tmpPath, paths.faithful);`. Leg (b) write-already-failed checks still immediately follow the write — `:1032` `throw new Error(\`Failed to write faithful file: ${savedPath}\`);` and `:1036` `throw new Error(\`Faithful file written but empty: ${savedPath}\`);`. Leg (c): the marker is `applied_at`, still stamped only AFTER the write at `:1059-1060` `const markApplied = conn.prepare(\`UPDATE segment_edits SET applied_at = CURRENT_TIMESTAMP WHERE id = ?\`);`, and re-read as the retry gate at `:1002-1003` `const overlay = winner && winner.applied_at === null;` — a rollback leaves winners unapplied and they are re-overlaid next apply.
```
**Notes:** Refutation holds in substance, but ⚠️ one leg has WEAKENED since the review baseline and I record it rather than silently carrying it. Leg (b) — "the only failures that can happen after a successful write are verification checks that specifically fire when the write already failed" — is now false as literally written: item 20b added an in-transaction acceptance-lifecycle step AFTER the file write, `:1077-1078` `const lapsedAcceptances = acceptanceService.lapseDrifted(book, moduleId, segments, conn);` / `const acceptedCount = acceptanceService.stampApplied(book, moduleId, conn);` (added 2026-07-19, commit 79238056 "feat(item20b): apply gate widening + in-txn acceptance stamp/lapse + sidecar + apply-status"; further touched by cb3f158e). Two more DB calls now sit between the disk write and the transaction's end, widening the file-written/DB-rolled-back window the refutation dismissed as negligible. Legs (a), (c) and (d) still cover the consequence: no corrupt bytes (atomic rename), prior content in a timestamped `.bak`, DB left consistent by rollback, and the unapplied `applied_at` causes the same content to be re-applied idempotently on the next run. So: no reopen, but the refutation's "extremely difficult to trigger" premise is measurably less true than on 2026-07-11. Out of scope for a row, mentioned per scope discipline: the pre-write `contentVersionService.snapshotModule` at :1015 also writes on the same connection inside the same transaction, so a post-write failure discards the snapshot too — a different concern from the refuted one.
**Adversarial verdicts (2/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — Verdict intact, but with one correction the audit should record: the review's leg (b) is now literally FALSE. It claimed 'the only failures that can happen after a successful write are verification checks that specifically fire when the write already failed' — since the 2026-07-11 baseline, item 20b added post-write, in-transaction DB writes at segmentEditorService.js:1069-1070 (`acceptanceService.lapseDrifted(book, moduleId, segments, conn)` and `acceptanceService.stampApplied(book, moduleId, conn)`, both plain UPDATEs at acceptanceService.js:306-315 and :329-334) that did not exist when the refutation was written. The enumeration is imprecise; the conclusion is not, because legs (a), (c) and (d) each independently bound the failure and all three still hold. (a) segmentParser.saveModuleSegments:196-208 still does `fs.copyFileSync(paths.faithful, bakPath)` then `fs.writeFileSync(tmpPath, content, 'utf-8'); fs.renameSync(tmpPath, paths.faithful);` — no window exists in which partial/corrupt content can sit on disk, which kills the claim's 'bad or corrupted content stays on disk' half regardless of how many statements follow the write. (c) `applied_at` is still stamped only AFTER the write (:1059-1060 `UPDATE segment_edits SET applied_at = CURRENT_TIMESTAMP WHERE id = ?`) and re-read as the retry gate (:1002-1003 `const overlay = winner && winner.applied_at === null;`), so a rollback leaves winners unapplied and the next apply re-overlays byte-identical content over the file it just wrote — convergent, not lossy. (d) the new step-7 statements are the same failure CLASS (SQLITE_FULL/IOERR on a post-write UPDATE) as the pre-existing markApplied/markSuperseded UPDATEs that leg (d) already covered; the plausible non-exotic failure — a missing segment_acceptances table — throws BEFORE the write, at the `SELECT COUNT(*) AS n FROM segment_acceptances` gate at :934-940. Scope sweep for siblings: the only other saveModuleSegments caller is contentVersionService.js:30 (restoreVersion), which is NOT wrapped in a transaction, and the localized track deliberately writes outside the transaction (localizationReviewService.js:238 comment 'The file write is the one step a SQLite transaction cannot roll back', write at :253 before `conn.transaction(` at :265). Bounded residue worth naming, not a survival: a post-write rollback also reverts lapseDrifted, so segment_acceptances rows stay 'active' with accepted_content that no longer matches the file, and the post-commit writeReviewStatusSidecar (segmentEditorService.js:1092, own try/catch) could briefly emit 'accepted' for drifted bytes — transient, since the next apply re-runs lapseDrifted on the same segments and regenerates the sidecar.
- ❌ REFUTED — Leg (b) of the refutation is FACTUALLY FALSE on today's tree, and the verifier's evidence omits the code that falsifies it. The refutation's load-bearing claim was that 'the only failures that can happen after a successful write are verification checks that specifically fire when the write already failed'. Inside `applyTransaction` (segmentEditorService.js:923) the file write is at :1028 `const savedPath = segmentParser.saveModuleSegments(...)`, and AFTER it, still inside the same transaction, run: the `markApplied`/`markSuperseded` UPDATE loops (:1064-1075) and — new since the review baseline — step 7 `const lapsedAcceptances = acceptanceService.lapseDrifted(book, moduleId, segments, conn);` (:1077) and `acceptanceService.stampApplied(book, moduleId, conn)` (:1078). Those are real per-row SELECT/UPDATE work in `acceptanceService.js:298` and `:328` against `segment_acceptances`; they throw for reasons wholly unrelated to the file write (missing table on an un-migrated DB, constraint/IO error), and `acceptanceService.js` did not exist at the 2026-07-11 baseline (added by `b65594ae`, item 20b, after 2026-07-20). The structural hazard the finding named — file overwritten INSIDE a DB transaction that can still roll back — is therefore not only still present but has a materially WIDER post-write failure window than when it was refuted, and `applyTransaction.immediate()` (:1090) has no compensating file-side unwind. Being precise about what survives: legs (a), (c), (d) DO still hold — `segmentParser.saveModuleSegments` still takes a timestamped `.bak` (`segmentParser.js:195-200`) and writes tmp-then-`fs.renameSync` (`:205-208`), so partial/corrupt BYTES on disk remain impossible; `applied_at` is stamped only after the write (:1064-1066) and re-read as the retry gate via `const overlay = winner && winner.applied_at === null;` (:998), so rolled-back winners are re-overlaid on the next apply. The live residue is file/DB divergence (including the step-4b `contentVersionService.snapshotModule` version row rolling back after the file already changed), not corruption — but the refutation as written no longer describes the code, so CLOSED-BY-REFUTATION is not sustainable.
- ❌ REFUTED — The refutation's load-bearing leg (b) is factually false of the code as it now stands, because post-write work was added after the review. Baseline comparison: at 892fe7fc (last commit to this file before 2026-07-12) the region between the file write (`const savedPath = segmentParser.saveModuleSegments(...)`, old :909) and the end of `applyTransaction` (old :954) contained ONLY the write-already-failed verification checks and `markApplied`/`markSuperseded`. Current main inserts a whole new step 7 between the write and the transaction end: server/services/segmentEditorService.js:1073-1074 `const lapsedAcceptances = acceptanceService.lapseDrifted(book, moduleId, segments, conn);` / `const acceptedCount = acceptanceService.stampApplied(book, moduleId, conn);` — both mutating a SECOND table (`UPDATE segment_acceptances SET status = 'superseded'...`, acceptanceService.js:306-311; `UPDATE segment_acceptances SET applied_at = ...`, :331-333), inside the same `conn.transaction(...)` opened at :923 and committed by `applyTransaction.immediate()` at :1090. So 'the only failures that can happen after a successful write are verification checks that specifically fire when the write already failed' no longer describes this code, and the ordering the finding described — irreversible atomic file overwrite (segmentParser.js:206-208 `fs.writeFileSync(tmpPath, ...)` / `fs.renameSync(tmpPath, paths.faithful)`) performed INSIDE a DB transaction that can still roll back afterwards — is unchanged and now has a strictly larger post-write failure surface (step 6 + step 7 + the commit itself). Legs (a) atomic-write-with-.bak (segmentParser.js:196-201, :206-208), (c) retry via `applied_at` still stamped only after the write (:1054-1056) and re-read as the gate at :1002 `const overlay = winner && winner.applied_at === null;`, and (d) recoverability from the .bak all do still hold — so this row survives because the reasoning that retired it was invalidated by later code, not because I demonstrated a live crash. I cannot point at any current code that makes 'DB rolled back while the overwritten file stays on disk' impossible; a deterministic (non-transient) throw in step 7 would leave the faithful file advanced, no content_versions snapshot recorded (the 4b snapshot at :1014-1021 uses the same `conn` and rolls back with it), and the editor reporting the edits as unapplied on every retry.

### `code-ref-4` — CLOSED-BY-REFUTATION
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-server-code-review.md § "Refuted on second-pass verification" → `server/services/segmentEditorService.js:750`
**Original severity:** Low, CONFIRMED (failloud)
**Claim:** Original claim (refuted): a failure while taking the pre-apply safety snapshot is swallowed silently, with the success response giving the user no indication that the usual rollback safety net was not created for that save. The review refuted it: the failure IS logged at error level with the full underlying error through the standard logger; the only accurate residue is that the HTTP response carries no explicit warning flag, which is a deliberate, code-commented best-effort design choice ("non-fatal, continuing apply") meant to be visible to an operator in server logs.
**Current location:** `server/services/segmentEditorService.js:1013-1025 (step "4b. Snapshot current content before overwriting (for rollback)")`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
`segmentEditorService.js:1014-1025`: `try {` / `contentVersionService.snapshotModule(book, chapter, moduleId, currentSegments, appliedBy, conn);` / `} catch (snapErr) {` / `log.error({ err: snapErr }, 'Content snapshot failed (non-fatal, continuing apply)');` / `}` — the error-level log AND the exact code comment the refutation cited are both still present verbatim. The best-effort intent is reinforced by the surrounding comment at `:1005-1009`: `// Must write on the apply's own connection: this runs inside an IMMEDIATE // write transaction, so a second connection's INSERT would hit SQLITE_BUSY // and the snapshot would be silently dropped (leaving an empty version // history despite a successful apply).`
```
**Notes:** Refutation re-confirmed exactly — the "silent" premise of the original claim remains false, and the deliberate-design leg is still evidenced by the in-source comment. The same-connection `conn` argument (added for the SQLITE_BUSY reason documented at :1005-1009) strengthens rather than weakens the refutation. No disposition change.
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — The refutation holds verbatim and, on the scope lens, holds at every snapshot site — there is no silent sibling. At segmentEditorService.js:1013-1025 the failure is still logged at error level with the underlying error: `} catch (snapErr) {` / `log.error({ err: snapErr }, 'Content snapshot failed (non-fatal, continuing apply)');`, i.e. both the error-level log and the exact 'non-fatal, continuing apply' comment the refutation cited are present in current source, so the 'swallowed silently' core of the claim is false. The deliberate best-effort design is reinforced by the :1005-1009 comment explaining why the snapshot must run on the apply's own connection ('a second connection\'s INSERT would hit SQLITE_BUSY and the snapshot would be silently dropped'). Enumerating every contentVersionService.snapshotModule caller: segmentEditorService.js:1015 (logs, above); contentVersionService.js:392 in saveLocalizedWithSnapshot — the later-added localized-track sibling — also logs at error level (`log.error({ err, book, moduleId }, 'Localized pre-write snapshot failed (save proceeds)');` at :393); and contentVersionService.js:263 inside restoreVersion has NO catch at all, so it fails loud (stricter than the finding's premise, not laxer). The only accurate residue remains that the HTTP response carries no warning flag, which the review already characterised as a deliberate, code-commented choice.
- ✅ upheld — Refutation still holds; my best mechanism attack failed. The cited code is present verbatim at step '4b. Snapshot current content before overwriting (for rollback)': `segmentEditorService.js:1014-1025` wraps `contentVersionService.snapshotModule(book, chapter, moduleId, currentSegments, appliedBy, conn)` in try/catch with `:1024` `log.error({ err: snapErr }, 'Content snapshot failed (non-fatal, continuing apply)');` — error-level log plus the exact 'non-fatal, continuing apply' comment the refutation cited. I attacked the obvious hole — that swallowing a mid-insert throw could commit a PARTIAL version snapshot, which would be worse than none because `restoreVersion` would later write incomplete content back over the faithful file — and it does not work: `contentVersionService.snapshotModule` runs its per-segment inserts inside a nested `const insertAll = db.transaction(() => {...})` (`contentVersionService.js:112-133`) on the apply's own connection, and a nested better-sqlite3 transaction is a SAVEPOINT, so any throw rolls back to the savepoint and the outcome is all-or-nothing. The only residue is the one the refutation already conceded — the HTTP success response carries no warning flag — which is the documented best-effort design, reinforced by the comment at :1005-1009 explaining why the snapshot must share the apply's connection.
- ✅ upheld — Code is verbatim what the refutation described, and the baseline comparison shows nothing about the snapshot's failure semantics regressed since. Current: server/services/segmentEditorService.js:1014-1025 `try { contentVersionService.snapshotModule(book, chapter, moduleId, currentSegments, appliedBy, conn); } catch (snapErr) { log.error({ err: snapErr }, 'Content snapshot failed (non-fatal, continuing apply)'); }` — the error-level log with the full error object AND the exact 'non-fatal, continuing apply' comment the refutation cited are both present. At the review baseline 892fe7fc the same block already read `contentVersionService.snapshotModule(book, chapter, moduleId, currentSegments, null, conn)` (old :903) with the identical catch and log at old :905; the only delta since is threading `appliedBy` in place of `null` (item 12 attribution), which does not touch the failure path. The refutation's design-intent leg is also reinforced in current code by the comment at :1010-1013 explaining why the snapshot must ride the apply's own connection (a second connection would hit SQLITE_BUSY and drop the snapshot silently). Honest residue, which the review itself conceded as the only accurate part of the original claim: the HTTP response still carries no warning flag — the apply returns normally and the caller cannot tell the rollback snapshot was skipped. Treating that residue as 'the claim survives' would make every refuted finding permanently open, since a refutation by construction leaves the code shape alone.

**Register item:** P3 · item 23 · "Batch 3 — docs authority triage" (docs/plans/2026-07-21-post-item17-followup-campaign.md:112), whose scope per the joint summary line 28 "Draws from: Editorial §2 drift catalog + rec #1, #4"

The human-verified translation memory — one of the project's three headline deliverables (CLAUDE.md § Purpose) — does not exist yet."  |  CLAUDE.md:450 — "...250 MT-preview pages vs **1 faithful module** project-wide; `tm/` **empty** in every book — the TM deliverable doesn't exist yet..."  |  `git log -- docs/plans/2026-06-12-editorial-throughput-roadmap.md` newest entry is `939e7e77` (2026-06-13, "Unit 3.5: term-decision mining") — i.e. NO commit has touched the file since the review was written (2026-07-11); `git log -S 'tm/` **empty** in every book' -- CLAUDE.md` → only `eddbb0f6`, the original introduction.  |  On disk today: `books/efnafraedi-2e/tm/efnafraedi-2e-2026-06-13.tmx` exists (3686 bytes) — the sentence is false for that book.

Item 23 is nonetheless the live campaign's designated home for the §2 drift catalog, so per the prefer-TRACKED rule it is cited here; if item 23 is executed to its literal enumeration, this sentence will survive uncorrected. Corroborating (not load-bearing): the committed TMX is still the 3-TU 2026-06-13 file although efnafraedi-2e now has 4 faithful modules — consistent with the pathspec gap, since regeneration output cannot be committed. (3) A further scope hole in 'fired after every write ... keeps the TM current': tools/generate-tm.js:67 `const { licence, obtained } = getBookLicence(book); // fail-loud on unknown slug` throws for any book without a licence block (tools/lib/book-licences.cjs), the child exits non-zero, and tmService.js:52-66 only `log.warn`s ('TM regeneration exited non-zero') — a silently stale TM, tracked as I21-R2 (register line 99). Latent today only because stjornufraedi/testbook carry no faithful content. What IS true and unchanged: scheduleTmRegen exists (tmService.js:80-91, DEBOUNCE_MS=5000 at :21, spawn of tools/generate-tm.js at :20/:32) and both faithful writers call it (segmentEditorService.js:1129 inside applyApprovedEdits, contentVersionService.js:304 on restore) — but confirming a half the finding itself labelled a MATCH is not closure of the drift the finding actually reported.

On-disk corroboration: of 24 status.json files carrying a `tmCreated` field, exactly 2 are `complete:true`, dated `2024-12-01` and `2026-01-12` — the latter with `"notes": "Currently being aligned in Matecat"`, i.e. pure Matecat-era residue predating the review.
**Notes:** SEARCHED ALL FOUR SOURCES for coverage — `grep -n 'orphan|tmCreated'` over docs/plans/2026-07-21-post-item17-followup-campaign.md, docs/plans/2026-07-11-pre-semester-coding-campaign.md, CLAUDE.md and MEMORY.md returns zero relevant hits (the only CLAUDE.md hit is line 275, the documentation OF the stage; the only MEMORY hit is "orphaned images", unrelated). Deliberately NOT absorbed into item 23: that item is doc triage and would at best fix CLAUDE.md:275 and the schema's retired "via Matecat Align" wording — it does not address a stage nothing advances. The nearest TM-adjacent register items (C3 · I20-R1 git-backup pathspecs; I21-R2 licence-row-before-regen) are about TM durability, not stage advancement.

CONSIDERED AND REJECTED as a partial refutation: `bookRegistration.scanAndUpdateStatus` (server/services/bookRegistration.js:1013, reachable via `POST /api/status/:book/sync` and `/:book/:chapter/sync`, requireAuth+requireAdmin, routes/status.js:1830/1880) DOES contain tmCreated promote/demote logic (:1143-1159, `results.changes.push({chapter: ch, stage: 'tmCreated', action: 'promoted to complete'})`). But its predicate can never fire: :1043-1046 `tmCreated: (book, ch, section) => fs.existsSync(path.join(BOOKS_DIR, book, 'tm', `ch${ch}`, filename))` keys on `tm/chNN/<section>.tmx`, while the generator writes book-level date-stamped files — tools/lib/tm-export.cjs:467-470 `defaultOutPath` → `path.join(BOOKS_DIR, book, 'tm', `${book}-${date}.${EXT[format]}`)`, and `ls books/*/tm/` shows exactly `efnafraedi-2e/tm/efnafraedi-2e-2026-06-13.tmx` (+ an empty `exports/`). So the only code that references advancing tmCreated keys on a directory layout `generate-tm.js` does not produce: that path can only ever DEMOTE. The review's claim stands intact and is in fact strengthened.

SEVERITY-RAISING CONSEQUENCE the review did not draw (note only, per scope discipline — not a new ledger row): `transitionStage` enforces sequential prerequisites for base stages — server/services/pipelineStatusService.js:161-172 `const priorStage = BASE_STAGES[idx - 1]; ... if (!priorRow || priorRow.status !== 'complete') { throw new Error(`Cannot complete ${stage}: ${priorStage} must be complete first`); }`. `BASE_STAGES[4]` is `tmCreated` and `BASE_STAGES[5]` is `injection`, so with tmCreated never complete in `chapter_pipeline_status`, EVERY `advanceChapterStatus(book, chapter, 'injection')` throws — and pipelineService.js:735-746 swallows it: `catch (err) { log.error({ err, chapter, stage }, 'Auto-advance status failed'); }`. Note that `scanAndUpdateStatus` writes status.json FILES, not the DB table the prerequisite reads, so it cannot unblock this even if its predicate matched. Corroborated by the suite itself: server/__tests__/pipelineStatus.test.js:105-117 has to hand-complete `tmCreated` in a loop before it can complete injection/rendering. The orphan is therefore not a cosmetic schema wart — it silently blocks DB-side injection/rendering advancement in production. Also worth an item-23 line: the schema description at :70 still names the retired tool ("via Matecat Align"), the same retired-pipeline account the review flags in master-pipeline.md.

## Group: `ed-qa-table`
### `ed-qa-0.1a` — CLOSED
**Source:** editorial-workflow-review.md §4 QA evidence table, row 0.1a
**Original severity:** PARTIAL
**Claim:** Editor opens a module preview normally — scored PARTIAL: the live result was a clean 404 ("run inject first"), correct for the fixture precondition but not the checklist's literal "renders HTML as before".
**Current location:** `server/e2e/segment-editor.spec.js:425`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
test('§0.1a preview of a real module renders expected HTML', async ({ page }) => { … const resp = await page.request.get('/api/segment-editor/efnafraedi-2e/1/m68664/preview'); … expect(resp.status()).toBe(200); expect(resp.headers()['content-type']).toContain('text/html'); … expect(body).toContain('<article class="cnx-module'); expect(body).toContain('data-module-id="m68664"');
```
**Notes:** Closed by A4 PR-1 (PR #326, merge 0f179883); the PARTIAL was an artifact of testing against the un-injected __e2e-fixture__ book — the new test targets efnafraedi-2e/m68664, whose mt-preview CNXML is tracked in git, so it hard-pins 200 + rendered HTML. Runbook Phase 1 step 5 lists §0.1a as 🟢. A4 itself is still an unsigned [LEAD] gate, so the row will be re-confirmed by running the suite, but the check now has a durable machine pin.
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — Defect not reachable. The PARTIAL's specific gap was that the live walk got a 404 ("Run inject before previewing this module") instead of rendered HTML. Traced the production path independently: GET /:book/:chapter/:moduleId/preview (server/routes/segment-editor.js:1501) defaults track='mt-preview' (:1508) and calls renderService.renderModule, which reads books/<book>/03-translated/<track>/chNN/<moduleId>.cnxml (server/services/renderService.js:66-78) and calls renderCnxmlToHtml (tools/cnxml-render.js:591), whose emitter writes `  <article class="cnx-module..." data-module-id="...">` at tools/cnxml-render.js:813. The 404 precondition is gone for the tested target: `git ls-files books/efnafraedi-2e/03-translated/mt-preview/ch01/m68664.cnxml` returns the path, so the file exists on any fresh clone. Corroborated (not relied on) by server/e2e/segment-editor.spec.js:425 hard-pinning 200 + text/html + both markers.
- ✅ upheld — Mechanism traced end-to-end. The original PARTIAL was the 404 branch at server/routes/segment-editor.js:1525-1530 ('Translated CNXML not found' / 'Run inject before previewing this module'), reached because the fixture module had never been injected. The new test (server/e2e/segment-editor.spec.js:425-437) retargets efnafraedi-2e/1/m68664, whose injected source IS tracked in git — `git ls-files books/efnafraedi-2e/03-translated/mt-preview/ch01/m68664.cnxml` returns the path — so renderService.renderModule cannot take the 'not found' branch for it. The assertions are non-vacuous: hard 200 (not merely 'not 400'), content-type text/html, and body containing both '<article class="cnx-module' and 'data-module-id="m68664"', which a 200-with-error-payload or broken renderer would fail. The checklist's literal 'renders HTML as before' is now met and pinned. Confirmed running: gh run view of the latest test.yml run on main shows job `e2e: success`.
- ✅ upheld — Scope holds. The preview route chain at server/routes/segment-editor.js:1501-1506 is `requireAuth, requireRole(ROLES.EDITOR), validateBookChapter, validateModule` — there is NO requireBookAccess(), so the seeded `editor` in loginAs() is not book-scoped out of efnafraedi-2e and the 200 assertion is reachable. Default track is `req.query.track || 'mt-preview'` (:1508), and books/efnafraedi-2e/03-translated/mt-preview/ch01/m68664.cnxml IS git-tracked (confirmed via `git ls-files`), so the file exists on a fresh clone as the test comment claims. The original PARTIAL was purely evidentiary — the review itself called the fixture's 404 'the correct behavior for this precondition'; the missing piece was a demonstrated real-module render, and segment-editor.spec.js:425 now hard-pins status 200, content-type text/html, `<article class="cnx-module` and `data-module-id="m68664"`. NOTE: this is 'the test asserts X and the code path supports it', not 'observed green' — the suite was not run (read-only rule).

### `ed-qa-0.1b` — CLOSED
**Source:** editorial-workflow-review.md §4, row 0.1b
**Original severity:** PASS
**Claim:** Path-traversal `track` guard on the live-preview endpoint — PASS, returned 400.
**Current location:** `server/e2e/segment-editor.spec.js:439`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
test('§0.1b traversal track query is rejected 400', … page.request.get('/api/segment-editor/efnafraedi-2e/1/m68664/preview?track=..%2F..%2F..%2Fetc%2Fpasswd'); expect(resp.status()).toBe(400);
```
**Notes:** Was one of the three curl checks named in the review's brief; now a standing e2e regression pin (A4 PR-1, #326). Prior state per docs/plans/2026-06-10-qa-checklist.md:44 was '⏳ VALID_TRACKS constants unit-tested; live 400 not asserted — curl-checkable'.
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — Guard present in production code, independently located: server/routes/segment-editor.js:1512 `if (!VALID_TRACKS.includes(track))` → 400, with the comment naming the exact hazard (track flows into path.join(... '03-translated', track, ...) inside renderService). VALID_TRACKS is a fixed allowlist ['mt-preview','faithful','localized'] at server/constants.js:54, so '../../../etc/passwd' cannot pass. The traversal failure is unreachable.
- ✅ upheld — The guard is an allowlist, not a sanitizer, and it sits upstream of the sink. server/routes/segment-editor.js:1512-1516: `if (!VALID_TRACKS.includes(track)) { return res.status(400)... }` with the preceding comment 'Guard against path traversal via the track query param — it flows into path.join(... 03-translated, track, ...) inside renderService'. Because it is a membership test against a fixed list, no encoding of '../../../etc/passwd' can pass; the 400 returns before renderService.renderModule (line 1519) is ever called. server/e2e/segment-editor.spec.js:439-445 pins the 400 for the URL-encoded traversal payload, and the e2e job is green on main.
- ✅ upheld — Scope attack fails: I enumerated every track-consuming site. `grep -rn 'req.query.track|query.track' server/routes/*.js server/services/*.js` returns exactly ONE hit — server/routes/segment-editor.js:1508 — and it is guarded three lines later at :1512 with `if (!VALID_TRACKS.includes(track)) return res.status(400)`, with the comment 'Guard against path traversal via the track query param — it flows into path.join(... 03-translated, track, ...) inside renderService.' server/routes/localization-editor.js has no track query param (its only 'track' mention is the comment at :759 'track-parameterized to localized throughout'). The observed behavior the row recorded (reaches the next layer, not 403) is what the code still does — validateChapterParams runs after the authz middleware (publication.js:136), so an out-of-range chapter yields 400.
- ✅ upheld — The described behavior is enforced by explicit, unconditional code, not merely by a passing assertion. server/middleware/requireRole.js:67-70: `// Admins can access any book` / `if (req.user.role === ROLES.ADMIN) { return next(); }` — placed BEFORE the `books.includes(book)` check, so an admin can never receive the book-scope 403 on any requireHeadEditor-guarded route regardless of membership. I note the test's assertion is loose — `expect(resp.status()).not.toBe(403)` at rbac.spec.js:140 would also be satisfied by a 401/404/500 — but the mechanism itself is unambiguous in code, and the paired §0.3d test proves the same route DOES 403 a non-owning head-editor, so the bypass is the operative difference.

So for the admin in rbac.spec.js:134 the book-scope check is genuinely executed and genuinely declines to reject (admin bypass) before chapter 999 produces its 400; `expect(resp.status()).not.toBe(403)` is therefore a real observation of the bypass, not an artifact of validation short-circuiting ahead of authz. - ✅ upheld — The emission site carries who and when, and the test's assertion is run-unique rather than 'some row exists'. server/services/contentVersionService.js:334-346 calls activityLog.log with `type: ACTIVITY_TYPES.VERSION_RESTORED` (activityLog.js:22 = 'version_restored'), `userId: String(restoredBy.userId != null ? restoredBy.userId : 'system')` and `username: actorName` (the 'who', resolved at :245-246), scoped to book/chapter/section, with metadata {restoredVersion, snapshotVersion, ...}; the 'when' is the table's created_at, which activityLog's own read queries order by (activityLog.js:83-107). review-cycle.spec.js:225-232 asserts the pair (restoredVersion, snapshotVersion) matches THIS run's restore — the spec comment at :218-224 explains that restoredVersion alone repeats across runs against a reused DB, so the pair is what makes it non-vacuous. A dropped log call fails the assertion.
- ✅ upheld — The 'who/when' half is guaranteed by code I can quote, not merely by the test. server/services/contentVersionService.js:335-350 calls `activityLog.log({type: activityLog.ACTIVITY_TYPES.VERSION_RESTORED, userId: String(restoredBy.userId != null ? restoredBy.userId : 'system'), username: actorName, book, chapter, section: moduleId, description: 'Færði ... í útgáfu ...', metadata: {restoredVersion, snapshotVersion, ...}})`, and server/services/activityLog.js:78 inserts `(type, user_id, username, book, chapter, section, description, metadata)` into a table whose rows are read back with `createdAt: row.created_at` (:309) — so actor and timestamp are structurally present on every emission. The e2e at review-cycle.spec.js:212 asserts type plus the restoredVersion/snapshotVersion pair (deliberately, per its comment, to scope the assertion to THIS run rather than 'some row exists'). Noted but not flipping: the test does not itself assert actor/timestamp, and activityLog.log is a never-throw sink (server/__tests__/activityLogNeverThrow.test.js:57 'never-throw contract' — returns null and pino-logs on a missing table/malformed payload), so a silent audit-write loss would not fail this test; that swallowing behavior is the separately-registered companion finding #21, not this row.
