```
**Notes:** Closed by A4 PR-1 (#326). The 403 fires in requireHeadEditor before the module/version ids are used, which is why a non-existent module id is a valid target here.
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — Cross-book restore 403 still enforced: POST /:book/:chapter/:moduleId/restore/:version (server/routes/segment-editor.js:1428) mounts requireHeadEditor() at :1430 ahead of validateBookChapter/validateModule and ahead of the version parse at :1433, so requireRole.js:78-83 returns 403 before any module or version id is used. Throwaway ids (m99999, version 1) cannot route around it.
- ✅ upheld — The restore route is guarded by the same URL-param book check as apply, and the guard fires before any version/module id is dereferenced. server/routes/segment-editor.js:1427-1433: `router.post('/:book/:chapter/:moduleId/restore/:version', requireAuth, requireHeadEditor(), validateBookChapter, validateModule, ...)`, with the route doc at :1424 stating 'Book-scoped head-editor only (admin bypasses)'. server/middleware/requireRole.js:73-81 returns 403 when neither the ADMIN bypass nor books.includes('liffraedi-2e') matches, and the head-editor token's books are ['efnafraedi-2e','__e2e-fixture__'] (server/e2e/helpers/auth.js). rbac.spec.js:143-149 asserts a positive 403 — and because the guard precedes the handler, the nonexistent m99999/version 1 can never be reached, so the 403 is genuinely the authz result and not a coincidental error.
- ✅ upheld — I verified the RESTORE route's own guard chain rather than inferring it from the apply route. server/routes/segment-editor.js:1427-1432: `router.post('/:book/:chapter/:moduleId/restore/:version', requireAuth, requireHeadEditor(), validateBookChapter, validateModule, ...)` — requireHeadEditor() precedes both validators and the handler's own version parse (:1434-1437), so the cross-book head-editor is rejected before m99999 or version 1 are used. (m99999 does satisfy ^m\d{5}$, so validateModule could not have pre-empted the check and produced a misleading pass.) rbac.spec.js:143 asserts exactly 403, and the code comment at :1424 states the intent: 'Book-scoped head-editor only (admin bypasses).'

### `ed-qa-4c` — CLOSED
**Source:** editorial-workflow-review.md §4, row 4c
**Original severity:** PASS
**Claim:** 8-stage pipeline view + tracks hidden from the editor role — PASS, confirmed at the source (display:none gated on isHeadEditor) and against live role state.
**Current location:** `server/e2e/a4-coverage.spec.js:167`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
test('§4c editor does not see pipeline/apply panels', … await expect(page.locator('#pipeline-panel')).toBeHidden(); await expect(page.locator('#apply-panel')).toBeHidden(); })  — plus the role-sensitivity proof at :179 test('§4c head-editor DOES see them (role-sensitivity proof)', … await expect(page.locator('#pipeline-panel, #apply-panel').first()).toBeVisible(…)
```
**Notes:** Closed by A4 PR-1 (#326, commit 9c16dd36). The paired both-directions assertion is what makes this a real pin rather than a vacuous 'always hidden' — a regression that hid the panels from everyone would go red on the second test. This upgrades qa-checklist:102's prior '◐ … editor-specific pipeline-hide is a visual check'.
**Adversarial verdicts (1/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — Role gating still present and fail-closed by default — stronger than the row asserted. Markup default: server/views/segment-editor.html:1449 `<div class="pipeline-panel" id="pipeline-panel" style="display: none;">` and :1489 the same for #apply-panel, so an editor sees nothing even if the JS never runs. Gating: server/public/js/segment-editor.js:1913-1917 showPipelinePanel and :2108-2114 showApplyPanel each compute `['head-editor','admin'].includes(getEffectiveRole())` and set display accordingly; both are invoked unconditionally on module load at :280/:282 and re-run on roleVisibilityChanged at :2120-2121.
- ✅ upheld — Gating and the anti-vacuity control both check out. Markup default is hidden: server/views/segment-editor.html:1449 `<div class="pipeline-panel" id="pipeline-panel" style="display: none;">` and :1489 the same for apply-panel; visibility is granted only by showPipelinePanel()/showApplyPanel(), called from loadModule at server/public/js/segment-editor.js:280-282 (defined at :1913 and :2108) gated on effective role. The test asserts BOTH directions (a4-coverage.spec.js:167-177 hidden for editor, :179-187 visible for head-editor), and the positive control is not vacuous: #pipeline-panel precedes #apply-panel in the DOM (1449 < 1489), so `page.locator('#pipeline-panel, #apply-panel').first()` resolves to #pipeline-panel — the head-editor test genuinely proves that panel becomes visible, which is the substance of the row. It also waits for `.segment-row` first (:174), so it cannot pass merely because the module had not finished loading. Residual nit, not a closure failure: #apply-panel's positive case is not independently proven by the .first() selector.
- ❌ REFUTED — Scope failure on the literal claim. The row is '8-stage pipeline view + TRACKS hidden from the editor role'; a4-coverage.spec.js:167 asserts only `#pipeline-panel` and `#apply-panel` are hidden. There is a THIRD panel holding a track control that neither the fix nor the test reaches: server/views/segment-editor.html:1472-1484 defines `<div class="preview-panel" id="preview-panel" style="display: none;">` containing `<select id="preview-track">` with `<option value="mt-preview">MT</option>` and `<option value="faithful">Bein þýðing</option>` — i.e. the exact track vocabulary the row says must be hidden. Its show function is UNGATED: server/public/js/segment-editor.js:2058-2061 `function showPreviewPanel() { const panel = document.getElementById('preview-panel'); if (panel) panel.style.display = 'block'; }` — no role test at all, in direct contrast to its two siblings called on the adjacent lines, showPipelinePanel (:1913-1917) and showApplyPanel (:2108-2112), which both begin `const isHeadEditor = ['head-editor','admin'].includes(getEffectiveRole())`. All three are invoked together from loadModule at :280-282, so a plain `editor` opening any module is shown a visible track selector. Provenance: `git log -S` puts both `preview-track` and `showPreviewPanel` at 1f0d9a59 (2026-03-24, 'feat(server): add live preview'), i.e. FOUR MONTHS BEFORE the 2026-07-11 review — so the original PASS ('confirmed at the source, display:none gated on isHeadEditor') was already incomplete when written, and the A4 lock inherited the same blind spot. Not covered by any carve-out in the register's A4 PR-1 findings list (campaign :127).

Prefer the precise per-row line over the Quick map. The row is nonetheless CLOSED on the pre-existing unit pin above, which covers the SEGMENT editor (the row's actual subject). Second caveat for the walker: server/e2e/concurrent-editing.spec.js:99's `expect(saveB.status()).toBe(409)` is the LOCALIZATION editor, and its own :37 comment plus a test.skip guard mean it self-skips when the fixture lacks a 03-faithful-translation seed — which per project memory is gitignored in __e2e-fixture__, so that assertion likely never runs in CI. The segment-editor describe at :107 only covers `otherPendingSegments` awareness, never a 409.
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — 409 path intact end to end. Detection: server/services/segmentEditorService.js:57-74 checkEditConflict queries segment_edits for a row by a DIFFERENT editor with id > baseEditId and throws err.code='SEGMENT_CONFLICT'; it is called from saveSegmentEdit at :98. Surfacing: server/routes/segment-editor.js:475-477 `if (err.code === 'SEGMENT_CONFLICT') return res.status(409)...`. Reachability in the real UI (the part that makes this a live guarantee rather than a dormant one): the client sends the token at server/public/js/segment-editor.js:1367 `baseEditId: latestEditIdForSegment(segmentId)` — without it checkEditConflict is a documented no-op. Coverage-shape note, not a defect: the Pass-1 409 is unit-pinned (server/__tests__/segmentEditConflict.test.js:63); server/e2e/concurrent-editing.spec.js:99 asserts 409 for the LOCALIZATION editor only.
- ✅ upheld — The full two-editor chain exists in production code, not just in the unit test's service call. (1) The client actually sends the token — server/public/js/segment-editor.js:1367 `baseEditId: latestEditIdForSegment(segmentId)` — so the 'skips the check when no token is supplied' backward-compat branch is not what real editors hit. (2) The service raises on a stale token — server/services/segmentEditorService.js:72 `err.code = 'SEGMENT_CONFLICT'` (documented at :48 'so the caller reloads instead of silently racing'). (3) The route converts it to the status the row names — server/routes/segment-editor.js:475-476 `if (err.code === 'SEGMENT_CONFLICT') { return res.status(409).json({ error: 'conflict', ... }); }`, i.e. a specific branch ahead of the generic 500. The unit test at server/__tests__/segmentEditConflict.test.js:63-75 pins the throw for exactly the described case (editor A saves; editor B saves the same segment with baseEditId 0), and its sibling cases pin that an up-to-date token and self-edits do NOT conflict, so the guard is not simply 'always throw'.
- ✅ upheld — The full HTTP-level chain the row describes exists in current code and I can quote every link, so the described failure (a second editor's stale save silently overwriting) is prevented. server/services/segmentEditorService.js:58-75 `checkEditConflict` runs `SELECT editor_username FROM segment_edits WHERE book=? AND module_id=? AND segment_id=? AND editor_id != ? AND id > ?` against baseEditId and, on a hit, throws with `err.code = 'SEGMENT_CONFLICT'` (:72); server/routes/segment-editor.js:475 maps it: `if (err.code === 'SEGMENT_CONFLICT') { return res.status(409).json({error:'conflict', message: err.message}); }`. Note the `editor_id != ?` clause — the two-DIFFERENT-editors precondition the row names is in the SQL itself. Scope caveats worth recording but not flipping: the cited lock (server/__tests__/segmentEditConflict.test.js:63) asserts err.code, not the 409, so no single test spans service->HTTP; and the only e2e asserting a 409 (server/e2e/concurrent-editing.spec.js:99) is the LOCALIZATION editor's stale-lastModified path, a different mechanism — §4d is likewise absent from the register's machine-verified row list at campaign :126.

### `ed-qa-5a` — CLOSED
**Source:** editorial-workflow-review.md §4, row 5a
**Original severity:** PASS
**Claim:** Anonymous /admin → redirect, no flash of admin UI — PASS, 302 → /login, and because the redirect is server-side no client-render flash is architecturally possible.
**Current location:** `server/e2e/a4-coverage.spec.js:113`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
test('§5a anon /admin redirects to login, no admin DOM painted', … const resp = await context.request.get('/admin', { maxRedirects: 0 }); expect(resp.status()).toBe(302); expect(resp.headers()['location']).toContain('/login'); const body = await resp.text(); … expect(body).not.toContain('data-tab="users"'); … await anon.goto('/admin'); … expect(anon.url()).toContain('/login');
```
**Notes:** Closed by A4 PR-1 (#326, commits 15ce6531 + fix-wave 137ddfc5). Notably this pins the review's ARCHITECTURAL argument, not just its observation: asserting the 302 body carries no admin-only markup (`data-tab="users"`) is a transport-level proof that a flash is impossible — a client-side-only guard would 200 with admin.html and fail here. Upgrades qa-checklist:114's '◐ … the "no visible flash" part is a visual check'.
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — Server-side redirect still in place, so the 'flash of admin UI' remains architecturally impossible. server/routes/views.js:57 `router.get('/admin', requirePageAuth(ROLES.ADMIN), ...)`; requirePageAuth at :28-43 reads the auth_token cookie, and on a missing/invalid token returns `res.redirect(302, '/login?redirect=...')` at :32-35 — before sendView(res,'admin.html') is ever called, so no admin markup is written to the response body. An authenticated-but-under-privileged user is bounced to '/' at :39.
- ✅ upheld — The no-flash property is structural, and the test proves it at the transport layer rather than by observing the browser. server/routes/views.js:57 `router.get('/admin', requirePageAuth(ROLES.ADMIN), (req, res) => sendView(res, 'admin.html'));` — the middleware precedes sendView, so an anonymous request never has admin.html written to its response body. a4-coverage.spec.js:120-126 requests /admin with `maxRedirects: 0` and asserts 302, a Location containing /login, AND that the body does not contain `data-tab="users"` (admin.html-only markup, per the comment at :124-125) — that third assertion is exactly what would fail if the guard were client-side-only, which is the failure mode the row rules out. :129-132 then confirms a real browser lands on /login. Assertions are positive and specific throughout.
- ✅ upheld — The test is stronger than the original curl and covers both sub-claims. a4-coverage.spec.js:113 asserts transport-level `status()===302` with `location` containing '/login' using `maxRedirects: 0`, then — the 'no flash' half — asserts the 302 body does not contain `data-tab="users"`, markup its comment identifies as admin.html-only, which is what rules out a client-side-only guard that would 200 with the admin shell and redirect from JS. It then re-checks at browser level that the anon page actually lands on /login. Scope is correctly bounded: the row names /admin specifically, and /admin is what is tested; sibling page-auth routes (/assignments = HEAD_EDITOR per the Unit-5 bullet in CLAUDE.md) belong to other rows, not this one.

### `ed-qa-5b` — CLOSED
**Source:** editorial-workflow-review.md §4, row 5b
**Original severity:** PASS
**Claim:** State-changing request without a valid session → rejected — PASS, 401.
**Current location:** `server/e2e/rbac.spec.js:151`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
test('§5b state-changing request with no session → rejected', async ({ browser }) => { const context = await browser.newContext({ baseURL: 'http://localhost:3456' }); const anon = await context.newPage(); const resp = await anon.request.post('/api/segment-editor/efnafraedi-2e/1/m68663/apply'); expect([401, 403]).toContain(resp.status()); …
```
**Notes:** Closed by A4 PR-1 (#326). The assertion accepts 401 or 403 rather than pinning the review's observed 401 — slightly looser than the live finding, but either code is a valid rejection and the test still fails on any 2xx.
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — No-session rejection still enforced at the first middleware. POST /:book/:chapter/:moduleId/apply (server/routes/segment-editor.js:1252-1254) mounts requireAuth ahead of requireHeadEditor; server/middleware/requireAuth.js:34-40 returns 401 when neither the auth_token cookie nor a Bearer header supplies a token, and :43-49 returns 401 on an invalid/expired token. req.user is only attached at :52 after both checks, so the downstream 401 fallbacks in requireRole.js:25 and requireHeadEditor requireRole.js:59 are belt-and-braces.
- ✅ upheld — Rejection happens in the first middleware of the chain, before any handler logic. The apply route is `requireAuth, requireHeadEditor(), validateBookChapter, validateModule` (server/routes/segment-editor.js:1252-1256); with no auth_token cookie req.user is unset, and both requireRole-family guards short-circuit on that — server/middleware/requireRole.js:24-29 and :59-64 each `return res.status(401).json({ error: 'Authentication required' ... })`. rbac.spec.js:151-157 builds a genuinely cookie-less context (browser.newContext, no loginAs) and POSTs apply, asserting the status is in [401,403]. The accepted set is looser than the row's observed 401, but both members mean 'rejected', and no success status can satisfy it — the state change cannot occur.
- ✅ upheld — The row was a single-request spot check and the lock reproduces exactly that request. rbac.spec.js:151 builds a FRESH browser context (no injected auth cookie — loginAs is deliberately not called) and POSTs /api/segment-editor/efnafraedi-2e/1/m68663/apply, asserting `[401,403]` contains the status; the route it hits is server/routes/segment-editor.js:1252-1253, where `requireAuth` is the FIRST middleware in the chain, ahead of requireHeadEditor()/validateBookChapter/validateModule — so the rejection provably comes from the session check and nothing downstream. I considered and rejected a scope refutation: I enumerated mutating routes and the only anonymous-tolerant writers are POST /api/analytics/event (routes/analytics.js:79, `optionalAuth`, reader-side telemetry restricted to page_view/chapter_view/section_view/error/search/download with a 2048-char metadata cap) and POST /api/auth/logout — both deliberately anonymous, so citing them would be manufacturing a finding rather than auditing this claim. Worth recording as a note only: there is NO global /api auth gate — requireAuth is applied per-route, and only routes/pipeline.js and routes/pipeline-status.js use router.use(requireAuth) — so this row's guarantee rests on per-route discipline that one probe cannot generalize.

### `ed-w6a` — CLOSED
**Source:** editorial-workflow-review.md §3 · Head-editor persona (LIVE), Bug (a) — first half (the state machine)
**Original severity:** real, reachable through ordinary use, head-editor-facing
**Claim:** No code path anywhere moves a segment_edits row out of `discuss` or `rejected` status once set; a fresh save on a segment carrying a stale discuss row inserts a new row rather than resolving the old one, and migration 008's UNIQUE(book, module_id, segment_id, status, editor_id) then makes re-discussing the new pending edit collide.
**Current location:** `server/migrations/039-segment-edit-exit-path.js:74/102; server/services/segmentEditorService.js:136-141 and :174-179 (supersede-on-save, both branches); :536 returnEditToPending; server/routes/segment-editor.js:838-858`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
039:74 → CHECK status IN ( 'pending', 'approved', 'rejected', 'discuss', 'superseded' )
039:102 → `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_segment_edits_one_pending` (partial, pending-only — the 5-column table UNIQUE is gone)
segmentEditorService.js:138 → `UPDATE segment_edits SET status = 'superseded' WHERE book = ? AND module_id = ? AND segment_id = ? AND editor_id = ? AND status IN ('discuss', 'rejected')` (identical sweep at :175 on the INSERT branch, both inside the save transaction)
segmentEditorService.js:536 → `function returnEditToPending(editId) { … if (edit.status !== 'discuss' && edit.status !== 'rejected') { throw new Error('Edit is not in discuss/rejected status'); } … UPDATE segment_edits SET status = 'pending', reviewer_id = NULL … }`
routes/segment-editor.js:843-847 → `router.post('/edit/:editId/return-to-pending', requireAuth, requireHeadEditorFor(bookFromEditId), … segmentEditor.returnEditToPending(...))`
```
**Notes:** Closed by PR #270 (merge b758dff6, branch fix/segment-edit-exit-path; migration 039 commits 65517860, a2616cdf, 94ac094e). Three independent exits now exist where the review found zero: (1) supersede-on-save in BOTH branches of saveSegmentEdit, with an explicit comment that the update branch's sweep is load-bearing for legacy pre-039 rows; (2) the head-editor manual `return-to-pending` route; (3) the partial index means a discuss row no longer occupies index space at all, so the collision is structurally impossible. Consequence-side improvement too: dashboardReadModel.js:120-126 getUserActionableEdits now surfaces `status IN ('rejected','discuss')` as the editor's own actionable queue, so stranded rows are visible rather than invisible.
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — The described defect is genuinely eliminated, and I verified the one thing that could have made the fix inert — migration registration. (a) 039 IS reached: services/migrationRunner.js lists `require('../migrations/039-segment-edit-exit-path')` in the explicit migrations array (between 038 and 040), so it runs on every boot including a fresh DB. (b) The colliding constraint is gone: 039 rebuilds segment_edits without `UNIQUE(book, module_id, segment_id, status, editor_id)`, adds 'superseded' to the status CHECK (039:74-76), and replaces it with the partial `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX ... idx_segment_edits_one_pending ON segment_edits(book, module_id, segment_id, editor_id) WHERE status = 'pending'` (039:102-104). markForDiscussion (segmentEditorService.js:463-481) is a bare UPDATE to status='discuss' — that index cannot match it, so the raw 'UNIQUE constraint failed: segment_edits.book, ...' text can no longer be produced by re-discussing. (c) The 'no code path moves a row out of discuss/rejected' half is closed twice over: a fresh save sweeps the editor's own stale rows to 'superseded' inside the save transaction on BOTH branches — UPDATE-existing-pending at :136-142 and INSERT at :173-179, both `WHERE ... AND status IN ('discuss','rejected')`; plus an explicit manual exit `returnEditToPending` at :536-573 (guarded by a PENDING_EXISTS check at :545-558) exposed as POST /edit/:editId/return-to-pending at routes/segment-editor.js:841-861. (d) The user-facing harm is closed at the route: that handler maps `err.code === 'PENDING_EXISTS'` to 409 with the Icelandic message 'Yfirlesarinn á nýrri breytingu í bið á þessum bút…', not raw SQL.
- ✅ upheld — Both halves of the defect are mechanically dead. (1) The UNIQUE collision: server/migrations/039-segment-edit-exit-path.js rebuilds segment_edits WITHOUT the 5-column `UNIQUE(book, module_id, segment_id, status, editor_id)` (guard at :38 keys on that exact string) and replaces it with `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX ... idx_segment_edits_one_pending ON segment_edits(book, module_id, segment_id, editor_id) WHERE status = 'pending'` — a partial index, so two rows sharing a non-pending status can no longer collide, and a pending->discuss transition REMOVES the row from the index rather than colliding. 'superseded' is added to the CHECK vocabulary. (2) The 'no exit path' half: saveSegmentEdit now sweeps stale rows on BOTH branches inside the save transaction — segmentEditorService.js:145-150 (UPDATE-existing-pending branch) and :175-180 (INSERT branch), each `UPDATE segment_edits SET status = 'superseded' ... AND status IN ('discuss','rejected')`. Plus a manual head-editor exit at :536 `returnEditToPending` (rejects non-discuss/rejected, refuses with PENDING_EXISTS if a pending sibling exists) exposed at routes/segment-editor.js:844 behind `requireHeadEditorFor`. Replaying the exact live scenario — stale discuss row, save a second revision, click discuss again — the save now flips the stale row to 'superseded' before inserting, and the subsequent discuss touches an unindexed status. The raw SQLite constraint message cannot be produced.
- ✅ upheld — GENUINELY CLOSED — all three prongs of the failure chain are broken, and I enumerated every writer into segment_edits rather than trusting the two cited sites. (1) The constraint: server/migrations/039-segment-edit-exit-path.js rebuilds the table, adds 'superseded' to the status CHECK (:73-75) and replaces the 5-column UNIQUE with a PENDING-ONLY partial index `idx_segment_edits_one_pending ON segment_edits(book, module_id, segment_id, editor_id) WHERE status = 'pending'` (:102-104) — so two 'discuss' rows can no longer collide at all. (2) The exit path now exists in BOTH save branches, not just one: the UPDATE-existing-pending branch runs `UPDATE segment_edits SET status = 'superseded' … AND status IN ('discuss','rejected')` at segmentEditorService.js:136-142 and the INSERT branch runs the identical sweep at :173-179, both inside the save transaction; the sweep is scoped to `editor_id = ?`, which is exactly the scope of the old collision key (status+editor_id), so no sibling case is left uncovered. Plus a manual head-editor exit: returnEditToPending() at :535-571 (discuss/rejected → pending, PENDING_EXISTS 409 guard) behind POST /edit/:editId/return-to-pending at server/routes/segment-editor.js:841-862. (3) markForDiscussion (:463-482) can now only transition FROM 'pending', and no unique index covers 'discuss'. Sibling-writer check: propagationService.createPropagatedEdits is the only other INSERT INTO segment_edits (propagationService.js:86-160); classifyOccurrence (:38-56) returns 'conflict' (→ skip) for any discuss row or another editor's pending, and for the editor's own pending the code UPDATEs in place at :138-144 instead of inserting — so it cannot hit the pending-only index either. unapproveEdit (:499-512) and returnEditToPending both pre-check for an existing pending row and throw a friendly Icelandic 409, so no raw SQLite text reaches the UI on those paths.

### `ed-w6b` — OPEN-UNTRACKED
**Source:** editorial-workflow-review.md §3 · Head-editor persona (LIVE), Bug (a) — second half (the delivery path)
**Original severity:** real, head-editor-facing (component of Bug (a))
**Claim:** The server responded HTTP 400 with a raw SQLite constraint message surfaced verbatim to the user via a browser alert() ("Villa: UNIQUE constraint failed: …"), root-caused in part to markForDiscussion having no try/catch around the constraint violation.
**Current location:** `server/services/segmentEditorService.js:463-482 (markForDiscussion); server/routes/segment-editor.js:802-806; server/public/js/segment-editor.js:1507-1509; server/public/js/ui-strings.js:16`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
segmentEditorService.js:463 → `function markForDiscussion(editId, reviewerId, reviewerUsername, reviewerNote) { const conn = getDb(); … conn.prepare(`UPDATE segment_edits SET status = 'discuss' …`).run(...)` — still no try/catch
routes/segment-editor.js:804 → `} catch (err) { res.status(400).json({ error: err.message }); }`
segment-editor.js:1507 → `} catch (err) { alert(UI.common.errorPrefix + err.message); }` (reviewEdit, used for both reject and discuss)
ui-strings.js:16 → `errorPrefix: 'Villa: ',`
```
**Notes:** The SPECIFIC reproduction is dead — 039's partial index removes the collision (see ed-w6a) — but the delivery mechanism the review named is unchanged: markForDiscussion still has no try/catch, its route still relays `err.message` verbatim in the 400 body, and the client still alert()s 'Villa: ' + that message. Any future SqliteError raised inside markForDiscussion reaches a head-editor's screen as raw SQL text. Notably the codebase DID address this for the two paths that got a designed refusal — unapproveEdit and returnEditToPending build Icelandic user text and set `err.code = 'PENDING_EXISTS'` for a 409 — which shows the fix was applied per-path, not generically. Register greps ('alert(', 'err.message', 'raw error', 'constraint failed', 'user-facing error') across all four sources found only an unrelated UNIQUE-constraint find in the post-item17 campaign (chapterFilesService.registerFiles). Nothing covers raw-error passthrough in the segment editor.

### `ed-w8` — OPEN (flipped by skeptics)
**Source:** editorial-workflow-review.md §3 · Admin persona (LIVE), bullet 1
**Original severity:** n/a — positive confirmation, no defect asserted
**Claim:** Dashboard truthfulness: needsAttention tile counts (pendingReviews, blockedIssues, unassignedWork, overdueCount) checked against live GET /api/status/dashboard JSON and the underlying segment_edits rows — no discrepancy; discuss counted alongside pending in "awaiting decision"; the activity feed reproduced the session's action sequence correctly ordered, attributed and in Icelandic.
**Current location:** `server/routes/status.js:121/197/278; server/services/dashboardReadModel.js:92-99`
**⚠️ Verifier said `CLOSED`; flipped to open by 3/3 skeptics.**
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
status.js:121 → `needsAttention: {` … :278 → `dashboard.needsAttention.pendingReviews = dashboardReadModel.getAdminHeadlineCount();`
dashboardReadModel.js:97 → `.prepare(`SELECT COUNT(*) AS c FROM segment_edits WHERE status = 'pending'`)`
```
**Notes:** NOT a defect claim. The dashboard still derives its headline from segment_edits directly (no fabricated numbers), which is what the review verified. One nuance for the record: the admin headline tile counts `status = 'pending'` only; the review's "discuss counted alongside pending" observation refers to the awaiting-decision framing, and discuss/rejected are now separately surfaced as per-editor actionable work (dashboardReadModel.js:120-126) — a strict improvement, not a regression.
**Adversarial verdicts (3/3 refuted):**
- ❌ REFUTED — The claim does not hold on main, on two independent grounds. (1) One of the four counts it names no longer exists: routes/status.js:120-126 builds `needsAttention` with exactly `pendingReviews`, `blockedIssues`, `unassignedWork`, `items` — there is no `overdueCount`. A repo-wide grep of server/routes, services, public/js and views finds the token `overdue` at ONE place only, routes/segment-editor.js:697 (`else if (ageMs > SLA_WARNING_MS) sla = 'overdue'`), an SLA string label on the review queue, not a dashboard tile. This was a deliberate removal (pre-semester register item 16: 'F28 overdue stat REMOVE', shipped in PR #304), so the claim's object is gone — 'checked against live JSON, no discrepancy' is no longer a checkable statement for that quarter of it. (2) For the surviving counts I can point at code that GUARANTEES a discrepancy rather than code that makes one impossible: routes/status.js:298 sets `dashboard.needsAttention.blockedIssues = discussEdits.length` where `discussEdits = segmentEditorService.getDiscussEdits(10)`, and getDiscussEdits (segmentEditorService.js:1314-1323) is `SELECT ... WHERE status = 'discuss' ORDER BY created_at DESC LIMIT ?` — a truncated row list, not a COUNT. views/my-work.html:1522-1523 renders that list length as a bare stat value (and it also feeds `totalIssues` at :1515-1516). With 11 or more discuss rows the tile permanently reads 10. The fixture the review walked had a handful of rows, which is why the live check agreed; the agreement was an artefact of small data, not of a truthful counter. Register status: I searched all four sources for overdue/overdueCount/blockedIssues/needsAttention/dashboard — the closest line, I16-R5 in docs/plans/2026-07-11-pre-semester-coding-campaign.md:109, covers `needsAttention.items[].assignedTo`/`daysOld` (different fields, the items array not the counts), and nothing in the live campaign docs/plans/2026-07-21-post-item17-followup-campaign.md covers the blockedIssues cap. So the surviving half is untracked.
- ❌ REFUTED — CLOSED is not supportable on two independent grounds, and the verifier's cites (status.js:121/197/278) address neither. (1) SUPERSEDED element: `overdueCount` — one of the four tiles the claim explicitly names as checked against live JSON — no longer exists anywhere in the response. server/routes/status.js:121-125 initializes only `pendingReviews`, `blockedIssues`, `unassignedWork`, `items`; a grep for overdueCount across server/ returns ONLY the negative pins server/__tests__/viewRouteContracts.test.js:148 `expect(src).not.toMatch(/overdueCount/)` and :153 `expect(serverFile('routes/status.js')).not.toMatch(/overdueCount/)`. The field is test-pinned ABSENT. A claim asserting a four-field count matched cannot be 'closed at' code where one of the four fields was deleted. (2) MECHANISM DEFECT survives: status.js:298 `dashboard.needsAttention.blockedIssues = discussEdits.length;` where :297 calls `segmentEditorService.getDiscussEdits(10)`, and segmentEditorService.js:1314-1322 runs `SELECT ... WHERE status = 'discuss' ORDER BY created_at DESC LIMIT ?` with `.all(Math.min(limit, 200))`. The tile is a COUNT derived from a row list hard-capped at 10 — structurally incapable of reporting an 11th discuss row. 'No discrepancy' was observed on the __e2e-fixture__ book holding two discuss rows; it is not a property of the current mechanism on any real book. The correct disposition is partly SUPERSEDED (overdueCount), partly OPEN on the saturating blockedIssues count.
- ❌ REFUTED — NOT CLOSED — the claim's counter set no longer exists on the endpoint, and the verifier's evidence covers 1 of the 4 counters it names. (a) `overdueCount`, explicitly named in the claim as one of the checked tile counts, was DELETED from GET /api/status/dashboard — commit ab64c471 'fix(item16-pr2): F28 — remove dead Tímafrestur overdue stat (absence-pinned)', and server/__tests__/viewRouteContracts.test.js:152-153 now pins its absence: `expect(serverFile('routes/status.js')).not.toMatch(/overdueCount/)`. The dashboard object initialised at server/routes/status.js:120-125 has only pendingReviews, blockedIssues, unassignedWork, items. A state that was removed rather than fixed is SUPERSEDED, not CLOSED, and no future reader can re-walk the claim as written. (b) The remaining truthfulness property is not guaranteed either: status.js:297-299 sets `dashboard.needsAttention.blockedIssues = discussEdits.length` where `discussEdits = segmentEditorService.getDiscussEdits(10)` and getDiscussEdits (segmentEditorService.js:1314-1323) is `SELECT … WHERE status = 'discuss' … LIMIT ?` — so the tile saturates at 10 and understates the real discuss backlog. The walkthrough's fixture DB never exceeded 10, which is why it saw 'no discrepancy'. The verifier's quoted evidence (status.js:278 → getAdminHeadlineCount, dashboardReadModel.js:97 `WHERE status = 'pending'`) supports pendingReviews only and says nothing about blockedIssues, unassignedWork or the deleted overdueCount.

### `ed-w9` — OPEN-TRACKED

**Claim:** The UI "Export CSV" button and the disk-writing glossary-unified.json export — the file api-translate.js actually consumes for MT priming — are two different code paths; the JSON export is a CLI/cron-only script with no route or scheduled-job wiring found anywhere in the repo, and only the CSV path is reachable from the editor UI.
**Current location:** `server/scripts/export-terminology.js:1-14 and :56; docs/technical/architecture.md:433`
**Register item:** CLAUDE.md, "Recent changes (2026-07-26) — Glossary sourcing", blocker (a): "nothing bridges the terminology DB → books/<book>/glossary/glossary-unified.json (which is what api-translate's loadGlossary reads, approvedOnly:true)"
**Evidence:**

```
export-terminology.js:10-14 → "Run from cron (the 2h git-backup already stages books/ …):\n *   node server/scripts/export-terminology.js              # all registered books"
export-terminology.js:56 → `const outPath = path.join(outDir, 'glossary-unified.json');`
architecture.md:433 → `| Glossary export (books/*/glossary/glossary-unified.json) | git | server/scripts/export-terminology.js (cron) + git-backup | nightly + ~2h |`
tools/api-translate.js:624 → `const glossaryPath = path.join(glossaryDir, 'glossary-unified.json');`
```
**Notes:** Still true as stated: `grep -rn export-terminology scripts/ .github/` returns ZERO hits, and no route references it — architecture.md's "(cron)" is a documented intention, not repo wiring. Tracked, but the citation and the finding do not line up perfectly: CLAUDE.md's blocker (a) says *nothing* bridges the DB to glossary-unified.json, whereas the script demonstrably does write exactly that file (terminologyService.js:1513 exportBookGlossary → export-terminology.js:56). What is actually missing is the SCHEDULING/route wiring, not the bridge. The two describe the same operational consequence (the file api-translate primes MT from is not refreshed from the terminology DB automatically) from different angles. Flagging the CLAUDE.md wording tension as a note per scope discipline — resolving it belongs to the glossary track, not this audit.

**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**

- ✅ upheld — Confirmed, but on my own anchors — the verifier's cite (server/routes/admin.js:918-931, `GET /api/admin/users/:id/chapters`) is the wrong endpoint and covers neither the progress figure nor the toggle. The assignments dashboard fetches `/api/admin/assignments/:book` (server/public/js/assignments.js:226). That route is server/routes/admin.js:995 `router.get('/assignments/:book', requireAuth, requireHeadEditor('book'), ...)`, which at :1005-1008 populates `chapterProgress = segmentEditorService.getEditorialProgress(book).chapters` and returns it at :1018 alongside :1019 `enforceAssignments: userService.isAssignmentEnforced(book)`. The client consumes both: :240 `adminData.chapterProgress || {}`, :258 `chapterProgress[ch.chapter]`, rendered at :142-149 `ch.progress.percentComplete`; toggle state at :246 `enforceToggle.checked = !!adminData.enforceAssignments`. The progress value is real and one-decimal — segmentEditorService.js:1462-1468 `chapters[chNum] = { ... percentComplete: chSegments > 0 ? Math.round((edits.approved_segments / chSegments) * 1000) / 10 : 0 }` — which is exactly the shape of the observed '1.2%'. The `|| { percentComplete: 0 }` fallback at :258 is a per-chapter miss guard, not a placeholder for the whole grid, and the key type matches (`chapters[chNum]` numeric). No placeholder literal anywhere on this path.
- ✅ upheld — POSITIVE OBSERVATION, NOT A DEFECT — behaviour confirmed, BUT THE VERIFIER CITED THE WRONG ROUTE and its evidence does not support the claim. server/routes/admin.js:918-931 is GET /api/admin/users/:id/chapters (a per-user assignment list); it returns neither a progress figure nor an enforcement flag, so a reader following that pointer finds nothing about either specific the claim names. The assignments dashboard is GET /api/admin/assignments/:book at server/routes/admin.js:995-1024: `const assignments = userService.getBookAssignments(book)`, `const editors = userService.getEditorsForBook(book)`, `const progress = segmentEditorService.getEditorialProgress(book); chapterProgress = progress.chapters`, and `enforceAssignments: userService.isAssignmentEnforced(book)`. getEditorialProgress (segmentEditorService.js:1391-1436+) computes DISTINCT edited/approved segment counts from segment_edits ∪ segment_acceptances against filesystem segment totals — real data, no placeholder literals. The client consumes both: server/public/js/assignments.js:240/258 renders chapterProgress[ch.chapter].percentComplete and :246 sets the toggle from adminData.enforceAssignments.

**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**

- ✅ upheld — Graceful degradation is a first-statement early return, not an incidental. server/services/segmentEditorService.js:324-325 `async function getModuleSpellFindings(book, chapter, moduleId) { if (!greynirEngine.isEnabled()) return { enabled: false, segments: [] };` — the exact 200 body the walkthrough saw, returned before any sidecar or file work. `isEnabled` is server/services/greynirEngine.js:77 `function isEnabled({ url = process.env.GREYNIR_URL } = {}) { return !!url; }`, so an unset GREYNIR_URL is false. The route (server/routes/segment-editor.js:1172-1186) spreads the result into a plain `res.json`, so with the sidecar absent the response is `{ moduleId, enabled: false, segments: [] }` at HTTP 200. Belt-and-braces: greynirEngine `check()` at :91-98 also returns [] when there is no url and never throws. Nothing on this path can 500 or block a save when the sidecar is down.
- ✅ upheld — POSITIVE OBSERVATION, NOT A DEFECT — graceful degradation still coded, and it is the ONLY spellcheck site (enumerated: greynirEngine is required only by segmentEditorService.js:20 and reached only from segmentEditorService.js:325/330; the sole route is GET /:book/:chapter/:moduleId/spellcheck at server/routes/segment-editor.js:1168-1190). getModuleSpellFindings at segmentEditorService.js:324-334 opens with `if (!greynirEngine.isEnabled()) return { enabled: false, segments: [] };` and isEnabled() at greynirEngine.js:77-79 is `return !!url` with `url = process.env.GREYNIR_URL`. The route responds `res.json({ moduleId, ...result })` — a 200 carrying {enabled:false, segments:[]} when GREYNIR_URL is unset. greynirEngine.check() at :90-100 additionally returns [] rather than throwing when disabled or on transport error.

- ✅ upheld — Both halves of the rebuild affordance survive. Half 1 (flag flips): getApplyStatus recomputes existence from disk on every call — it builds BOOKS_DIR/book/03-faithful-translation/chapterDir(chapter)/<moduleId>-segments.is.md, sets `faithfulExists = fs.existsSync(faithfulPath)` and `canRebuild = !faithfulExists && counts.unapplied_count === 0 && accCounts.unapplied_acceptances === 0 && (counts.applied_count > 0 || accCounts.applied_acceptances > 0)`, returned as `{ ...counts, ...accCounts, faithful_exists, can_rebuild }` (segmentEditorService.js:1181-1200). Deleting the file therefore flips can_rebuild:true on the next poll. Half 2 (re-apply recreates it): applyApprovedEdits' missing-file branch at :880-896 runs `UPDATE segment_edits SET applied_at = NULL WHERE book = ? AND module_id = ? AND status = 'approved' AND applied_at IS NOT NULL`, so the previously-applied edits (including the edit-again content, which is an approved row) become re-appliable and the subsequent write at :1028 recreates the file. The added MAX_APPLY_RETRIES guard (:882-890) only bounds infinite recursion; it does not block the first rebuild.
- ✅ upheld — Both halves still hold, and I specifically checked that the new MAX_APPLY_RETRIES guard does not break the second half. Detection: segmentEditorService.js:1183-1197 in `getApplyStatus` — `faithfulExists = fs.existsSync(faithfulPath); canRebuild = !faithfulExists && counts.unapplied_count === 0 && accCounts.unapplied_acceptances === 0 && (counts.applied_count > 0 || accCounts.applied_acceptances > 0);` returned at :1200 as `{ ...counts, ...accCounts, faithful_exists, can_rebuild }`. So deleting the on-disk file with all edits marked applied still flips can_rebuild:true. Recovery: :882-899 detects the missing file and runs `UPDATE segment_edits SET applied_at = NULL WHERE ... status = 'approved' AND applied_at IS NOT NULL` (plus the segment_acceptances mirror at :902-907), then :908-914 recurses into `applyApprovedEdits` and — critically — calls `_applyRetryState.delete(retryKey)` on BOTH the success return (:910) and the catch/rethrow (:913). Since the per-key counter is cleared either way, `MAX_APPLY_RETRIES = 1` (:808) only bounds infinite recursion within a single call; it does not accumulate across repeated delete-and-rebuild cycles in one process. The recovery path the walkthrough exercised is unchanged in behavior.
- ✅ upheld — POSITIVE OBSERVATION, NOT A DEFECT — both halves survive, including under the new retry guard I checked specifically because it could have broken the second half. Half 1 (flag flips): getApplyStatus at server/services/segmentEditorService.js:1183-1200 stats the 03-faithful-translation path and sets `canRebuild = !faithfulExists && counts.unapplied_count === 0 && accCounts.unapplied_acceptances === 0 && (counts.applied_count > 0 || accCounts.applied_acceptances > 0)`, returned as `can_rebuild` at :1200. Half 2 (re-apply recreates): applyApprovedEdits at :882-913 detects the missing file and resets `UPDATE segment_edits SET applied_at = NULL … status='approved' AND applied_at IS NOT NULL` (plus the segment_acceptances mirror) then recurses. MAX_APPLY_RETRIES = 1 (:808) does NOT disable rebuild: the retry key is deleted on both the success and the error path (:908/:911), so the counter never persists across calls — it only prevents unbounded recursion within a single call.

### `ed-w19` — OPEN-TRACKED
**Source:** editorial-workflow-review.md §3 · Mutating pass (LIVE), Bug (b)
**Original severity:** robustness/observability, explicitly "not a production defect" (all five real slugs start alphanumeric)
**Claim:** TM auto-regeneration silently WARN-skips for a book slug starting with an underscore: generate-tm.js --book __e2e-fixture__ exits non-zero because parseArgs' BOOK_SLUG_PATTERN requires an alphanumeric FIRST character; the failure is logged only as a server-side pino WARN — invisible to humans, absent from the activity log — while the apply's HTTP response still reports unqualified success:true. Fixture-only today; a robustness/observability finding, and the validator's error message is imprecise about which character it rejects.
**Current location:** `tools/lib/parseArgs.js:23 and :33-36; server/services/tmService.js:52-66; server/services/segmentEditorService.js:1129`
**Register item:** I21-R2 (pre-semester campaign register line 230; carried into the LIVE post-item17 campaign at line 99 under P2/C-tail, and into CLAUDE.md + MEMORY.md as a durable rule): "TM auto-regen … a missing row is a LOUD 500 on the route but a SILENT warn-only stale TM on the fire-and-forget cron; onboard new books licence-first."
**Evidence:**

```
parseArgs.js:23 → `const BOOK_SLUG_PATTERN = /^[a-zA-Z0-9][a-zA-Z0-9_-]*$/;`
parseArgs.js:33-35 → `if (!BOOK_SLUG_PATTERN.test(val)) { console.error('Error: --book must be alphanumeric with hyphens/underscores'); process.exit(1); }` — still says nothing about the LEADING character
tmService.js:57-63 → `if (code === 0) { log.info({ book }, 'TM regenerated'); } else { log.warn({ book, code, stderr: … }, 'TM regeneration exited non-zero'); }` and the doc comment at :9-10 "fire-and-forget, so it can never break or slow the apply path (failures are logged, never thrown)"
segmentEditorService.js:1129 → `tmService.scheduleTmRegen(book);` (fire-and-forget; apply's return value carries no TM status)
books/__e2e-fixture__/ still exists on disk
```
**Notes:** Every mechanical element of the claim is intact: the pattern, the imprecise error string, the warn-only branch, and the fire-and-forget call site whose caller returns success:true regardless. The PRIMARY claim — a silent-failure path exists in the TM pipeline, invisible to humans, while the apply reports success — is squarely covered by I21-R2, which names the same regenerateTm warn-only branch and even names `__e2e-fixture__` explicitly (its licence-row instance was fixed in-branch). Different trigger, same silent path, and I21-R2 already proposes "consider a loud cron guard". The two SUB-claims — the leading-character rejection in BOOK_SLUG_PATTERN itself, and the imprecise validator message — are NOT covered by any of the four sources (greps for 'parseArgs', 'BOOK_SLUG', 'alphanumeric', 'underscore', '__e2e-fixture__' returned only fixture-test-isolation items: SR4-F1, I14-R8, C2-R4). I did not split them into separate rows because the review itself caveats them as fixture-only and not a production defect.

### `ed-w20` — CLOSED
**Source:** editorial-workflow-review.md §3 · Mutating pass (LIVE), bullet 7
**Original severity:** n/a — positive confirmation, no defect asserted
**Claim:** The Pass-2 localization round trip with enforce_localization_review ON for the fixture completed cleanly end to end: the editor's save queued rather than direct-saved, the head-editor's approval wrote the real 04-localized-content/ file, and the toggle reset cleanly afterward.
**Current location:** `server/services/localizationReviewService.js:6, :54-56`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
localizationReviewService.js:6 → "`enforce_localization_review` ON, localized edits are held as *pending* edits"
localizationReviewService.js:54-56 → `.prepare(`SELECT enforce_localization_review FROM book_settings WHERE book = ?`) … return !!(row && row.enforce_localization_review);`
```
**Notes:** NOT a defect claim. The per-book toggle and the queue-vs-direct-save branch are unchanged. Adjacent register items exist for edge cases in this service (I12-R3 stranded-approved-rows deploy sanity query; I12-M1 write-then-mark residual) — both are outside this bullet's claim and outside this group's scope.
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — The full Pass-2 enforced round trip is still wired end to end. Toggle read: localizationReviewService.isReviewEnabled (:51-56) `SELECT enforce_localization_review FROM book_settings WHERE book = ?`, coerced with `!!(row && row.enforce_localization_review)`; setReviewEnabled (:58-70) is an upsert, so toggling ON and back OFF is clean and leaves no residue. Queue-instead-of-direct-save: routes/localization-editor.js:380-392 — `// Review tier: when enforced for this book, hold the edit as pending for head-editor approval instead of writing 04-localized-content/ directly.` then `if (localizationReview.isReviewEnabled(req.params.book)) { const submitted = localizationReview.submitEdit({...}) }`; the same branch exists on the save-all path at :585. Approval writes the real file: POST /loc-edit/:editId/approve (routes/localization-editor.js:142-160), `requireHeadEditorFor(bookFromLocEditId)`, calling `localizationReview.approveAndApply(...)` which returns `{ edit, savedPath }` — the service header (:1-14) and :203 document that it applies to 04-localized-content/, and the route surfaces savedPath in the response. Self-approval policy mirrors Pass 1 (:11-14), matching what the walkthrough observed.
- ✅ upheld — Read the actual write path rather than the toggle (the verifier's evidence — the module doc comment at :6 and the toggle read at :54-56 — proved only that the toggle exists). All three legs hold. (1) Queue-vs-direct-save branch: server/routes/localization-editor.js:382 and :586 both branch on `localizationReview.isReviewEnabled(req.params.book)`, with the toggle state exposed at :93/:689; the toggle read is localizationReviewService.js:53-56 `SELECT enforce_localization_review FROM book_settings WHERE book = ?` -> `!!(row && row.enforce_localization_review)`, set/reset by `setReviewEnabled` at :60-70 (upsert), so 'toggle cleanly reset afterward' still works. (2) Approval writes the REAL localized file: localizationReviewService.js:215 `approveAndApply` -> :244 `segmentParser.loadModuleForLocalization(...)`, :245-253 builds the full segment list substituting the approved segment, then :254-260 `const { savedPath } = contentVersionService.saveLocalizedWithSnapshot(edit.book, edit.chapter, edit.module_id, segments, reviewerUsername);` — the single documented write path into 04-localized-content/ (contentVersionService.js:~358 header: 'The single localized write path (item 15) ... All writers of 04-localized-content/ MUST go through this'). (3) The write is ordered BEFORE the status flip (:239-242 comment: 'The file write is the one step a SQLite transaction cannot roll back, so it runs before any status change'), so the approved-but-unapplied stranding risk noted elsewhere in the report is closed here, not reopened. Self-approval parity with Pass 1 is preserved (no reviewer/editor identity guard in approveAndApply).
- ✅ upheld — POSITIVE OBSERVATION, NOT A DEFECT — all three steps of the round trip verified at their real sites, and I enumerated the write path for bypasses (the verifier's evidence covered only the toggle read). Step 1 (queue not direct-save) is enforced at BOTH save routes, not one: server/routes/localization-editor.js:382-404 (single-segment save) and :586-615 (batch save) each do `if (localizationReview.isReviewEnabled(req.params.book)) { … submitEdit(...); res.json({success:true, pending:true, …}); return; }` before ever reaching the direct write. Step 2 (approval writes the real file): localizationReviewService.approveAndApply at :215-300 calls `contentVersionService.saveLocalizedWithSnapshot(...)` at :253 FIRST and only then marks approved/applied. Bypass check: saveLocalizedWithSnapshot has exactly four references repo-wide (definition contentVersionService.js:377, export :412, the two routes at localization-editor.js:407/:621 which sit AFTER the isReviewEnabled early-return, and approveAndApply) — no path writes 04-localized-content/ around the review tier. Step 3 (toggle): isReviewEnabled reads `SELECT enforce_localization_review FROM book_settings WHERE book = ?` (:52-57) and setReviewEnabled upserts and returns the new state (:60-70). NOTE: the verifier named a symbol that does not exist — it is `isReviewEnabled`, not `isLocalizationReviewEnforced`.

### `ed-rec-2` — CLOSED
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-editorial-workflow-review.md §6 rec 2 (drawing on code findings #1 pipeline.js:29, #2 sections.js:627, #4 sections.js:156; also §5 dimension 1 RISK; joint summary §2(a) + Batch 1)
**Original severity:** [JUDGMENT] rank 2 of 6; underlying code findings #1 High/CONFIRMED, #2 High/CONFIRMED, #4 Medium/CONFIRMED — "the only two High findings" in the code review
**Claim:** Close the book-scoping gaps on pipeline.js and sections.js by applying the existing requireHeadEditorFor/requireBookAccess pattern: pipeline.js gates inject/render/run on a global role check only; sections.js gates approve-review/assign-reviewer/assign-localizer/request-changes (and the status route's elevated branch) the same way; and an EDITOR-level upload route writes unscoped into 03-faithful-translation/ and 04-localized-content/.
**Current location:** `server/routes/pipeline.js:69 (also :120, :170); server/routes/sections.js:92, :175, :429, :501, :302-306`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
All three findings closed, each with its own commit naming the finding number. Finding #1 — pipeline.js:24 now imports the scoped guard (`const { requireRole, requireHeadEditorFor, ROLES } = require('../middleware/requireRole');`) and each of the three mutating routes carries `requireHeadEditorFor((req) => req.body?.book),` at :69 (inject), :120 (render), :170 (run); commit 366206b5 "fix(authz): book-scope pipeline inject/render/run (review finding 1)". Finding #2 — all four cited sections.js routes now carry `requireHeadEditorFor((req) => req.sectionData?.bookSlug),`: :92 `/:sectionId/assign-reviewer`, :175 `/:sectionId/assign-localizer`, :429 `/:sectionId/approve-review`, :501 `/:sectionId/request-changes`; and the status route's elevated branch is book-scoped at :302-306 — "const isOwningHeadEditor = req.user.role === ROLES.HEAD_EDITOR && req.user.books?.includes(section.bookSlug); if (req.user.role !== ROLES.ADMIN && !isOwningHeadEditor) { return res.status(403)…`requires a head editor assigned to ${section.bookSlug} (or admin)` }"; commit fc6b8b67 "fix(authz): book-scope section actions + elevated status transitions (review finding 2)". Finding #4 — the route is gone, taking the review's own alternative fix ("or confirm the route is legacy and retire it outright"): `grep -rn "upload/:uploadType" server/` finds it only in a test pin, and commit 69619720 "fix(authz): retire unused section upload route (review finding 4 — write path into protected tiers)" removed 207 lines from sections.js. Pinned by server/__tests__/crossBookAuthz.test.js:512-525: "describe('section upload route is retired (design decision 2026-07-11)' … expect(registeredPaths).not.toContain('/:sectionId/upload/:uploadType') … it('POST → 404 even for admin')".
```
**Notes:** CLOSED is scoped to findings #1/#2/#4 AS WORDED — do not read it as "these two files are now fully book-scoped". Two adjacent gaps survive and are separately tracked, both under C5 (Authz batch 2): (1) sections.js:259-261 `POST /:sectionId/status` still carries route-level `requireRole(ROLES.EDITOR)`, so only the two elevated transitions got scoped — non-elevated transitions remain un-book-scoped = C5 · B1-F2 ("an editor of book A can drive book B's section through editor-level transitions"). (2) pipeline.js:30 still has the blanket `router.use(requireAuth, requireRole(ROLES.HEAD_EDITOR));`, which is now belt-and-braces for the three mutating routes but still the only gate on `GET /jobs` and `GET /jobs/:jobId` = C5 · B1-F5 / I12-R1, issue #328 (per MEMORY.md the agreed fix CHANGED: delete `/jobs` — zero callers — then scope `/jobs/:jobId`, 404 not 403).
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — All three sub-defects independently confirmed unreachable. (a) pipeline.js — `router.use(requireAuth, requireRole(ROLES.HEAD_EDITOR))` at :30 is now supplemented per-route: inject (:67-69), render (:118-120) and run (:168-170) each carry `requireHeadEditorFor((req) => req.body?.book)`. I checked the resolver source matches the handler's: `validateParams` (:35-36) reads `const { book, chapter, track } = req.body`, the SAME `req.body.book` the guard resolves, so there is no param-confusion bypass; and `requireHeadEditorFor` (server/middleware/requireRole.js) fails CLOSED on a falsy resolve (`if (!book) return res.status(404)`), so omitting `book` cannot slip through. (b) sections.js — all four named routes now run `loadSection` BEFORE `requireHeadEditorFor((req) => req.sectionData?.bookSlug)`: assign-reviewer (:88-92), assign-localizer (:171-175 / :423-427 region), approve-review (:425-429 region), request-changes (:497-501 region). `loadSection` (:22-43) 404s if the section is missing and sets `req.sectionData` from `bookRegistration.getSection`, which maps `bookSlug: section.book_slug` — so the resolver has a real slug, and a missing one fails closed with 404. The status route's elevated branch is scoped at :302-306: `const isOwningHeadEditor = req.user.role === ROLES.HEAD_EDITOR && req.user.books?.includes(section.bookSlug); if (req.user.role !== ROLES.ADMIN && !isOwningHeadEditor) return res.status(403)…`. (c) The unscoped upload route is genuinely gone, not merely renamed: `grep -rn 'uploadType|multer|upload.single' server/` returns hits only in routes/terminology.js and routes/books.js — sections.js is 573 lines with ZERO multer/upload references. I checked the only remaining multer write near protected tiers, books.js:547-551 `POST /:bookId/chapters/:chapter/import`, and it is book-scoped (`requireHeadEditor('bookId')`) and writes into 02-for-mt, not 03-faithful-translation/04-localized-content. Caveat noted but out of claim scope: sections.js `/:sectionId/status` non-elevated transitions are still EDITOR-level with no book scoping — finding #2 named only the elevated branch, which IS scoped.
- ✅ upheld — All three constituent findings (#1 pipeline, #2 sections, #4 upload) are dead at the mechanism level, verified by tracing the failure step-by-step.

#1 — server/routes/pipeline.js:24 imports `requireHeadEditorFor` and each mutating route carries it: :69 (/inject), :120 (/render), :170 (/run), all as `requireHeadEditorFor((req) => req.body?.book)`. The resolver reads exactly the field `validateParams` (:35 `const { book, chapter, track } = req.body;`) later destructures, so there is no second book channel to smuggle through. Trace: a HEAD_EDITOR of efnafraedi-2e POSTs `{book:'liffraedi-2e', chapter:3}` → requireRole passes (router.use :30) → requireHeadEditorFor: not ADMIN (requireRole.js:119), resolveBook returns 'liffraedi-2e', `req.user.books.includes('liffraedi-2e')` false (:135) → 403 at requireRole.js:139, before `pipeline.hasRunningJob` at :75 is ever reached. Fail-closed on `req.user.books` undefined (`&&` short-circuits to the 403). Commit 366206b5 exists and is titled for finding 1.

#2 — server/routes/sections.js: `loadSection` (:22-43) sets `req.sectionData = section` and runs BEFORE the guard on all four routes: :88/:92 assign-reviewer, :171/:175 assign-localizer, :425/:429 approve-review, :497/:501 request-changes — each `requireHeadEditorFor((req) => req.sectionData?.bookSlug)`. So the section ID guess the failure scenario relies on now resolves the owning book and compares it. The status route's elevated branch is scoped inline at :301-312: `const isOwningHeadEditor = req.user.role === ROLES.HEAD_EDITOR && req.user.books?.includes(section.bookSlug); if (req.user.role !== ROLES.ADMIN && !isOwningHeadEditor) { return res.status(403)... }` — reached before `bookRegistration.updateSectionStatus` at :315. Commit fc6b8b67 exists. CAVEAT (stated so this closure is not read as blessing the whole route): the NON-elevated branch of POST /:sectionId/status is still only `requireRole(ROLES.EDITOR)` at :261, so a chemistry EDITOR can still drive a biology section through e.g. mt_uploaded→review_assigned. That is outside this claim (finding #2's Fix says 'extend the same ownership check to the status route's ELEVATED branch') — logged as a note, not a refutation.

#4 — the route is gone, taking the review's own stated alternative ('or confirm the route is legacy and retire it outright'). `grep -n upload server/routes/sections.js` returns only the three status-string matches 'mt_uploaded' (:105, :277, :278); no multer, no upload route. Commit 69619720 exists. I then attacked the harm rather than the URL: `grep -rn "upload.single|multer|03-faithful-translation|04-localized-content" server/routes/` finds no sibling unscoped writer — the only multer upload routes are books.js:547 `/:bookId/chapters/:chapter/import` guarded by `requireHeadEditor('bookId')` and terminology.js (CSV, unrelated dirs); books.js:521 and :364 are read paths (`readdirSync` / GET download), and :510 is `requireBookAccess()`. Pinned by server/__tests__/crossBookAuthz.test.js:512-525 (router introspection + POST→404 for admin).
- ✅ upheld — Every site the claim names is scoped, and I found no sibling site keeping the defect. (a) pipeline.js:24 imports requireHeadEditorFor and all three mutating routes carry `requireHeadEditorFor((req) => req.body?.book)` — :69 /inject, :120 /render, :170 /run. No TOCTOU: the guard reads req.body.book and validateParams (:35 `const { book, chapter, track } = req.body;`) reads the same field, so the value authorized is the value used. requireHeadEditorFor (middleware/requireRole.js:101-146) is genuinely book-intersecting: admin bypass, then `req.user.books.includes(book)` else 403, and falsy book → 404. (b) All four sections.js routes now run loadSection BEFORE `requireHeadEditorFor((req) => req.sectionData?.bookSlug)` — :89 assign-reviewer, :172 assign-localizer, :426 approve-review, :498 request-changes. The status route's elevated branch is scoped inline at sections.js:302-312: `const isOwningHeadEditor = req.user.role === ROLES.HEAD_EDITOR && req.user.books?.includes(section.bookSlug); if (req.user.role !== ROLES.ADMIN && !isOwningHeadEditor) return res.status(403)`. (c) The upload route is gone — `grep -rn "upload/:uploadType" server/` returns only the negative test pin at server/__tests__/crossBookAuthz.test.js:519. Commits 366206b5, fc6b8b67, 69619720 all exist and name the finding numbers. SCOPE ATTACK (the reason this is not refuted): I enumerated the actual harm the finding named — unscoped writes into 03-faithful-translation/ and 04-localized-content/ — rather than trusting the route deletion. The only production writers are segmentParser.saveModuleSegments and saveLocalizedSegments; their callers are applyApprovedEdits (segment-editor.js:1252 requireHeadEditor(), :1296 apply-and-render requireHeadEditor(), :887 review-complete auto-apply behind requireHeadEditorFor), contentVersionService restore (segment-editor.js:1427-1430 requireHeadEditor(); localization-editor.js:855-857 requireHeadEditorGuard), and the localization save paths (localization-editor.js:295-298 /save and :471-474 /save-all, both requireBookAccess()). No unscoped writer survives. I also checked for sibling exposures of the same actions (grep approve-review/assign-reviewer/assign-localizer/request-changes across server/routes + index.js → sections.js only; bookRegistration.updateSectionStatus/assignLocalizer have no other route caller) and the other multer route, books.js:546-549 /:bookId/chapters/:chapter/import, which carries requireHeadEditor('bookId') and writes to 02-for-mt, not a protected tier. RESIDUAL, out of the claim's stated scope: the NON-elevated branch of POST /:sectionId/status (sections.js:258-262) is still `requireRole(ROLES.EDITOR)` + loadSection with no book intersection, so an editor of one book can drive another book's section through the ordinary transitions. The claim and finding #2's own code block cite only the elevated branch (:506-514 in baseline terms), so this does not sustain refutation — flagged in notes.

### `ed-rec-3` — CLOSED
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-editorial-workflow-review.md §6 rec 3 (from §3 head-editor persona bug (a); §5 dimension 1 RISK; joint summary §2(b) + Batch 2)

**Claim:** Give the discuss/rejected segment-edit states a resolution path: no code path moves a segment_edits row out of discuss or rejected, so a re-save + re-discuss surfaced a raw `UNIQUE constraint failed: segment_edits.book, …, segment_edits.status, segment_edits.editor_id` verbatim to a head-editor via a browser alert(). Minimum fix = try/catch returning a clean Icelandic message; fuller fix = an explicit transition out of discuss.
**Current location:** `server/migrations/039-segment-edit-exit-path.js:101-104; server/services/segmentEditorService.js:536-572; server/routes/segment-editor.js:840-862; server/services/segmentEditorService.js:129-142`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
Both the minimum and the fuller fix shipped, and the root cause was removed at the schema level. (a) Root cause gone — migration 039 rebuilt the table, replacing 008's five-column UNIQUE with a partial index on pending only: server/migrations/039-segment-edit-exit-path.js:101-104 "CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_segment_edits_one_pending ON segment_edits(book, module_id, segment_id, editor_id) WHERE status = 'pending';". Its header (:4-11) names this exact review: "The 008 table-level UNIQUE(book, module_id, segment_id, status, editor_id) made every repeat transition into an occupied status collide with a raw SQLite error (re-discuss was live-reproduced… ). The only load-bearing invariant is one PENDING edit per (book, module, segment, editor) — keep exactly that as a partial unique index. 'superseded' joins the status vocabulary so a stale discuss/rejected row can be resolved by a newer save without deleting history." Commits 6b5a7563, 65517860, a2616cdf (2026-07-12). (b) Explicit exit transition — segmentEditorService.js:536-572 `returnEditToPending(editId)`, documented at :531-535 as the "head-editor manual exit path", guarded `if (edit.status !== 'discuss' && edit.status !== 'rejected') throw new Error('Edit is not in discuss/rejected status')`, exposed at server/routes/segment-editor.js:840-843 `router.post('/edit/:editId/return-to-pending', requireAuth, requireHeadEditorFor(bookFromEditId), …)` with a clean status split at :860 `res.status(err.code === 'PENDING_EXISTS' ? 409 : 400)`, and reachable in the UI (server/public/js/segment-editor.js:865 "&#8634; Opna aftur" → :1530 `fetchJson(…/return-to-pending)`; ui-strings.js:412 `reopenEdit: 'Opna aftur til yfirferðar'`). Commits 33354b2d, 77625b50. (c) Automatic healing on save — segmentEditorService.js:129-142 runs, inside the save transaction, "UPDATE segment_edits SET status = 'superseded' WHERE book = ? AND module_id = ? AND segment_id = ? AND editor_id = ? AND status IN ('discuss', 'rejected')", with an inline comment naming the legacy case: "pre-039 saves never superseded a stale discuss/rejected row, so production can hold a pending row that COEXISTS with an older stranded discuss/rejected row… Without this sweep… returnEditToPending would keep 409ing with PENDING_EXISTS forever." (d) The one clean-message example the review asked for exists verbatim at :554-557 — `'Yfirlesarinn á nýrri breytingu í bið á þessum bút — farið yfir þá breytingu í staðinn.'`

**Notes:** Two residuals, neither in scope for this recommendation as worded. (1) The service still throws bare English for the ordinary guard cases — segmentEditorService.js:467 `throw new Error('Edit is not pending')`, :539 `'Edit is not in discuss/rejected status'` — and the routes return `{ error: err.message }` straight to the client's alert(). So the class of "an English/technical string reaches a head-editor's screen" is narrowed (no more raw SQL) but not eliminated; I found no register item naming these specific strings. (2) A stranded CROSS-editor discuss row now blocks MT acceptance instead of erroring — that is the newer C6 · MTA-R9 ("the DISCUSS_OPEN acceptance guard matches ANY discuss row with no recency scoping… Scope via pickLatest or add a resolve affordance"), and C6 · MTA-R8 separately notes returnEditToPending does not call acceptanceService.supersedeForEdit.
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — The root cause is removed at the schema level, so the described failure is structurally impossible, not merely message-wrapped. Migration 039 (server/migrations/039-segment-edit-exit-path.js) rebuilds segment_edits and replaces 008's table-level `UNIQUE(book, module_id, segment_id, status, editor_id)` (still visible at 008-segment-editing.js:39, the sole surviving occurrence) with a PARTIAL index: `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_segment_edits_one_pending ON segment_edits(book, module_id, segment_id, editor_id) WHERE status = 'pending'`. I grepped all migrations 040-043 — none re-adds the five-column UNIQUE, and 039's guard keys on that exact string so it runs on any DB still holding it. Consequence: two rows in `discuss` (or `rejected`) for the same editor+segment no longer collide at all, which is precisely the re-save+re-discuss sequence the review reproduced. Beyond that, both requested fixes exist: the fuller fix is `returnEditToPending(editId)` at segmentEditorService.js:536-572 (guarded `if (edit.status !== 'discuss' && edit.status !== 'rejected') throw`), exposed at routes/segment-editor.js:840-843 `router.post('/edit/:editId/return-to-pending', requireAuth, requireHeadEditorFor(bookFromEditId), …)` with `res.status(err.code === 'PENDING_EXISTS' ? 409 : 400).json({ error: err.message })`; and the clean Icelandic message the review asked for is the thrown text itself — 'Yfirlesarinn á nýrri breytingu í bið á þessum bút — farið yfir þá breytingu í staðinn.' I also verified the save path can no longer strand rows: BOTH branches of `saveEdit` (segmentEditorService.js:129-142 update-existing and :164-176 insert) run `UPDATE segment_edits SET status = 'superseded' … AND status IN ('discuss','rejected')` inside the save transaction, and 'superseded' is in the new CHECK vocabulary.
- ✅ upheld — The raw `UNIQUE constraint failed: segment_edits...` cannot be produced by the described sequence any more, because the constraint that produced it no longer exists.

Root cause removed at the schema level: server/migrations/008-*.js:39 created `UNIQUE(book, module_id, segment_id, status, editor_id)`; server/migrations/039-segment-edit-exit-path.js rebuilds the table (guarded at :38-40 on that exact string still being in sqlite_master, so it fires on both an upgraded prod DB and a fresh clone where 008 runs first) and replaces it with a partial index at :101-104: `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_segment_edits_one_pending ON segment_edits(book, module_id, segment_id, editor_id) WHERE status = 'pending';`. It is wired into the runner at server/services/migrationRunner.js:71.

Trace of the reproduced bug: editor saves (row A pending) → HE discusses (A→discuss) → editor re-saves → HE discusses the new row. The colliding write was `markForDiscussion`'s `UPDATE segment_edits SET status='discuss'` (segmentEditorService.js:469-479). Under the partial index, only status='pending' rows are constrained, so two 'discuss' rows on the same (book, module, segment, editor) are now legal and that UPDATE cannot collide. The second step of the trace is additionally removed: the re-save supersedes the stale row first — segmentEditorService.js:136-142 (update-existing branch) and :171-179 (insert branch), both inside the save transaction: `UPDATE segment_edits SET status='superseded' WHERE book=? AND module_id=? AND segment_id=? AND editor_id=? AND status IN ('discuss','rejected')`, with 'superseded' added to the CHECK vocabulary at 039:76-78.

The review's 'fuller fix' also shipped: `returnEditToPending(editId)` at segmentEditorService.js:536-572 (guard `if (edit.status !== 'discuss' && edit.status !== 'rejected') throw`; :540-541), exposed at server/routes/segment-editor.js:841-844 `router.post('/edit/:editId/return-to-pending', requireAuth, requireHeadEditorFor(bookFromEditId), ...)` with `res.status(err.code === 'PENDING_EXISTS' ? 409 : 400)` at :860, and reachable from the UI (server/public/js/segment-editor.js:865 '&#8634; Opna aftur' → :1530 POST). The clean Icelandic message the 'minimum fix' asked for is at :554-556: 'Yfirlesarinn á nýrri breytingu í bið á þessum bút — farið yfir þá breytingu í staðinn.' `unapproveEdit` carries the mirror guard (:493-500 comment + code). Commits 6b5a7563, 65517860, a2616cdf, 33354b2d, 77625b50 all exist, dated 2026-07-12.
- ✅ upheld — The claim's failure scenario is dead at the schema level, not merely papered over. Root cause: migration 039-segment-edit-exit-path.js rebuilds segment_edits, dropping 008's `UNIQUE(book, module_id, segment_id, status, editor_id)` (still visible at 008-segment-editing.js:39, which is why 039's guard at :37 keys on it) and replacing it with a pending-only partial index at :101-104 — `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_segment_edits_one_pending ON segment_edits(book, module_id, segment_id, editor_id) WHERE status = 'pending';`. I confirmed no other migration reintroduces a status-bearing UNIQUE on that table (only 036 tm_segments and 031 content_versions have unrelated ones). I traced the reproduced sequence against current code: save → pending; discuss → UPDATE to 'discuss'; re-save takes the INSERT branch which first sweeps `UPDATE segment_edits SET status = 'superseded' … AND status IN ('discuss','rejected')` (segmentEditorService.js:174-179, inside the same conn.transaction as the INSERT) → one pending row; re-discuss is an UPDATE into a status the index does not constrain. The collision cannot occur. The explicit exit path exists and is reachable end to end: returnEditToPending (segmentEditorService.js:536-572, guarded `if (edit.status !== 'discuss' && edit.status !== 'rejected') throw`), route segment-editor.js:841-844 `router.post('/edit/:editId/return-to-pending', requireAuth, requireHeadEditorFor(bookFromEditId), …)` with `res.status(err.code === 'PENDING_EXISTS' ? 409 : 400)`, and a real UI path — segment-editor.js:864 renders the '&#8634; Opna aftur' button only for isHeadEditor && (discuss||rejected) && !applied_at, calling reopenEdit (:1528), which IS exposed for the inline onclick at :2903 `window.reopenEdit = reopenEdit;` (I checked this specifically because the file is IIFE-wrapped — an unexposed function would have made the exit path dead). The clean Icelandic message the claim asked for is at :554-556: 'Yfirlesarinn á nýrri breytingu í bið á þessum bút — farið yfir þá breytingu í staðinn.' Legacy pre-039 rows self-heal via the same sweep on the UPDATE branch (:137-142). NOTE, not sufficient to refute: returnEditToPending is not transactional — its pending SELECT (:546-553) and its UPDATE (:561-570) are separate statements, so a concurrent save landing between them would violate idx_segment_edits_one_pending and the route's `res.json({ error: err.message })` plus reopenEdit's `alert(UI.common.errorPrefix + err.message)` would put a raw SQLite UNIQUE string in front of a head-editor. That is a race, whereas the claim describes a deterministic single-user sequence that is now impossible.

(b) It cannot regress — server/__tests__/activityLogCallsiteGuard.test.js recursively scans server/routes/ AND server/services/ and asserts three patterns per file: `SILENT_CATCH = /catch\s*(?:\([^)]*\)\s*)?\{\s*\/\*\s*fire-and-forget\s*\*\/\s*\}/`, `TRY_WRAPPED_AUDIT = /try\s*\{\s*activityLog\.log\(/`, `EMPTY_CATCH_NO_COMMENT = /catch\s*(?:\([^)]*\)\s*)?\{\s*\}/` — "it(`${rel}: no try-wrapped activityLog.log (it is never-throw)`)". The approve/reject/unapprove sites the finding named are now bare calls inside the ordinary handler try — e.g. the reject route at routes/segment-editor.js:748-773 calls `activityLog.log({ type: 'segment_edit_rejected', … })` with no inner try/catch. String(req.user.id) : null) }` — so the value is not perpetually null.
