### `ed-rec-6` — CLOSED
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-editorial-workflow-review.md §6 rec 6 (from §3 Mutating pass "Version-numbering nuance"; §5 dimension 6 GAP)
**Original severity:** [JUDGMENT] rank 6 of 6 — explicitly scoped as "worth checking before it's worth building"; the underlying semantics were live-discovered but NOT asserted as a UI bug
**Claim:** Spend two minutes checking whether the "Saga útgáfa" restore modal already disambiguates version numbering: version rows are pre-write snapshots, not post-write results, so "version 1 = my first change" is backwards and a head-editor restoring the lowest-numbered version could silently discard the newest approved edit. If the modal doesn't already make this clear, the fix is a one-line copy change ("this was the content *before* this version's edit"), not new logic.
**Current location:** `server/views/segment-editor.html:1605; server/views/localization-editor.html:2065`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
The check was done and the copy change shipped — in both history modals, not just the segment editor's. server/views/segment-editor.html:1598-1606: "<!-- Version history modal (HEAD_EDITOR only) --> … <h3 id=\"vh-title\">Útgáfusaga</h3> … <p class=\"vh-caption\">Hver útgáfa sýnir efnið eins og það var <strong>áður en</strong> viðkomandi vistun átti sér stað.</p>" — literally the review's proposed sentence ("Each version shows the content as it was BEFORE that save took place"). The identical caption is at server/views/localization-editor.html:2065, with the shared `.vh-caption` style at segment-editor.html:1359 and localization-editor.html:1374. Commit 4bc9d86f (2026-07-18) "fix(item15): I12-R4 — version-history modals lead with actor+date and state pre-write snapshot semantics" — note the plural "modals", matching the two sites. The reversibility half is separately reinforced in the confirm dialog at server/public/js/segment-editor.js:2380-2382: "'Færa þessa einingu aftur í útgáfu ' + … '?\n\nNúverandi efni er fyrst vistað sem ný útgáfa, svo þetta er afturkræft.'" and again in the success toast at :2399-2401.
```
**Notes:** This is the one recommendation whose deliverable was a CHECK rather than a fix; the check resolved against the modal (it did not already disambiguate) and the one-line copy change followed, so the recommendation is discharged in full. The attribution is firm — the commit message names the exact semantic ("state pre-write snapshot semantics") and the register id I12-R4. Note the fix generalized beyond what the review asked: the localization editor's history modal was never inspected by the review but got the same caption.
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — The recommendation was explicitly conditional ('check first; if it doesn't disambiguate, the fix is a one-line copy change, not new logic'), and the copy change is present in both history modals. server/views/segment-editor.html:1605 and server/views/localization-editor.html:2065 both carry, verbatim and identically: `<p class="vh-caption">Hver útgáfa sýnir efnið eins og það var <strong>áður en</strong> viðkomandi vistun átti sér stað.</p>` — i.e. 'each version shows the content as it was BEFORE that save', the exact semantics the review said were backwards. The caption is inside the live modal markup (`<div class="vh-modal" role="dialog" aria-modal="true" …>` at segment-editor.html:1600, `<h3 id="vh-title">Útgáfusaga</h3>` at :1602) with its `.vh-caption` style at :1359 / :1374, so it renders above the list rather than sitting in dead markup. I also confirmed the list this modal fills is the one the user acts on and that it does not reintroduce the confusion: server/public/js/segment-editor.js:2321 targets `#vh-list`, and :2337-2360 renders each row leading with actor+date (`who + ' · ' + when`) then `'Útgáfa ' + v.version`, tagging the newest with `' · nýjasta'` — so the lowest number is not presented as 'my first change'. The residual-risk step the review worried about (silently discarding the newest approved edit) is further covered by the confirm at :2379-2382, 'Núverandi efni er fyrst vistað sem ný útgáfa, svo þetta er afturkræft.'
- ✅ upheld — The recommendation was explicitly conditional — check the modal, and if it does not already disambiguate, make a one-line copy change, 'not new logic'. The copy shipped, verbatim in the sense the review specified.

server/views/segment-editor.html:1604, inside the '<!-- Version history modal (HEAD_EDITOR only) -->' block (:1597-1607) and above `<div class="vh-list" id="vh-list">`: `<p class="vh-caption">Hver útgáfa sýnir efnið eins og það var <strong>áður en</strong> viðkomandi vistun átti sér stað.</p>` — i.e. 'each version shows the content as it was BEFORE that save took place', the review's proposed sentence. The same caption is at server/views/localization-editor.html:2065. The `.vh-caption` rule (segment-editor.html:1359, localization-editor.html:1374) sets only margin/padding/font-size/color/border — it is not hidden.

Attacking the concrete failure ('a head-editor restoring the lowest-numbered version could SILENTLY DISCARD the newest approved edit'): the caption removes the 'version 1 = my first change' misreading; server/public/js/segment-editor.js:2337-2343 additionally labels the top row ' · nýjasta' and leads each row with actor+date (`v.applied_by`, `vhFormatDate(v.applied_at)`), so the newest is identified rather than inferred from the number; and the 'silently' half is separately defeated at :2380-2382 `window.confirm('Færa þessa einingu aftur í útgáfu ' + version + '?\n\nNúverandi efni er fyrst vistað sem ný útgáfa, svo þetta er afturkræft.')` plus the toast at :2397-2404 naming `result.snapshotVersion` — the discarded content is snapshotted first, so it is neither silent nor lost.

Attribution correction to the verifier (does not change the disposition, which tracks code state): commit 4bc9d86f (2026-07-18) touched only server/public/js/segment-editor.js and server/views/segment-editor.html (2 files, per `git show --stat`); the localization-editor caption came from a7f91253 ('feat(item15): Útgáfusaga modal in localization editor'), i.e. it was built in, not added by 4bc9d86f. Both captions are on main today.
- ✅ upheld — The claim's recommended action was to check the modal and, if it did not disambiguate, add one line of copy. That copy exists at BOTH history modals — the scope point, since a fix in the segment editor alone would have left the localization editor's identical modal misleading: server/views/segment-editor.html:1605 and server/views/localization-editor.html:2065 both read `<p class="vh-caption">Hver útgáfa sýnir efnið eins og það var <strong>áður en</strong> viðkomandi vistun átti sér stað.</p>`, with the shared style at segment-editor.html:1359 / localization-editor.html:1374. I checked that these are the only two version-history entry points (grep 'Útgáfusaga' → segment-editor.html:1500 trigger + :1602 modal, localization-editor.html:1551 trigger + :2062 modal). Critically for a copy fix, I verified the caption is not wiped by the JS that populates the modal: in both files the <p class="vh-caption"> is a SIBLING of the list div (<div class="vh-list" id="vh-list"> at segment-editor.html:1606, id="ed-vh-list" at localization-editor.html:2066), and the JS only assigns list.innerHTML (segment-editor.js:2321/2323/2340, localization-editor.js:1280) — so repopulation cannot remove the sentence. The claim's second worry (silently discarding the newest edit) is separately mitigated: restore snapshots current content first, stated in the confirm at segment-editor.js:2380-2382 ('Núverandi efni er fyrst vistað sem ný útgáfa, svo þetta er afturkræft.') and echoed in the success toast at :2399-2401, and each row leads with actor+date before the version number (:2340-2352, with ' · nýjasta' marking the newest). NOTE on the verifier's supporting evidence, not the claim: the 'lead with date' half depends on vhFormatDate, which I did not verify against the blank/Invalid-Date defect class reported separately as findings #26/#27; the caption sentence — which is what this claim actually asked for — does not depend on it.

The only load-bearing invariant is one PENDING edit per (book, module, segment, editor) — keep exactly that as a partial unique index. 'superseded' joins the status vocabulary so a stale discuss/rejected row can be resolved by a newer save`, plus the explicit exit route segment-editor.js:840-846 `POST /edit/:editId/return-to-pending ... Return a discussed/rejected edit to pending for re-review (manual exit path)` backed by segmentEditorService.js:536-543 `function returnEditToPending(editId) { ... if (edit.status !== 'discuss' && edit.status !== 'rejected') throw new Error('Edit is not in discuss/rejected status'); }` and a clean 409/400 instead of a raw constraint string (`res.status(err.code === 'PENDING_EXISTS' ? 409 : 400).json({ error: err.message })`).

### `ed-dim-2` — OPEN-TRACKED
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-editorial-workflow-review.md §5 dimension 2 ("TM lifecycle — GAP", MIXED: LIVE + CODE-READ) and §3 Mutating pass bug (b)
**Original severity:** GAP (bug (b) explicitly scoped fixture-only / robustness-observability, not a production defect)
**Claim:** Project-wide TM thinness (one book with a 3-unit TMX, four books with none) restated as known context; what the review adds is that the auto-regeneration mechanism does fire but can fail with zero human-visible signal — live, tools/generate-tm.js rejected the fixture slug __e2e-fixture__ because parseArgs' BOOK_SLUG_PATTERN requires an alphanumeric first character, and the failure surfaced only as a server-side pino WARN while the apply's own HTTP response still reported unqualified success:true.
**Current location:** `tools/lib/parseArgs.js:23,33-36; server/services/tmService.js:53-66; books/*/tm/ (on-disk state)`
**Register item:** C8 · I21-R2 (live campaign, docs/plans/2026-07-21-post-item17-followup-campaign.md:99): "TM auto-regen ... LOUD 500 on /api/tm/export but SILENT warn-only stale TM on the cron. Onboard-books-licence-first is the rule; consider a loud cron guard." Also C3 · I20-R1 (same file:66): git-backup.sh stages neither books/*/tm/ nor books/*/glossary/, so the TMX is not actually durable.
**Evidence:**

```
The slug validator is byte-for-byte unchanged: tools/lib/parseArgs.js:23 `const BOOK_SLUG_PATTERN = /^[a-zA-Z0-9][a-zA-Z0-9_-]*$/;` and :33-36 `if (!BOOK_SLUG_PATTERN.test(val)) { console.error('Error: --book must be alphanumeric with hyphens/underscores'); process.exit(1); }` — still requires an alphanumeric FIRST character while the message names no character, so `__e2e-fixture__` still exits non-zero with an imprecise error. The silent-failure path is also unchanged: tmService.js:55-63 `const { code, stderr } = await runner(book); if (code === 0) { log.info(...) } else { log.warn({ book, code, stderr: ... }, 'TM regeneration exited non-zero'); }` inside a function whose own doc comment at :12 reads `fire-and-forget, so it can never break or slow the apply path (failures are logged, never thrown)` — a WARN, no activity-log entry, no change to the apply response. The data picture is also unchanged: `ls books/*/tm/` still yields exactly one TMX, `books/efnafraedi-2e/tm/efnafraedi-2e-2026-06-13.tmx`, with every other book's tm/ empty or absent (faithful-module counts: efnafraedi-2e 3, all other real books 0).
```
**Notes:** The register covers the observability half of bug (b) exactly (a silent warn-only TM-regen failure with a proposed loud cron guard) and the durability half (I20-R1) — I verified the latter directly: scripts/git-backup.sh PATHSPECS (:75-83) list 03-faithful-translation, 03-translated, 04-localized-content, 04-localization, 05-publication, chapters, translation-errors.json, residue-report — no tm/ and no glossary/. The narrow fixture-slug TRIGGER itself (BOOK_SLUG_PATTERN's alphanumeric-first rule) is named in no register, but the review itself scoped that as fixture-only and unreachable for all five real book slugs, so it is not a separate untracked defect. Dimension is MIXED (LIVE + CODE-READ); its GAP verdict is not re-scorable by code reading — A4's walk.

### `ed-dim-3` — OPEN-TRACKED
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-editorial-workflow-review.md §5 dimension 3 ("Terminology governance loop — GAP", MIXED: LIVE + CODE-READ), with §2 'DOCS-AHEAD, terminology CSV is disconnected from live MT quality'
**Original severity:** GAP
**Claim:** The human-facing half of the terminology loop is LIVE-verified end to end (create → approve → export-CSV), but the machine-facing half that actually determines MT quality — the glossary-unified.json export api-translate.js reads — is CLI/cron-only with no route or scheduled-job wiring found anywhere in the repo, so its automatic ongoing operation is unconfirmed; compounding this, editors are told a stale CSV is "the authoritative source for approved terminology" when it has no live effect at all.
**Current location:** `server/scripts/export-terminology.js:1-15,54-56; server/services/terminologyService.js:1524; tools/api-translate.js:624; docs/editorial/terminology.md:220`
**Register item:** CLAUDE.md, "Recent changes (2026-07-26) — Glossary sourcing", blocker (a): "nothing bridges the terminology DB → books/<book>/glossary/glossary-unified.json (which is what api-translate's loadGlossary reads, approvedOnly:true)"; mirrored in MEMORY.md SESSION 2026-07-26 "▶ NEXT [CODE]: (1) the 2 glossary blockers". Related but NOT covering: C3 · I20-R1 (glossary/ not staged by git-backup) and C7 · I19-R1/R3/R4 (approvedOnly leak, book-scoping, approved-translation edits) — none of the three C7 sub-items is about export wiring.
**Evidence:**

```
The export is still a bare CLI script with ZERO callers anywhere in the repo: a repo-wide grep for `export-terminology` across server/, tools/, scripts/, package.json and .github/ returns only the script's own path — no route, no npm script, no workflow, no cron file in-tree. Its own header states the intended (external, unverifiable-from-code) wiring, server/scripts/export-terminology.js:5-13: `books/<book>/glossary/glossary-unified.json — the file tools/api-translate.js feeds to Málstaður as the MT glossary (Unit 6.1). The committed export had been stale since 2026-03-09 because nothing regenerated it. Run from cron`. The consumer is unchanged — tools/api-translate.js:624 `const glossaryPath = path.join(glossaryDir, 'glossary-unified.json');` — and the on-disk exports are still old (books/efnafraedi-2e and books/liffraedi-2e glossary-unified.json both dated Mar 11, lifraen-efnafraedi Mar 23). The documentation half is verbatim unchanged: docs/editorial/terminology.md:220 `These CSV files are the authoritative source for approved terminology.` (file last touched 3c39e161, 2026-03-02 — four months before the review).
```
**Notes:** ⚠️ Correction worth surfacing: CLAUDE.md's blocker (a) as literally worded — "nothing bridges the terminology DB → glossary-unified.json" — is OVERSTATED, and my own evidence refutes it. A bridge SCRIPT does exist (server/scripts/export-terminology.js:54 `const data = terminologyService.exportBookGlossary(b);` writing to books/<b>/glossary/glossary-unified.json). What is missing is anything that RUNS it — which is precisely the review's actual claim (CLI/cron-only, no route or scheduled-job wiring). I chose OPEN-TRACKED because the register item aims at the same seam, but a reader must not inherit the 'no bridge exists' premise. The terminology.md "authoritative CSV" documentation half is tracked NOWHERE — it is folded into the untracked documentation-corpus finding at row ed-dim-8, not double-counted here. Dimension is MIXED; its GAP verdict is not re-scorable by code reading (the live create→approve→export-CSV loop was a live positive) — A4's walk.

### `ed-dim-4` — CLOSED
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-editorial-workflow-review.md §5 dimension 4 ("QA gates — SOUND", MIXED: CODE-READ primary + LIVE supporting)
**Original severity:** SOUND (with one lower-confidence caveat)
**Claim:** The advisory-not-blocking design for all four mechanical QA checks is a deliberate, well-matched choice and all four gates match documentation exactly; one secondary caveat is that the single genuinely hard gate — structural marker damage blocked on save — is enforced client-side only, with no equivalent server-side check, flagged as incidental/out-of-scope rather than adversarially verified.
**Current location:** `server/routes/segment-editor.js:400-412; server/routes/localization-editor.js:367,566; server/public/js/segment-validation.js:27`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
The caveat's defect is fixed: the same validateStructure module is now invoked SERVER-side on the save path and blocks with a 400 — server/routes/segment-editor.js:400-412 `const structure = segmentValidation.validateStructure(\n  baseline.en,\n  baseline.is,\n  editedContent\n);\nif (structure.blocked) {\n  return res.status(400).json({\n    error: 'Vistun hafnað: byggingarmerki vantar eða hafa breyst.',\n    violations: structure.blocked,\n  });\n}` — reached only after an independent server-side baseline load (`Backstop baseline load failed` at :392), so the client cannot supply the comparison text. The same server-side call now guards both localization save paths (server/routes/localization-editor.js:367 and :566). The shared implementation remains server/public/js/segment-validation.js:27 `function validateStructure(enText, originalIs, editedIs)` with a CommonJS export at :161 so client and server run identical rules.
```
**Notes:** Attribution: commit 2767b9c4 'fix(editor): server-side structural-marker backstop on segment save (SR-OOS-2 D4/D5)'. The backstop is real enough that it broke two Playwright specs that had been POSTing synthetic segment IDs (register item C2, since fixed 2026-07-25) — independent corroboration that it actually rejects. NOT re-scorable by code reading: the dimension's LIVE supporting evidence was the spellcheck endpoint's graceful degradation with the Greynir sidecar absent (a clean 200 {enabled:false, segments:[]} in a shell with GREYNIR_URL unset). Whether that still holds against a real deployment is A4's walk, not a code read. The SOUND verdict is otherwise undisturbed — no code defect underpinning it is open.
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — The claim's only defect element is the caveat that the hard structural-marker gate was client-side only; it is genuinely closed by a server-side backstop, not by a cosmetic change. server/routes/segment-editor.js:400-412 calls segmentValidation.validateStructure(baseline.en, baseline.is, editedContent) and returns 400 with `Vistun hafnað: byggingarmerki vantar eða hafa breyst.` + violations when structure.blocked is truthy. Critically for the failure scenario (a bypassed client), the baseline is loaded SERVER-side at :383-396 via segmentParser.loadModuleForEditing with its own 404/500 handling, so the client cannot supply the comparison text; client-supplied originalContent is never used for the check. I checked every other content-write path rather than trusting the verifier: localization-editor.js:367 (single save, faithful baseline) and :540-582 (bulk save, batch-rejects the whole payload on any violation) both run the same function; propagationService.js:117 validates each propagated occurrence and skips blocked ones; the remaining POST/PUT routes in segment-editor.js are review-state transitions and apply, which move already-validated content. The shared rule set is one module, server/public/js/segment-validation.js:27, exporting via CommonJS at :161 — blocked rules (MATH/BR/xref/link/docref/MEDIA/SPACE/injected SEG markers) at :34-110, warnings-only at :112-150, and the route deliberately enforces only `blocked`, preserving the documented advisory posture (segment-editor.js:438-441 'Live QA (non-blocking)'). Identity edits skip validation (:400 `if (editedContent !== baseline.is)`), which cannot introduce new corruption relative to disk.
- ✅ upheld — The only defect in this claim is the caveat (client-side-only structural block); I enumerated every server route that writes segment content and each one runs the shared check. server/routes/segment-editor.js:333 POST `/:book/:chapter/:moduleId/edit` → :400-412 `segmentValidation.validateStructure(baseline.en, baseline.is, editedContent)` → `if (structure.blocked) return res.status(400)`, where `baseline` comes from an independent SERVER load (`segmentParser.loadModuleForEditing` at :385-391, with 'Backstop baseline load failed' at :392), so the client-supplied `originalContent` never feeds the comparison. server/routes/localization-editor.js:294 `/save` → check at :367-377 placed BEFORE the review-tier branch at :380, so both the direct-write path and `localizationReview.submitEdit` are gated. server/routes/localization-editor.js:470 `/save-all` → :558-577 loops over EVERY changed segment (`for (const e of auditEdits)`) and rejects the whole batch on any violation — not just the first segment. The bulk propagation path is covered too: server/services/propagationService.js:13 requires the same module and validates at :117. Remaining content-touching routes are non-writers of new text (`/accept` = acceptance marker, `/apply*` = writes already-validated stored edits, `restore/:version` = replays a stored snapshot). Implementation is genuinely shared, not a re-implementation: server/public/js/segment-validation.js:27 `function validateStructure(enText, originalIs, editedIs)` with `module.exports` at :161, required identically by client (segment-editor.js:1273, localization-editor.js:914) and server.
- ✅ upheld — The caveat's failure — a client that skips the hard structural-marker gate gets marker damage accepted because the server never re-checks — dies inside the save handler. server/routes/segment-editor.js:381-411 loads its OWN baseline (`segmentParser.loadModuleForEditing`, failing 404/500 via the `Backstop baseline load failed` branch at :391-396), so the comparison texts (`baseline.en`, `baseline.is`) are server-loaded and cannot be supplied by the caller; only `editedContent` comes from the body, and any `structure.blocked` returns 400 `Vistun hafnað: byggingarmerki vantar eða hafa breyst.` The same call guards both localization write paths (localization-editor.js:365-377 single-segment against `targetSeg.faithful`; :559-580 batch, rejecting the whole batch). The rule set is genuinely shared, not a re-implementation: server/public/js/segment-validation.js:27 `validateStructure(enText, originalIs, editedIs)` with a UMD tail exporting both `root.segmentValidation` and `module.exports` (:154-158), required server-side at segment-editor.js:38 and localization-editor.js:22, and its blocked rules cover exactly the named damage (math-missing, br-removed, xref-missing, link-removed, docref-missing, media-missing, space-removed, seg-marker-injected). I checked the sibling write paths for a way around it: `/accept` cannot introduce new bytes (acceptanceService.js:90-95 throws STALE_CONTENT unless `acceptedContent === seg.is`). ONE residual hole worth recording but not enough to flip this claim: `POST /:book/:chapter/:moduleId/propagate` (routes/segment-editor.js:1092-1138) takes `editedContent` from the body and calls `propagation.createPropagatedEdits` with NO `validateStructure` call, so pending edits carrying e.g. an injected `<!-- SEG:` can be created without the backstop — but that is an edit-fan-out route, not the save gate the claim describes, and the described save-path defect is fixed.

### `ed-dim-7` — CLOSED
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-editorial-workflow-review.md §5 dimension 7 ("Rollback & recovery safety — RISK", MIXED: LIVE + CODE-READ)
**Original severity:** RISK (rests on #6 medium + #3 medium + #19 low, all CONFIRMED)
**Claim:** The flagship Unit 1 restore mechanism is LIVE-verified sound; the RISK is in adjacent paths reachable in ordinary multi-editor operation — "Vista + Birta" applies an edit to the faithful file BEFORE checking whether a same-chapter render job is already running, leaving the edit applied-but-unpublished with a retry that dead-ends on "already applied" (#6, medium/KEEP); the Pass-2 approve step commits status='approved' before writing the localized file, so a write failure strands the edit approved-but-unapplied (#3, downgraded high→medium, still CONFIRMED); and the restore snapshot mechanism excludes empty segments, so a segment that is empty now but had content in a target restore version cannot be returned to empty through a restore-then-undo round trip (#19, low) — a direct break of the "restore is itself reversible" guarantee.
**Current location:** `server/routes/segment-editor.js:1304-1323; server/services/localizationReviewService.js:236-265; server/services/contentVersionService.js:112-131`
**Register item:** _(none cited)_
**Evidence:**

```
#6 fixed by inverting the order, with the reason recorded inline — segment-editor.js:1304-1312 `// Guard FIRST (item 12, F6): a running pipeline means we could not\n// render what we apply, so nothing is applied either — the 409\n// truthfully reports a no-op and the head-editor just retries later.\nconst existing = pipelineService.hasRunningJob(req.params.book, req.chapterNum, 'pipeline');\nif (existing) { return res.status(409).json({ error: 'Pipeline already running for this chapter', jobId: existing.id }); }` — note the check is also now book-scoped (`req.params.book` as the first argument), which closes finding #5's contribution to this scenario; a capacity guard was added ahead of the apply for the same reason (:1315-1322). #3 fixed by reordering write-then-commit — localizationReviewService.js:236-243 `// 1. Apply to the localized file FIRST (snapshot-before-save handled by .bak).\n// The file write is the one step a SQLite transaction cannot roll back, so it\n// runs before any status change: if it throws, the edit stays 'pending' —\n// visible in the queue, retryable, rejectable.` with the status/applied_at update moved into a single conn.transaction() at :265-271 (commit 109f3f0e, 'localization approve writes file before marking approved (F3)'). #19 fixed by recording empty segments — contentVersionService.js:114-119 `// Record every segment, empty string included: at restore time,\n// absence-from-snapshot is indistinguishable from "was never extracted",\n// which broke restore-undo for untranslated segments (item 12, F19).\n// Nullish content throws (NOT NULL / binding error) and aborts the\n// snapshot transaction — a bad caller must fail loud, not shrink history.` — the previous `if (seg.content)` guard is gone (commit cae59e8a, 'F19 snapshots fail loud on nullish content (drop guard)').
```
**Notes:** All three CONFIRMED code defects under this dimension are fixed on main. NOT re-scorable by code reading: the dimension's positive half was live (a full restore round trip verified byte-correct on disk in both directions, and the rebuild-from-history affordance confirmed for both halves) — re-confirming that is A4's walk. The review also records a refutation under this dimension (writing the faithful file inside the DB transaction was adversarially reviewed and refuted — atomic temp-plus-rename, .bak backup, rollback-and-retry); that refutation is unaffected by these three fixes and still holds in the current code (localizationReviewService's comment at :238-242 restates the same reasoning).
**Adversarial verdicts (0/3 refuted):**
- ✅ upheld — All three adjacent-path risks are closed by real order/semantics changes, not cosmetics. #6: segment-editor.js:1304-1313 places `pipelineService.hasRunningJob(req.params.book, req.chapterNum, 'pipeline')` and its 409 return BEFORE `applyApprovedEdits` at :1325, so the applied-but-unpublished dead-end (and the retry that 400s on 'already been applied' at :1362) cannot be entered; hasRunningJob is now book-scoped (pipelineService.js:469-481 matches `job.book === book`), which also removes the cross-book false positive that made the scenario more reachable. I checked for a residual TOCTOU: the handler is non-async and applyApprovedEdits/runPipeline are synchronous, so there is no yield point between guard and spawn, and the MAX_JOBS throw is pre-empted by the explicit `hasCapacity(2)` guard at :1318-1322 so it too lands before the apply. #3: localizationReviewService.approveAndApply now writes first — contentVersionService.saveLocalizedWithSnapshot at :253-259 — and only then commits `status='approved', applied_at=CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` inside conn.transaction() at :265-293, with the reason recorded inline at :237-242 ('if it throws, the edit stays pending — visible in the queue, retryable, rejectable'). A write failure therefore leaves the edit pending, not approved-but-unapplied. #19: contentVersionService.snapshotModule:112-131 has no content guard at all — the loop unconditionally `insert.run(..., seg.content, ...)` for every segment, with the comment at :115-119 stating empty string is recorded deliberately; restoreVersion:277 branches on `snapshotLookup.has(seg.segmentId)`, so a segment snapshotted as '' is restored to '', and the restore-then-undo round trip closes. One honest residual worth naming: rows written to content_versions BEFORE this change still omit empty segments, so restoring to a legacy version will hit the segmentsKept branch (:281-282) for those segments — a data artifact of old snapshots, not a surviving code defect.
- ✅ upheld — All three legs close, and the two adjacent layers #19 depends on were checked, not assumed. #6: the order is inverted with the reason inline — server/routes/segment-editor.js:1304-1313 `// Guard FIRST (item 12, F6) ... const existing = pipelineService.hasRunningJob(req.params.book, req.chapterNum, 'pipeline'); if (existing) return res.status(409)` — and `applyApprovedEdits` is only reached at :1325, after a further capacity guard at :1318-1322 that keeps a MAX_JOBS throw from re-creating the applied-but-unrendered dead-end via 500. The book argument is real, not cosmetic: `hasRunningJob(book, chapter, type)` is defined with a book field at server/services/pipelineService.js:469-481 (`job.book === book && job.chapter === chapter && ...`) and every caller passes a book (routes/pipeline.js:75,125,175; publicationService.js:216,353; admin.js:262,335), so the cross-book false 'already running' that fed this scenario is gone too. #3: write-then-commit — server/services/localizationReviewService.js:236-259 does `contentVersionService.saveLocalizedWithSnapshot(...)` FIRST under the comment 'The file write is the one step a SQLite transaction cannot roll back ... if it throws, the edit stays pending', with the `status='approved', applied_at=CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` UPDATE moved inside `conn.transaction()` at :265-290; the failure therefore leaves a retryable pending row, not a stranded approved one. #19: `snapshotModule` records every segment including `''` — the old `if (seg.content)` filter is gone, the loop at contentVersionService.js:112-131 calls `insert.run(...)` unconditionally. I also checked both hops on either side: (a) `restoreVersion` feeds it `cfg.currentContent(seg)`, and BOTH track configs coalesce (`(seg) => seg.is || ''` at :29 and `(seg) => (seg.hasLocalized ? seg.localized : seg.faithful) || ''` at :37), so the new fail-loud-on-nullish contract cannot abort a restore snapshot; (b) restore reconstructs with `snapshotLookup.has(seg.segmentId)` (:279), a presence test not a truthiness test, and the writer preserves emptiness — `assembleSegments` (server/services/segmentParser.js:60-67) emits `<!-- SEG:... -->\n${seg.content}` for every segment with no skip, so an empty segment lands on disk as an empty marker body and parses back as ''. The restore-then-undo round trip returns the segment to empty.
- ✅ upheld — Each of the three adjacent-path risks dies at a quoted step. #6: the order is inverted and the guard is now book-aware — routes/segment-editor.js:1303-1312 runs `pipelineService.hasRunningJob(req.params.book, req.chapterNum, 'pipeline')` and returns 409 BEFORE `applyApprovedEdits` (:1323), with a capacity pre-check at :1316-1321 so runPipeline's MAX_JOBS throw also cannot land after the write; `hasRunningJob` genuinely compares book (pipelineService.js:469-481 `job.book === book && job.chapter === chapter && …`) and spawnJob stamps `book` onto the job object (:386), so finding #5's cross-book false 'already running' can no longer strand a chemistry apply on a biology job. The check→apply→spawn sequence is entirely synchronous in a non-async handler (better-sqlite3 + sync fs), so no other request can interleave in the TOCTOU window. #3: localizationReviewService.approveAndApply now writes first — the inline comment at :236-243 ('The file write is the one step a SQLite transaction cannot roll back, so it runs before any status change: if it throws, the edit stays pending') sits above the `saveLocalizedWithSnapshot` call (:255-261), and status='approved' + applied_at are set together inside one `conn.transaction()` (:263-291); a failed write therefore leaves the row 'pending' and still visible/actionable in the queue, which was the whole defect. #19: the `if (seg.content)` guard is gone — contentVersionService.snapshotModule:112-131 inserts EVERY segment including the empty string (comment: 'Record every segment, empty string included: at restore time, absence-from-snapshot is indistinguishable from was never extracted'), and I traced the round trip the review said breaks: restore rebuilds via `snapshotLookup.has(seg.segmentId)` on a Map (:277), so a recorded '' is honoured rather than skipped, and the pre-restore snapshot cannot itself abort on nullish content because BOTH track configs coalesce — contentVersionService.js:28 `currentContent: (seg) => seg.is || ''` and :37 `(seg) => (seg.hasLocalized ? seg.localized : seg.faithful) || ''`. The related '#19 edge case' (an all-empty module producing a zero-row version that later throws 'version not found') is closed by the same change.

### `ed-dim-8` — OPEN-UNTRACKED
**Source:** docs/audit/2026-07-11-editorial-workflow-review.md §5 dimension 8 ("Onboarding load — RISK", MIXED: LIVE + document-comparison), with §2 drift catalog and §6 recommendation 1
**Original severity:** RISK
**Claim:** What the live system teaches a new editor is genuinely good and LIVE-confirmed; the RISK is what a new head-editor or admin would be told by the documentation corpus, which the review's own drift catalog found badly and in places actively wrong — most consequentially review-protocol.md, a skill that AUTO-TRIGGERS on "discussing reviews or approvals" yet documents an entirely different, retired review model with no connection to the live system; compounded for a non-technical audience by a raw SQLite constraint message reaching a head-editor via alert() (§3 bug (a)) and by a second dead end where the pipeline's 409 confirmation response has its explanatory text discarded client-side, leaving only "Villa: HTTP 409" (#14, medium/KEEP).
**Current location:** `.claude/skills/review-protocol.md:1-30; docs/workflow/master-pipeline.md:3; docs/editorial/terminology.md:220; package.json scripts block (absence) vs CLAUDE.md Commands table`

**Evidence:**

And `npm run update-status`, still documented in CLAUDE.md's Commands table and its Status Updates section, still does not exist — the package.json scripts block has no update-status entry (grep returns only `"validate": "node scripts/validate-status.js"`), the implementation remaining at scripts/archived/update-status.js. CLOSED HALVES, for completeness: bug (a) is fixed (see row ed-dim-1). #14 is fixed — the client now implements its half of the handshake at server/public/js/segment-editor.js:1960-1975 `if (err.data && err.data.requiresConfirmation) { if (window.confirm(err.data.warning || err.message)) { ... body: JSON.stringify({ ... confirmed: true }), }` with an explicit neutral decline path (`// User declined the confirmation dialog — this is not a failure`), against the unchanged server contract at server/routes/pipeline.js:88,138,188 (commit f7fe8ba0, 'surface dropped server messages — conflict text + pipeline confirmation handshake').

The live campaign's P3 tail explicitly collapses ~120 low findings by pointer rather than enumeration, but a bucket that names no item does not track this one, and in any case the review ranked this its #1 recommendation ('the cheapest available fix with the widest blast radius'), not a low-tail item.
